# Dev Journal

Accumulated learnings, discoveries, and decisions from Ember development. Things that aren't obvious from the code or docs — API quirks, licensing traps, UX lessons, technical gotchas.

**Agents: read this before starting work. Add entries when you learn something non-obvious.**

---

- **Both editions of the *Confessions* shipped one file per Book, not one per chapter — and one of them had Book XIII hidden inside Book XII (2026-09-02).** `augustine-confessions` (Outler) and `church-fathers/augustine/confessions` (Schaff/NPNF) were each 13 `.md` files, one per *Book* of the work, with the chapters as plain-text `CHAPTER I` lines (Outler) or `##` headings (Schaff) inside them. Book Ten alone was a 43-chapter, ~90KB blob — the whole point of per-`(chapter, language)` hashing is lost when the chapter *is* the book. Split into 275 chapter files for Outler (en-US + pt-BR) and 276 for Schaff, with each former Book file demoted to a TOC **group body** carrying only its `# Book N` H1 and Outler's summary paragraph.
  - **The Outler `book-twelve.md` contained Books XII *and* XIII.** `BOOK THIRTEEN` sat mid-file at line 197, in both languages, which is why `book.json` listed only twelve Books and Book Thirteen was unreachable from the TOC. Counting `^CHAPTER ` per file is what surfaced it: 69 chapters in a Book that has 32. **Chapter counts against the known structure of a work are a cheap import-integrity check** — worth running on any book imported from a paginated web source, where the page break and the structural break need not coincide.
  - **Outler's Book XIII genuinely skips Chapter XXXVI** (…XXXV, XXXVII, XXXVIII…). Chapter ids are derived from the printed Roman numeral, not from sequence position, so the file is `book-thirteen-chapter-37.md` and the gap is preserved rather than silently closed. Same principle as the Ambrose ¶48 entry below: a number the author chose is a citation handle, not an index.
  - **Footnotes had to be renumbered, and the ref regex needs `(?!:)` care.** Outler's footnote *definitions* all lived at the end of each Book file, numbered per Book (up to `[^202]`); splitting means each chapter carries only its own, renumbered from 1. Verifying the result, five chapters looked like they had a definition with no reference — all false positives: the reference in prose is followed by a colon (`would say[^1]: …`), so a naive `\[\^(\d+)\](?!:)` "not a definition" guard skips it. Match definitions by line anchor (`^\[\^\d+\]: `), never by what follows the bracket. pt-BR carries no footnotes at all — the translation dropped them — so only en-US needed the pass.
  - **Splitting does not change how the `N. ` paragraph numbers render**, despite the entry below. Every chapter boundary was already a non-list line inside the old file, so the `<ol>` was already broken there and already restarted with `start="N"`; the cut just moves the break into a file boundary. These two books remain on the fragile plain-`N. ` numbering and are still owed the `**N.**` conversion.
  - **Verification that actually catches a lossy split:** normalise every source file to a sorted multiset of stripped non-blank lines, do the same over the outputs, and diff the multisets. It found exactly one intentional drop (the redundant `As Confissões de Santo Agostinho` line above the Book One title) and nothing else. Line counts and chapter counts agree by construction and prove nothing.

---

- **A paragraph numbered `47.` `49.` `49.` in the source rendered as 47, 48, 49 — Markdown owns the numbers, not the author (2026-09-02).** Ambrose's *On the Mysteries* has a genuine quirk of its edition: it skips ¶48 and prints ¶49 twice. The en-US chapter recorded that faithfully, one paragraph per line, `47. `/`49. `/`49. `. But a line beginning `N. ` is an **ordered list item**, and `marked` renders the run as `<ol start="47">` — the literal numbers are discarded and the browser counts 47, 48, 49. The edition's quirk was erased and one paragraph was labelled with a number that appears nowhere in the text. Nothing catches this: the markdown is valid, `build:corpus` is clean, and the source file reads correctly to anyone opening it.
  - **The tell was a cross-language mismatch, not the rendering.** The pt-BR translation (#346) wrote its numbers as `**47.**` — bold, not a list — so the two editions of one book disagreed about paragraph markers. Sweeping all 14 bilingual books for numbering style, 12 matched across languages and Ambrose was the odd one out; that inconsistency is what led back to the `<ol>`. Both files are `**N.**` now, and the rendered numbers match the source.
  - **The rule: a number the author chose must never be written as a Markdown list marker.** `1.` delegates numbering to the renderer, which is correct only when the sequence is the trivial one. Any sequence with a gap, a repeat, or a start the author cares about has to be inert text — `**N.**` — or the renderer will silently rewrite it. This affects source-faithful patristic and catechetical texts specifically, where paragraph numbers are citation handles.
  - **Roughly a dozen other books use plain `N. ` paragraph numbering** (`augustine-confessions`, `aquinas-opera-omnia/catechetical-instructions`, `morrow-my-catholic-faith`, `sales-introduction-to-the-devout-life`, the montfort set). They render correctly today only because their numbering happens to be gapless and to start where the list starts — the display is coincidence, not fidelity. Worth a sweep for gaps/repeats before trusting any of them as citation targets. `pius-x-catechism` and `pius-x-greater-catechism` already use `**N.**` in both languages and are immune.
  - **Cheap detector:** parse a chapter with `marked` and assert the rendered numbers equal the numbers in the source, or simply `rg -c '^\d+\. ' content/books/**` and treat any file whose numbers aren't `1..n` contiguous as suspect.

- **The Opus Dei morning offering was praying the Apostleship of Prayer's formula (2026-08-27).** `morning-offering-opus-dei` built the heroic minute correctly — rise, kiss the floor, *Serviam!*, sign yourself — and then handed the day over with `offering-to-sacred-heart`: *O Jesus, through the Immaculate Heart of Mary… I offer them for the intentions of our bishops and of all Apostles of Prayer.* That is a real and good devotion, but it belongs to the Apostleship of Prayer, and a member of the Work praying it is announcing membership in a different league. The Work's own text is the customary *ofrecimiento de obras* of the *Pocket Prayer Book* — the household prayer St. Josemaría learned from his mother and kept morning and night. New `practice/offering-of-works` carries it; the flow heading is now "Offering of Works" / "Oferecimento de Obras", which is what the norm is actually called. `offering-to-sacred-heart` is untouched and still serves `morning-offering-sacred-heart` and `collection/sacred-heart`, where it belongs.
  - **The word that makes it the right text is *acciones*.** The Apostleship formula offers prayers, works, joys and sufferings *for* a list of intentions; the offering of works offers **the actions of the day** — whatever they turn out to be, before the day has said what it will contain. That is the whole of "sanctify your work" compressed into one clause, and it is why the prayer is said standing, at the moment of rising, and not later with the other morning prayers.
  - **No `la` key, deliberately.** A Latin rendering of this prayer is easy to *compose* and hard to *source* — it is a vernacular household prayer, not a liturgical one. Left absent, so a reader with Latin as secondary language gets an honest struck-through `LA` rather than a fabricated text (see the 2026-07-24 entry: the struck `LA` is a bug detector, and "no Latin exists for this" is one of its two legitimate answers).
  - **The general trap: a tradition-specific practice assembled out of a generic prayer ref will look right and be wrong.** The frame here was accurate down to the *Camino* §206 citation and the closing *Deo omnis gloria!*; only the principal text — the one thing the practice exists to pray — was borrowed. The manifest even *documented* the substitution ("Offering text from the Apostleship of Prayer") and it still survived the full audit of the Opus Dei norms (2026-07-24), because that audit checked that every norm **existed**, not that each norm's text was the one that tradition actually uses. Swept the other nine `morning-offering-*` variants for the same shape: all of them inline their own tradition's text and ref nothing but `sign-of-cross` (Montfort also `hail-mary`), so this was the only instance.

- **A completed practice showed muted text but an empty circle — `useAnimatedStyle` snapshots its `initial` style once, at first render (2026-08-25).** On Today, a row would read as done (muted title, block counter advanced) while its checkbox sat unfilled — reliably the first row you completed in a sitting, and only after leaving the screen and coming back. Everything on the row except the circle is a plain prop (`done ? '$colorSecondary' : '$color'`), which is why only the fill was ever wrong.
  - **The mechanism, from `hook/useAnimatedStyle.js`.** `initial` is computed behind `if (!animatedUpdaterData.current)` — once, at mount, never recomputed when a dependency changes. `initial.value` is the style `createAnimatedComponent` hands React, so it is what the native view is rebuilt from when a native screen is reattached under NativeTabs. A row unchecked at mount therefore comes back with a mount-time empty circle no matter what the worklet would compute now. Rows already complete at mount were immune, which is what made it look like "the first item".
  - **The rule: nothing reanimated computes may carry state that has to survive a reattach.** Not a hand-driven shared value (it resets to its creation value — the lesson already written on `FadeInView`), and not a prop-derived animated style either (its `initial` is frozen). The resting appearance has to be a plain style React re-commits on every render; reanimated gets decoration only. A shared value is still fine for a one-shot whose resting value equals its initial value — the checkbox's `pulse` rests at 1 and is created at 1 — and declarative `entering` animations are fine because presence stays plain React.
  - **Two wrong fixes first, both plausible, both aimed at the wrong layer.** Moving the fill from a shared value into the animated style (right instinct, wrong layer — `initial` is still frozen, so the symptom did not change at all). Then, chasing the sluggishness that fix introduced, splitting the styles up. Worth noting *why* that sluggishness happened, because it is a real trap: an animated style that both reads a shared value and returns an animation re-runs its worklet every frame, and `styleUpdater` then re-pushes the whole non-animated half through `updateProps` **with no `shallowEqual` guard** (that guard lives only on the animation-free branch). Box metrics — `width`, `height`, `borderWidth` — were being committed to the shadow tree ~60x a second, forcing a layout pass each time. **No layout prop should ever travel through a worklet.**
  - **The symptom that should have pointed at the right layer immediately:** the state was correct everywhere React owned it and stale only where reanimated owned it. That split is the fingerprint of a frozen snapshot, not of a miscomputed animation — chase where the value is *committed*, not how it is calculated.
  - **An `entering` animation is not a safe substitute either.** The first draft of the fix popped the glyph in with `ZoomIn`. But an entering view starts *hidden* and is revealed by the animation, so any run that never starts — reduced motion, a `LayoutAnimationConfig skipEntering` above it, mounting mid-transition — leaves a checked row with a gold circle and no tick. Same class of failure, smaller blast radius. The rule that actually holds: **nothing that decides whether state is visible may depend on an animation running.** The glyph is a plain conditional child now; the pulse is the feedback.
  - **`accessibilityState` never reaches the DOM.** Dumping the rendered HTML to check the fix showed `role="checkbox"` and `aria-label` but no `aria-checked` — react-native-web drops `accessibilityState`, so the web build shipped a checkbox whose state screen readers could not read. `aria-checked` is honoured on both platforms (RN maps `aria-*` since 0.71), so both are set now. Worth checking on any other `accessibilityState` in the app.
  - **The component had no tests at all, and now has five.** They cannot reproduce the reattach failure, but they pin the invariant: the fill must come out of an ordinary render. Negative control run — moving the two colour lines back into an animated style fails three of them. Two jsdom traps found while writing them: `lucide-react-native` draws through `react-native-svg` and renders **nothing** under jsdom (so never assert on the glyph), and jsdom normalises `transparent` to `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)`.
  - **The same frozen-snapshot pattern is latent in at least five other components** — `CadenceToggle` (thumb position), `RuleTree` (chevron rotation), `DateScrubber` / `DayCarousel` / `TrackPicker` (scroll offset seeded from an index). Each seeds a shared value from a prop or state and drives an animated style from it, so each can come back from a reattach showing a mount-time position while the React state beside it is correct. Not all are reachable — anything inside an RN `<Modal>` remounts and is safe — but the audit is worth doing before one of them is reported as its own mystery bug.
  - **This is invisible to the test suite.** The failure needs a real native screen detach/reattach; jsdom renders the component once and it looks right either way. Worth remembering before trusting a green run on any reanimated state bug.

- **Six Divine Intimacy chapters shipped with a tool's truncation marker rendered as scripture — `… acusando com since... (line truncated to 2000 chars)` (2026-08-09).** Seven paragraphs across `content/books/gabriel-stmm-intimita-divina/{pt-BR,en-US}` ended mid-word with the literal text a file-read tool emits when it clips a long line. The translations were produced against the Italian originals in `it/`; whatever read them clipped each paragraph at 2,000 characters, and the marker was translated straight through into the corpus along with everything else. It built clean, published clean, and was visible to a reader on day one — nothing in the pipeline looks at prose for signs that it is prose.
  - **The damage is not only the visible marker: one paragraph invented text to fill the gap.** `pt-BR/giorno-125.md` runs faithful to the Italian up to *"luta e esforço verdadeiramente"* and then continues *"heróicos, mas é precisamente nessa luta que se manifesta o"* — a fluent Portuguese clause that corresponds to nothing in the source, ahead of the marker. **A truncation marker is a signal that the text before it may also be wrong**, so restore from the original rather than just deleting the marker and closing the sentence.
  - **The same read also silently merged two paragraphs.** `pt-BR/giorno-271.md` had the Italian's `L7` and `L9` as one block, since the clip landed inside what the translator saw as a single run. Paragraph structure is worth diffing against the source language, not just word count.
  - **Two cheap detectors, both worth running after any bulk content generation.** `rg 'truncated to [0-9]+ chars' content/` finds the marker itself. For silent losses, compare character counts per file against the original-language edition — `wc -m` ratios below ~0.88 flagged the three worst pt-BR files, and prose translations otherwise sit within a few percent of their source.

- **Six more surfaces were excluded from Justif for reasons that turned out not to exist (2026-08-09).** Having fixed `prose`, I audited the rest and reported that the Bible, missal rich text, ℣/℟ verses and producer paragraphs *couldn't* be justified — superscript verse numbers are a different size, missal segments carry their own colours, a cross-reference is a tap target. Every one of those was a limit of our wrapper, not of Justif. The library's own `RunMetrics` is documented as **"one styling context inside a paragraph (the paragraph itself, an `<em>`, *a size change*…)"**, and `Measure.width(text, run)` receives the run — so per-run size was always free. `justifyText.ts` was closing over a single `fontSizePx` and describing a run by nothing but a `TextStyleName`.
  - **The check that would have caught it in one minute: read the dependency's type definitions before declaring what it can't do.** `node_modules/justif/dist/*.d.ts` names `RunMetrics.fontKey` as "family+weight+style+**size**+spacing", carries `noHyphens` per run, and has `RunText.atomicKey` for "no break opportunity between boxes that share a key" — three of the exact features I was about to call impossible.
  - **`StyledSegment` now separates what changes an advance from what doesn't.** `style` / `fontSizePx` / `letterSpacing` are measured; `render` (colour, opacity, leading) and `onPress` are drawn and never measured; `atomic` makes a run rigid. The split is the whole safety property: **a metric-bearing property smuggled through `render` places lines against widths the screen then contradicts**, which is the same class of bug as the synthetic-oblique one from 2026-08-06.
  - **A run's `render` must hold RESOLVED colours, never Tamagui tokens.** It is applied as a raw RN `style`, and `$colorSecondary` does not resolve there — it renders as no colour at all. Read it off `useTheme()` under a memo, because runs are grouped by the *identity* of `render`/`onPress` and a fresh object per verse mints a run per verse.
  - **Two cross-references must never share a run.** Pieces merge across an appearance-preserving boundary, so keying runs by style alone would fuse two adjacent links into one tap target — you would tap "1213" and get "1214". Regression test does exactly that.
  - **`letterSpacing` was measured before it was rendered, and a test caught it.** Adding it to the run's width without adding it to the piece's draw style makes the screen narrower than the breaker planned, so the last word re-wraps. The lone-space flex has to *add* to the run's tracking rather than replace it.
  - **jsdom can drive the justified path after all.** The suite's `ResizeObserver` stub is a no-op, so every component test was silently exercising only the fallback. Stubbing `Element.prototype.getBoundingClientRect` to return a real width is enough: the DOM then shows per-gap `letter-spacing`, negative values where the line shrinks, and a hyphen drawn as its own piece. **A component test for justified text that doesn't stub the measure is testing nothing.**

- **`prose` primitives were justified by the platform, not by Justif — the one surface where that is worst (2026-08-09).** `ProseBlock` spread `useReadingStyle()` straight onto a Tamagui `Text`, so a book chapter got `textAlign: 'justify'` with no hyphenation: greedy line filling over 2,000-character paragraphs, which is how a Divine Intimacy meditation inside `practice/intimita-divina` came to read as rivers of whitespace. `PrayerLines` had been on `JustifiedText` since #329 and the book reader on Justif's DOM renderer, so prose was the third path that quietly kept the platform default — and `docs/design/typography-justification.md` had already named the failure mode ("justified *without* hyphenation — the worst combination") without noticing prose was in that bucket. Paragraphs, list items and blockquote paragraphs now go through the same Knuth–Plass pass.
  - **When you add a justified renderer, enumerate the primitives, not the screens.** The surface that needed it most was reachable from a practice, a chapter route and the catechism, and belonged to none of them.

- **Holy Mass had no Amen button because its manifest said `"completion": "manual"`, and `manual` means the opposite of what it sounds like (2026-08-06).** The field is read in exactly one place — `PracticeFlowView.tsx:183`, `manifest.completion !== 'manual'` — and its only effect is to *suppress* the Amen button at the end of the flow. So `manual` does not mean "the user marks it manually", it means "no in-flow completion affordance at all"; the only remaining path is the Today checklist's `useToggleSlot`. Set `mass` to `flow-end` (284 of the 288 practice manifests already say that) so the Mass closes the way every other practice does.
  - **Mass was probably given `manual` because a check-in already completes it.** `features/mass-times/checkins.ts:60` logs a `mass` completion when you check in at a church with `kind: 'mass'` — a real second path, but one that only exists if you used the Mass-times finder. Both paths can now fire on the same day; that is harmless because every consumer dedupes (`toCompletedSet` keys on practice + sub_id, streak counts key on date), though the check-in writes `sub_id: 'default'` while the Amen button writes the plan slot's id.
  - **Residual: `confession`, `grace-meals` and `spiritual-reading` are still `manual`** and have the same dead end — nothing inside the flow closes them. Left alone as out of scope here, but the mode is now used by three practices and buys nothing the Today checkbox doesn't already give; it is a candidate for deletion along with the `'flow-end' | 'manual'` union in `manifestTypes.ts:122`.

- **A `collapsible` around a book chapter deleted the chapter, silently — `collectBookChapterRefs` recursed by allow-list and `collapsible` wasn't on it (2026-08-06).** Every one of the three Liguori blocks in `practice/visit-blessed-sacrament` rendered as *nothing*, and every layer failed quietly. `collectBookChapterRefs` (`packages/content-engine/src/engine/flow.ts`) switched on `prose | cycle | repeat | options | prayer | select`; with `collapsible`, `group` and `liturgical-color-scope` absent, a nested `prose` collected no refs → `resolveFlowAsync` took its zero-refs early return and passed the *un*hydrated context through → that context has only `loadBookChapterTextAsync`, so the sync `loadBookChapterText` at `resolve.ts:320` returned `[]` → `resolve.ts:456` dropped the now-empty container. Corpus built clean, `validate-flows` passed, the published blob had every field right, and the practice rendered three chapters as zero sections. The app's own twin walk (`PracticeFlow/findTrackIds.ts:28`) handled `collapsible` correctly, which is why nothing else caught it.
  - **Fixed with a `default:` branch that recurses into any `sections` array, not a fourth `case`.** Plain containers wrap a body without changing which chapters it needs, so an allow-list here is a trap that re-arms every time someone adds a container type. Regression test in `engine/__tests__/flow.test.ts` covers `collapsible` and `group`.
  - **Two lessons about what "verified" means.** Building the corpus and reading the published blob proves the *data* is right; it says nothing about whether the app renders it. And **a resolver that returns `[]` on failure cannot be distinguished from content that is legitimately empty** — the whole chain here degraded silently by design. Run the flow through `resolveFlowAsync` with a stub `loadBookChapterTextAsync` that records what it was asked for; if the recorded list is empty, the content is gone.
  - **`validate-flows.ts` now checks `prose.book` / `prose.chapter` against the book's chapter files**, because the corpus had 33 such references and nothing verified any of them — `refIntegrity.test.ts` covers `prayer ref` only. That gap is what let a chapter be renamed inside a book in the same commit that added references to it. Templated chapters (`{{chapterId}}`) are skipped; books with no `content/books/<id>/book.json` (external, producer-resolved) warn rather than error.
  - **Also parallelised the chapter preload** (`flow.ts`): it was a `for…of` with `await` inside, so this practice's three chapters × three candidate languages meant nine serial round trips before first paint. Chapters now fetch concurrently; languages stay sequential within a chapter because they merge into one `LocalizedContent` and candidate order decides which text wins a key.

- **Rewrote `practice/visit-blessed-sacrament` as the Work's actual form; the `select` was carrying three traditions and a duplicate of `practice/holy-hour` (2026-08-06).** The practice offered *Short Visit* / *Holy Hour* / *Opus Dei* as `select` options. That is precisely the pattern `docs/features/unified-flow-system.md` retired: `select` is conditional branching on context (day, hour, user preference), and **different traditions of the same devotion belong in separate practices grouped by `alternativeTo`** — the shape `spiritual-communion-opus-dei`, `three-hail-marys-opus-dei` and the nine `morning-offering-*` variants already use. The Holy Hour branch was also a near-copy of the standalone `practice/holy-hour` (Gethsemane opening, Anima Christi, Scripture, silence, act of reparation), so the visit was shipping a second, drifting edition of another practice inside a tab. Collapsed to one flow, no `select`.
  - **The `opus-dei` option that flow carried had the acclamation in the wrong place *and* the wrong text.** It said the acclamation **once**, then `repeat count: 3` over Our Father / Hail Mary / Glory Be. The custom is the reverse nesting: the acclamation opens **each of the three sets**, as an act of faith and reparation, so the `response` block belongs *inside* the `repeat`. Two levels of a `repeat` are easy to get backwards when the acclamation reads like an opening line.
  - **The acclamation is a two-part versicle and response, not one line echoed — and it is not the *Adoremus*.** opusdei.org's English article describes the leader pronouncing *Adoremus in aeternum sanctissimum Sacramentum* with everyone "repeating the same acclamation", which reads as `v` and `r` carrying identical text. That is not what is actually prayed: the acclamation is the traditional *Sia lodato e ringraziato ogni momento il Santissimo e Divinissimo Sacramento*, split between two voices — pt-BR **V.** *Graças e louvores sejam dados a todo momento* / **R.** *ao Santíssimo e Diviníssimo Sacramento*, en-US the couplet **V.** *O Sacrament most holy, O Sacrament divine* / **R.** *all praise and all thanksgiving be every moment thine*. The English-language description of a devotion is not the devotion; when a vernacular form exists, get the vernacular text.
  - **The Our Father / Hail Mary / Glory Be are `ref`s, collapsed.** They were briefly inlined — the argument being that a `prayer ref:` renders as a collapsed chevron row (2026-07-24 entry on `morning-offering-opus-dei`) and a visit prayed off a card should show its text. That argument does not survive contact with this practice: the three prayers repeat *three times each*, so inlining put nine full prayer texts on one screen and buried the acclamation that gives the visit its shape, while adding 1.2KB of duplicated text to a hashed blob fetched on every open. These are the three prayers every Catholic knows by heart — the collapsed title row is the right affordance, and the 2026-07-24 rule is about a flow's *principal* text, which here is the acclamation, not the Pater. Reverted to `ref`; the practice also rejoins `refIntegrity.test.ts`, the only automated check for prayer-ref rot.
  - **The flow carries no rubrics at all.** Where and when to make the visit, the genuflection, the sign of the cross, and how the dialogue is divided are all *about* the practice rather than part of it, so they live in the manifest's `description` / `howToPray`, which is what the practice screen shows before you start praying. A rubric that the user reads once and never needs again is manifest text wearing a flow costume.
  - **The old flow had *O Sacrament most holy, O Sacrament divine* sitting loose at the end as a stray inline prayer, and a closing Sign of the Cross block.** The couplet is not stray at all — it is the English form of the acclamation, so it moved into the `response` where it belongs. The Sign of the Cross came out. Source: the Opus Dei *Pocket Prayer Book* — note opusdei.org 403s both WebFetch and curl through the agent proxy, so it cannot be re-verified from a sandboxed session.
  - **Where each vocal prayer splits in the dialogue is deliberately not encoded.** The Hail Mary (at *Jesus*) and the Glory Be (at *Holy Spirit*) are unambiguous, but the Our Father's break point varies by language and house custom, so `howToPray` says one side begins and the other completes and the flow stays neutral.
  - **Nothing needed rewiring.** `plan-of-life-templates/{opus-dei,cursillo}.json` and `collections/{eucharistic,dies-thursday,opus-dei}.json` all reference the bare `visit-blessed-sacrament` id — none of them ever selected an option, so the templates kept working unchanged. `collection/eucharistic` describes its block as "the simple visit to the tabernacle and the formal Hour of reparation", which only became true once the Hour came out of the visit. `estimatedMinutes` 15 → 3, and the daily slot picked up `"time": "13:00"` to match "preferably at midday, after lunch."
  - **Then folded St. Alphonsus's *Visits* into the same practice, as a `collapsible` under the norm.** `book/liguori-visite-al-ss-sacramento` (*Visite al SS. Sacramento ed a Maria Santissima*, 1745) has been in the corpus in it / en-US / pt-BR all along, listed in `collection/eucharistic` and `collection/alphonsus-liguori`, but nothing ever prayed it — it was a book you could read, not a practice you could make. Its thirty-one visits are indexed to the day of the month by the author's own design, which is exactly `cycle` + `indexBy: "day-of-month"`, the machinery `meditacoes-ligorio` already uses. `data/visits.json` holds 31 `{chapterId}` entries; the `cycle` substitutes `{{chapterId}}` into a `prose` block. Each chapter is self-contained (the visit, then the companion visit to Our Lady, then the fixed daily prayer to the BVM), so the flow only wraps it with `atti-principio-visita` before and `atto-comunione-spirituale` after.
  - **Then restructured it again: the Liguori material interleaves with the norm, it does not sit under it.** The first pass put the whole devotion in one collapsible below the norm, which read as an appendix. Alphonsus's book is not an appendix — it has a beginning, a middle and an end, and the norm belongs inside them. The flow is now five slots: his *acts at the beginning of every visit* → the three Opus Dei sets → the day's visit → the spiritual communion → his *prayers at the end of every visit*. The three lifted-from-the-book blocks are separate `collapsible`s, all `defaultOpen: false`, so what is visible by default is still exactly the three-minute norm.
  - **A fixed daily prayer was being dropped 30 days out of 31.** Alphonsus closes Visit I with a Prayer to the Blessed Virgin Mary carrying his own rubric — *"which should be repeated every day at the end of the Visit"* — but it lived inside the `visita-01` chapter file, so the day-of-month cycle showed it on the 1st and never again. Split into its own chapter. The cheap detector: `grep -h '^## ' <lang>/visita-*.md | sort | uniq -c` returned `31 ## To Mary` / `1 ## Prayer to the Blessed Virgin Mary`, and that lone `1` was the bug. **Before cycling a book by date, check the chapter set for non-uniform headings.**
  - **Named the new chapter `preghiere-fine-visita` — a container, not a single prayer.** *Prayers to Be Made at the End of Every Visit*, mirroring the existing `atti-principio-visita`, so the two now frame the 31 visits symmetrically in the TOC. Named for the slot rather than the prayer because the printed edition the maintainer owns carries a further fixed daily Marian prayer that this IntraText source (`sources[0].url`) lacks; when that text turns up it drops into this chapter instead of needing a new one.
  - **The *Prayer of St. Ephrem* in Visit I stays in Visit I — do not "fix" it.** It reads like a standing purity prayer (*"O Immaculate and entirely pure Virgin Mary…"*), but 23 of the 31 visits carry their own named prayer of a Father in `## To Mary` (Anselm, Athanasius, Augustine, Bernard ×3, Germanus, Ildephonsus, John Damascene, Peter Damian, Andrew of Crete, William of Paris…), and Visit II's is headed *"Prayer of the **same** St. Ephrem"* over a different text — "the same" names the Father, not a repeated prayer. Splitting it would strip Visit I of its proper Marian prayer while every other visit kept theirs.
  - **Every visit marks the spiritual communion but never supplies it.** All 31 carry a bare rubric line between the Blessed Sacrament half and `## To Mary` — `Spiritual Communion.` / `La comunione spirituale.` / `A comunhão espiritual.` The act itself is in `atto-comunione-spirituale`, which holds two: the full one and, after *"And more briefly:"*, a short one. Those two plus the Opus Dei form are now a `select` picker at that point in the flow. **This is not the `select` the earlier commit removed** — that one branched the whole practice into three traditions, a slot-level choice that `unified-flow-system.md` assigns to `alternativeTo`; this one picks one short prayer among three at a single point, which is what `select` is for. A labelled `select` with no `on` is a visible picker: `computeSelectedId` (`engine/sections/select.ts:72-78`) takes `default` as the initial option, and every branch is pre-materialised (`engine/flow.ts:245-250`) so switching is instant. **The choice does not survive closing the practice** — `useSelectOverrides.ts:15-22` keeps `selectOverrides` in component `useState` and resets it on every flow load, so a user who prefers Alphonsus's act re-picks it daily. `as: "spiritualCommunion"` only names the override key within one open flow. Real persistence would need an `alternativeTo` practice bound to a plan slot, which is not what this is.
  - **Known residual:** `visita-NN` is monolithic, so its internal order is meditation → `Spiritual Communion.` rubric → `## To Mary`, and the picker can only land after the whole chapter. Closing that would mean splitting all 31 visits at the rubric line in three languages — 93 files, and 62 book chapters where a reader currently sees 31. Left alone deliberately.
  - **`defaultOpen: false` is load-bearing here, not cosmetic.** The whole point of the preceding rewrite was that the norm is three minutes and must not become a second period of formal prayer; appending a ten-minute devotion inline would have undone it. Collapsed, the norm still ends at the spiritual communion and the day's visit is one tap below it. `estimatedMinutes` stays at 3 for the same reason — it measures the obligation, not the ceiling.
  - **Confirmed the `data/` vs `tracks/` split publishes correctly this time.** The 2026-07-24 entry records `bible-catechism-in-year` silently shipping `trackHashes: null` because its `lectio` tracks lived under `data/`. The inverse applies to `cycle`: it reads `dataHashes`, so `visits.json` belongs under `data/`. Verified in the built corpus rather than assumed — `dataHashes: [{name: "visits", …}]`, `trackHashes: null`, and the blob carries `indexBy: day-of-month` with 31 entries. Day-of-month resolution spot-checked against the engine at the 1st, 6th, 31st and a 28-day February.
  - **Left alone:** `docs/features/spiritual-checkup.md` still schedules the visit *weekly* in the Grower and Endurer Ideal tiers. That track design predates this change and is unimplemented, but a 3-minute norm scheduled weekly is worth revisiting when those tracks get built.

- **One emphasis rule for both registers, and the two bugs that fell out of writing it (2026-08-06).** `InlineText` mapped `italic → italic` regardless of the block it sat in, so `*x*` inside a meditation — italic throughout — was italic inside italic and therefore invisible. Same defect the rubric had. Now one `composeStyle(node, base)` composes emphasis over the block's face and both registers call it: upright text slants, italic text flips to roman, bold adds weight and leaves the slant alone. `InlineMarkdownRubric` is `InlineText` with `base="italic"` rather than a second copy of the rule, and `PrayerText` feeds the same function when building justif segments, so the face the breaker measures and the face the renderer draws cannot drift.
  - **A block set with `fontStyle: 'italic'` over a roman family renders a SYNTHETIC OBLIQUE, whose advances are the roman advances.** Threading the base face into the segments made the justifier measure the true italic table while the browser sheared the roman one — and lines visibly re-wrapped, exactly the failure the spec warns about. Confirmed in the DOM: the meditation's `<span>` was `EBGaramond_400Regular` + `font-style: italic`. The fix is to name the face (`blockFace`) on the block's own `<Text>` instead of asking for a slant, which both makes the metrics honest and gets real EB Garamond Italic where a shear was standing in.
  - **A named italic face must say `fontStyle: 'normal'`.** These faces are registered as their own families (`EBGaramond_400Regular_Italic`), so adding `font-style: italic` on top makes the browser shear a true italic a second time. Only the synthetic path — families that load Regular only — should ask for `italic`. Split out as `faceProps`, and it is what lets an upright face inside an italic block stay upright at all.

- **`justif` does not resolve under Metro, and the failure is in its packaging, not ours (2026-08-06).** Every `justif` subpath publishes exactly `{types, import}` — no `require`, no `default`. Metro builds its condition set in `metro-resolver/src/utils/matchSubpathFromExportsLike.js` as `{'default', isESMImport ? 'import' : 'require', ...unstable_conditionNames, ...unstable_conditionsByPlatform[platform]}`, and Expo's `getDefaultConfig` leaves `unstable_conditionNames: []` with `conditionsByPlatform` of `{ios/android: ['react-native'], web: ['browser']}`. Nothing in that set appears in justif's map, so `justif/core` matches no condition and the **whole bundle dies** at `lib/typography/justifyText.ts` — web and native alike.
  - **Vitest hides it.** Vite resolves through Node's ESM rules, which honour `import`, so all 680 tests pass against a module Metro cannot load. A green suite is not evidence the app boots; the reader half of the feature is also immune, since it loads the vendored `justif.raw.js` inside the WebView rather than the package.
  - **`unstable_conditionNames = ['require','import']` does not lift it** (tried, with cleared caches). What works is mapping the subpaths onto the built files in `metro.config.js`'s `resolveRequest` — deliberately narrow, one package, and it falls away the moment justif publishes a `default` condition. Worth revisiting on any justif upgrade.

- **Rubrics parse markdown now, and emphasis inside them flips to roman (2026-08-05).** The rubric register is burgundy *italic*, so the obvious implementation — reuse `InlineMarkdownLine`, which resolves `*x*` to an italic face — would have produced emphasis indistinguishable from its surroundings. Emphasis inside an italic run goes **roman**; that is the standing typographic convention (Bringhurst, Chicago) and the only one that reads at rubric size. `**x**` keeps the slant and adds weight, `***x***` is bold roman. New `InlineMarkdownRubric` in `components/prayer/InlineMarkdown.tsx`, called as the child of `<Typography variant="rubric">`; five tests in `InlineMarkdownRubric.test.tsx` assert the face each node lands on.
  - **Every markdown emphasis span in the app had been rendering at 14px, `line-height: normal`, in black — for as long as `InlineText` has existed.** React Native Web's `Text` compiles to `<div class="css-text-146c3p1">`, whose base rule is `{ color: rgb(0,0,0); font: 14px -apple-system, …; }`. **`font` is a shorthand**, so it resets size, leading, weight, style and family in one go; an inline style naming only `fontFamily` overrides the family and leaves the rest of the reset standing. Nothing inherits.
    - Measured in the DOM rather than guessed at, which is the only reason the size half was found at all. The rubric span: `14px` / `line-height: normal` / `rgb(0,0,0)` against a parent at `16px` / `27px` / `rgb(107,29,42)`. Prayer bodies were worse — emphasis at **14px inside 22px text** with default leading, in `thursday-eucharist`, `holy-hour` and every meditation that italicises a phrase, plus annotation rows and todo notes.
    - Nobody had reported it because black-on-brown (`rgb(0,0,0)` vs `rgb(44,36,24)`) and 14-inside-22 read as "the italic looks a bit tighter." Against burgundy, at rubric size, the same bug is unmissable — which is how a content change surfaced a renderer defect that predates it.
    - Fixed once in `emphasisStyle` + the rubric spans with a shared `inheritFromParent`: `{ color, fontSize, lineHeight }` all `'inherit'`, web-only (native nested `<Text>` inherits correctly) and cast, since the CSS keyword isn't in RN's style types. Verified after: span and parent now agree on size, leading and ink at every call site, differing only in the face. **Emphasis names a face; it must never name an ink, a size or a leading.**
  - **Every span must name its face *and* say `fontStyle: 'normal'`.** The chosen EB Garamond face already carries whatever slant it needs (`EBGaramond_700Bold_Italic` for bold-in-rubric), so leaving the parent's italic in play slants a true italic a second time on web, where it is inherited CSS. On native the opposite failure applies — RN drops an inherited `fontStyle` once `fontFamily` is set. Naming both satisfies both platforms. This is the same trap `emphasisStyle` documents for prayer bodies, one level further in.
  - **Applied to all four authored-rubric call sites**, not just the flow primitive: `PrimitiveBlock`'s `rubric` case plus the `prompt` on `RenderedCaptureResolutionBlock`, `RenderedReviewResolutionBlock` and `RenderedCaptureMovementBlock`. Those prompts are authored content in the same register (the 2026-07-26 entry below: "a prompt is a `rubric`"), so leaving them raw would have made the same JSON behave differently depending on which block it sat in.
  - The 10 pre-existing instances across `morning-rule-byzantine`, `evening-rule-byzantine`, `wednesday-joseph` and `friday-passion` — all of them titles, foreign terms, or quoted prayer text — now render as their authors evidently intended, without touching those files.

- **The two mental prayer methods write into the resolutions loop, each at the point its own source names (2026-08-05).** Both sources end at a resolution and neither treats it as optional, so `capture-resolution` (`level: daily`, `for: "current"`) is a transcription, not an addition. Alphonsus puts it in Part II §3 — *"Nunca terminemos a nossa oração sem havermos formado uma resolução particular"* — and the Teresian method in part 6, the Offering: *"presenting the Lord with some good resolution — something which, in concluding prayer, is always useful to do."*
  - **Verified the loop end to end, not just the block:** captured a resolution in the morning mental prayer, reopened the practice (came back prefilled from the projection with a *Change* affordance, per `inWindow`), then opened `practice/examination-of-conscience` — which found it as the day's pending resolution to review. A resolution taken in Alphonsus's meditation at 07:30 is the one the examen asks about at night. That loop is the whole argument for wiring this up; a capture block that only talked to itself would not have been worth adding.
  - **What I did *not* add: a `review-resolution mode:"show"` in Alphonsus's Conclusion §2**, where the prayer says "the resolutions I have *just* taken" — which reads like an invitation to echo the capture back. It would be empty on the first pass every day: `withSpiritualThreads` takes `useEventStore.getState()` **once at resolve time** and the capture mutations don't invalidate the practice-content query, so a block placed after a capture in the same flow never sees it (documented in the 2026-05-13 entry as "flow resolution is a snapshot"). It reads correctly anyway — the saved resolution stays on screen as a carried line right above the conclusion, so the offering prayer already has its referent in view.
  - Both members of the `alternativeTo` group capture `for: "current"` into the same daily window, so praying one after the other in a single day revises rather than duplicates.

- **`practice/mental-prayer` was a house synthesis; replaced by the two methods we actually own the sources for (2026-08-05).** The shipped flow blended Teresa (friendship), Escrivá (conversation), de Sales (the bee, the bouquet) and Alphonsus (the fixed acts) into four "movements," padded with authored reflection prompts — *"What am I avoiding saying?"*, *"What part of me has not yet come to the surface?"* — that belong to no school and cite no text. Nothing in it was wrong; it just wasn't *from* anywhere, which makes it unteachable and unauditable. Both replacements are transcribed from books already in the corpus:
  - **`practice/mental-prayer` — St. Alphonsus.** Source is `content/books/liguori-meditacoes/pt-BR/modo-fazer-oracao-mental.md` (Herder 1921 Portuguese of the 1760 preface). Three parts, nine acts: preparation (faith in the presence / humility by a brief contrition / petition for light, then a Hail Mary and a Glory Be), meditation with its three fruits (affections, petitions, resolutions), conclusion (thanksgiving + pardon / offering of the resolutions / petition for the grace to keep them, then the commendation with an Our Father and Hail Mary), plus the *Depois da Meditação* trio. **The old flow had silently dropped the second preparatory act** — *"Lord, at this hour I should be in hell because of my sins"* — presumably as too severe; it's restored, because the method is nine acts and editing it into eight is exactly the customization being removed. pt-BR is the source text nearly verbatim; en-US follows the standard Benziger renderings, modernized to *You* (e.g. "Prelates," not "Pastors," for *Prelados*).
  - **`practice/mental-prayer-teresian` — Divine Intimacy.** Source is Fr. Gabriel of St. Mary Magdalen's own preface, in the corpus in all three languages at `content/books/gabriel-stmm-intimita-divina/{it,en-US,pt-BR}/prefazione.md`. Seven parts, and the preface is explicit about their weight: two introductory (preparation = presence of God, reading), two substantial (meditation, colloquy), three *facultative* (thanksgiving, offering, petition) that exist only to prolong the colloquy for souls who want variety. The flow marks that structure rather than flattening it — *"For many souls this is enough. If the colloquy carries you, stay in it and end here."* Unlike Alphonsus, the Teresian method fixes **no formulas**, so the flow is rubric-only apart from Teresa's definition (*Life* 8, 5) sitting under the preparation as the "good thought" that orients the soul.
  - **Two practices, not one flow with a `select`.** Schools/traditions of the same act are an `alternativeTo` group in this corpus (rosary/rosary-montfort, the stations, the morning offerings) — `select` is for context branching within one tradition. `mental-prayer` keeps the canonical id (six plan-of-life templates and `collection/base` ref it) and now declares `alternativeTo: {id: "mental-prayer", label: "St. Alphonsus Liguori"}` pointing at itself, which is how the canonical member of a group gets a variant label in `AdoptSheet`/`getAlternatives`. `plan-of-life-template/carmelite` re-pointed at the Teresian variant — templates may ref a non-canonical member, and `AdoptSheet` reads that as a variant swap rather than a second practice.
  - **Only the primary group member seeds** (`db/seed.ts` skips any manifest whose `alternativeTo.id` ≠ its own unqualified id), so the variant carries `tier: "extra", enabled: false` defaults like every other alternative — they're metadata for the adopt sheet, not a second daily slot.
  - `collection/mental-prayer`'s "Methodus Orationis Mentalis (St. Alphonsus Liguori) — as a practice, not the book" todo is now done and removed. The `collection/opus-dei` todo for a Josemaría variant still stands and its wording ("in place of the St. Alphonsus acts the current flow uses") is now literally accurate rather than approximately so.
  - **Superseded the same day — see the entry above: rubrics now parse markdown.** Kept for the diagnosis. **`rubric` did not parse markdown — and nothing in the schema told you that.** Both flows were authored with `*Divine Intimacy*` and `*not a general one*` in rubrics; on screen they render with the asterisks visible. `PrimitiveBlock.tsx:70` passes rubric text straight to `<Typography variant="rubric">`, while `prayer`/`meditation`/`text` go through `PrayerLines` → `InlineMarkdownLine` and *do* parse it. The two are indistinguishable in JSON. The old mental-prayer flow had the same bug in four places (`**Chew the word.**`, `*Imitate the bees…*`) and had presumably been shipping literal asterisks for months. Since a rubric is already fully italic, emphasis has nowhere to land — write plain text. Rule added to `primitives-guide.md` with a checklist item. **Nothing catches this at build time** — a grep of every practice flow for markdown inside a `rubric` finds 10 more instances in 4 practices (`morning-rule-byzantine` ×4, `evening-rule-byzantine` ×2, `wednesday-joseph` ×2, `friday-passion` ×2), all shipping literal asterisks today; left alone here as unrelated to this change.
  - **`subheading` renders at display size, so it is a label, not a sentence.** "1. Thanksgiving for the lights received, and pardon for the faults committed during the prayer" — a faithful transcription of Alphonsus's own act description — took four lines of large italic display type and buried the prayer under it. Shortened all three conclusion acts to the short labels `practice/meditacoes-ligorio` already uses for this same source text ("1º Agradecimento e pedido de perdão", …); the descriptive clause is redundant anyway, since the prayer immediately below says it in full. Faithful transcription is the right instinct for prayed text and the wrong one for headings.
  - **A method with no fixed formulas renders as a wall of burgundy.** The Teresian flow is nearly all rubric — the source gives directions, not prayers — and it opened with seven lines of red before anything else. The opening framing paragraph ("In mental prayer there is the exercise of the intelligence and that of the will… That is all.") is not a stage direction, it is reflective teaching, so it became a `meditation` (black italic). Correct primitive by the guide's own test, and it gives the flow a readable opening. Rendered check: `pnpm serve:hearth` + `pnpm start:web`, driven with Playwright in en-US and pt-BR; the dev console errors on both practices are identical in count and message to untouched practices (`rosary`, `angelus`), so they're Tamagui dev noise, not content.
- **Bible readings inside practices fell back to the Douay-Rheims for every non-English translation, online or not (2026-08-05).** `resolveBollsBookId` turned a DRB slug into a Bolls book id by matching the DRB's *English* book names against `/get-books/<translation>/` — but Bolls answers in the translation's own language ("Mateus" under CNBB, and something else again under VULG). Nothing matched, `getChapter` threw, and the `catch` handed back bundled DRB with `fallback: true`. So a pt-BR user's New Testament reading was English, under a notice reading *"(offline) — selected translation unavailable"* while they were online. **Since `pt-BR`'s default translation is CNBB (`defaultTranslationForLanguage`), this was every Portuguese user, every reading, since the lectio tracks shipped.**
  - **Fix: align by canon position, not by name.** Book *order* is the one thing every translation agrees on, so the nth book of the DRB index is the nth book of a 73-book list, and of a 66-book list once the seven deuterocanonical books are dropped (`tobias`, `judith`, `wisdom`, `ecclesiasticus`, `baruch`, `1-machabees`, `2-machabees` — everything else keeps its place). The id is then read *off the matched entry*, so nothing in the app hardcodes Bolls' numbering. A deuterocanonical book asked of a 66-book translation finds no position and correctly falls through to the bundled DRB, which is what `content-sources.md` already promised.
  - **The `(offline)` in both fallback notices was a lie in the common case** and sent the diagnosis in the wrong direction — the fallback fires on any resolution failure, not just a dead network. Copy now says what it knows.
  - **Corroboration for the numbering, if you ever need it:** `getBooks` splits testaments on `bookid <= 46`, and the DRB index has exactly 46 OT books with `matthew` at position 47. That is the Catholic canonical order, interleaved deuterocanon and all.

- **The bundled Douay-Rheims is corrupt in 22 of its 73 books — chapters missing, verses numbered book-wide (2026-08-05).** Not fixed, and it deserves its own pass. Two overlapping defects in `content/bible/drb/`: **21 books stop short** (`job` has 19 chapters of 42, `judges` 17 of 21, `john` 19 of 21 — no chapter 20 or 21, so the Douay-Rheims in this app has no Resurrection appearances in the Fourth Gospel), and **20 books number verses continuously across the whole book** instead of restarting each chapter (`ruth` chapter 2 holds verses 44–65, and verses 23–43 exist nowhere). `index.json`'s chapter counts agree with the broken files, so nothing in the app detects it. Affected: `numbers`, `josue`, `judges`, `ruth`, `2-kings`, `1-paralipomenon`, `2-esdras`, `judith`, `esther`, `job`, `proverbs`, `ecclesiasticus`, `jeremias`, `ezechiel`, `osee`, `amos`, `micheas`, `zacharias`, `2-machabees`, `john`, `1-corinthians`, `james`. **A verse-count check against a reference table belongs in the corpus build** — this has been shipping silently since the Bible landed, and it is the offline fallback for every translation.

- **Every numbered verse in the app hung one gutter past the right margin, and `flex={1}` was already there — on the wrong element (2026-08-05).** `VersesBlock`'s numbered rows are `XStack → [verse number, width 36] + BilingualBlock`, and the flex sat on the `PrayerText` *inside* `BilingualBlock`'s own `YStack`. That stack has no flex of its own, and RN's Yoga default is `flexShrink: 0` — so it took its intrinsic width, which the text measures at the **full row width**, ignoring the 36 px number column. Result: every verse wrapped at the correct measure but was translated right by the gutter, and the last ~44 px of each line was clipped off the screen edge. Present since the block was written (#283); the `style="vr"` branch two functions up has the correct `<YStack flex={1}>` wrapper, which is what made the diff obvious.
  - **The general rule: a flex prop only constrains the element it is on.** `renderText` callbacks hand their node to a component that wraps it, so any sizing intent has to be expressed on the wrapper the parent actually lays out — passing `flex={1}` down through `renderText` is inert by construction.
  - **The symptom reads as a justification bug and isn't.** Verse text is plain `PrayerText` under RN's native `textAlign: justify`, not `JustifiedText` — so the ragged first line, the wide inter-word gaps and the clipping all look like the Knuth–Plass work landing badly. Overflow that is *constant per row* is a measure bug; look at the container before the breaker.

- **Justified both reading surfaces with justif; the traps were all in the integration, not the algorithm (2026-08-05).** Spec: `docs/design/typography-justification.md`. Reader = justif's DOM renderer inside the foliate WebView; prayer = `justif/core` + build-time TTF metrics + a custom `<Text>` renderer, because React Native has no `wordSpacing` (confirmed in Fabric's `TextAttributes.h`).
  - **Justif does not change the plain-text character stream, which is why `highlightAnchor.ts` survives it.** It re-renders paragraphs as per-line `<span class="justif-seg">` clones, but inserted hyphens are painted *outside* the text tree (a segment showing `blind-` has `textContent === "blind"`) and inter-line spaces stay real spaces. Byte-identical over 25,347 chars with hyphenation active; 40/40 anchors round-tripped. Existing highlights and bookmarks needed no migration — **re-check this invariant on any justif upgrade.**
  - **The reader's real blocker was timing.** At parse time foliate has not sized the iframe, so the body is zero-width and justif declines every paragraph with `"zero content width"`. **Neither `rescan()` nor `refresh()` rescues it** — `rescan()` only re-lays out paragraphs whose *styling* changed, and a container-width change is not a style change, so it silently reports zero skips and does nothing. The `justify()` call itself has to wait for a real measure (paginator `load` handler + a ResizeObserver in the chapter document).
  - **A literal closing-script-tag sequence anywhere in `bootstrap.raw.js` breaks the reader — including inside a comment.** The host embeds the file as a script element and `JSON.stringify` does NOT escape `/`, so the tag ends early and the page dies with "Unexpected end of input" / `window.__foliateInit is not a function`. Cost an hour; the comment that introduced it was the one *documenting* the hazard. Write it split (`'</scr' + 'ipt>'`).
  - **Foliate's shadow root is closed**, so nothing in a chapter document is reachable from host page JS — hence the `window.__justifSkips` debug channel, and why headless verification goes through Playwright's frame API rather than `querySelector`.
  - **Native pipeline, three traps.** (1) **f-ligatures**: `afflict` measures 2.53 px wide unless U+FB00–FB04 are substituted before summing advances — enough to overflow a line and cascade a re-wrap. (2) **Letterfit tracking is a fraction of the line's set width, not a per-character amount**; modelling it as `trackRatio × 0.03 × fontSize` over-counted ~26 px/line. Fixed with `tracking: false` — word spaces are the only flex, RN hits those to the pixel, cost is one line in nineteen. The general rule: **any flex the breaker is allowed must actually be rendered, or lines silently re-wrap.** (3) **Soft hyphens** from `lib/hyphenate.ts` must measure zero-width. TTF advance widths validated against Chromium `measureText` over 276 corpus words: mean error 0.0002 px.
  - **Emphasis is justified, not excluded — and that's what per-face metrics are for.** `buildItems` takes an array of runs so a paragraph can mix faces, so `*Mater Dei*` is measured in the real italic and broken with everything else. The output is *pieces*, not words: `*Mater Dei*,` puts the comma back in regular, so one word can span two. The generator only emits faces the app actually **loads** — metrics must describe what renders. EB Garamond loads real italic/bold; the other six load Regular and synthesize, where **synthetic italic is an oblique shear that preserves advances** (regular metrics stay exact) but **synthetic bold is an unpredictable smear**, so bold on those families declines.
  - **Kerning is knowingly unmodelled.** These fonts have GPOS pair adjustments and no legacy `kern` table; expanding the class matrices gives ~3,000 pairs per face, ~1 MB across seven families. Measured cost of ignoring it on 36 real justified lines at 170 px: **0.72 px mean, 2.03 px worst**, nearly always leaving a line slightly short of the margin rather than overflowing. Not worth the bundle; `JustifiedText`'s existing 1 px headroom absorbs it, at the cost of a line occasionally sitting a hair short of the margin. Storing GPOS in its native class form would cost far less if this ever needs revisiting.
  - **Still unverified, and it gates the prayer surface:** whether UIKit widens a lone space under `letterSpacing` like CSS does (needs a simulator). `JustifiedText` falls back to plain wrapped text when the line model is unavailable, so the failure mode is "no justification", not broken text.
  - **`lang="la"` silently rewrites Latin orthography in EB Garamond.** A `locl` substitution swaps in classical epigraphic forms — `meum → mevm`, `quoque → qvoqve` — at **identical advance widths**, so no width check catches it. Live in the book reader today (`blobUrl()` sets `<html lang>`). Not fixed.
  - **Two measurement gotchas if you benchmark this.** Headless Chromium ships no hyphenation dictionaries, so `hyphens: auto` silently does nothing and native-vs-justif comparisons overstate the gap (iOS WebKit does honour it) — pre-insert soft hyphens for a fair baseline. And `Range.getClientRects().length` miscounts lines once soft hyphens or per-line spans exist; use `getBoundingClientRect().height / lineHeight`.

- **Divine Intimacy rendered empty (just the day rubric + Amém) — practice flows lost the race against the deferred book-manifest warm (2026-07-27).** The liturgical map, EF position, and chapter files were all correct (`post-pentecost/9/1 → giorno-255`); what failed was `loadBookChapterText → residentItem` — a sync resident-only read of the book manifest. Hearth book manifests are warmed by `warmDeferredManifests` *in parallel with first paint*, and `usePracticeContent` resolves with `staleTime: Infinity` and no `catalogVersion` in its query key — so a practice opened before the warm finished resolved zero chapter text and stayed empty for the rest of the day. Classic symptom: the rubric renders (the liturgical label comes from the practice's own data blob, warmed as `practice` kind) but every book-backed prose section vanishes silently. Fix follows the 2026-07-04 durable pattern: `ensureBookEntry()` (canonicalize → `ensureManifestBody`) resolves the manifest on demand inside `loadBookChapterText`, and `usePracticeContent` pre-ensures each `flow.resolve[].book` before `resolveFlowAsync` so the *sync* `getBookChapterTitle` calls (options labels, `meditationTitle`) also see it. `useMeditationSubtitle`'s Explore card had the same race (its comment even said "the first run loses the race to warming") — now also on `ensureBookEntry`. Rule: nothing outside boot warming may rely on a book manifest being resident; always ensure at the point of use.

- **Completed the Opus Dei norms of piety in the corpus; `trackHashes` only comes from a `tracks/` dir, and the flow validator's allow-list was two years stale (2026-07-24).** Audited the 18 daily norms of the Opus Dei plan of life against the corpus. Thirteen were already there; the template at `content/plan-of-life-templates/opus-dei.json` wired sixteen entries. Filled six gaps: `practice/prayer-pope-bishop`, `practice/particular-examination`, `practice/new-testament-reading`, `practice/spiritual-communion-opus-dei`, an `opus-dei` option on `practice/visit-blessed-sacrament`, and *Bendita sea tu pureza* + the holy-water rubric on `practice/three-hail-marys-opus-dei`. New `collection/opus-dei` gathers them in the order they fall through the day.
  - **`trackHashes` is populated *only* from a practice's `tracks/` directory — declaring `"tracks": {"x": "data/x.json"}` in the manifest silently produces a `dataHashes` entry instead.** `build_practices` walks `data/**` → `dataHashes` and `tracks/**` → `trackHashes` as two independent directory scans; the manifest's `data`/`tracks` blocks only supply *logical names* for the `data/` scan, and are stripped from the published manifest either way. The runtime split is hard: `loadPracticeData` reads `dataHashes` (for `cycle`), `loadPracticeTracks` reads `trackHashes` (for `lectio`), and the engine renders `[Reading track not loaded]` when `context.trackDefs[track]` is missing. **`practice/bible-catechism-in-year` has this bug today** — all four of its `lectio` tracks live under `data/`, so it publishes `trackHashes: null` and every reading block in it must be rendering the not-loaded rubric. Not fixed here (untouched by this work), but it's a one-line move of four files.
  - **Blob dedup makes "copying" a track free.** `new-testament-reading/tracks/nt-reading.json` is byte-identical to the one in `bible-catechism-in-year`, so it hashes to the same blob (`9b27d316…`) and the corpus grew by one 4KB catalog entry, not a second copy. Duplicating a data file across practices is the right move when the two practices want independent cursors — which is exactly the case here, since the norm's reading and the year-plan's reading advance separately.
  - **`scripts/validate-flows.ts` rejected five section types the engine has always supported.** `group`, `offering`, `capture-movement`, `capture-resolution` and `review-resolution` are all in `FlowSection` (`packages/content-engine/src/types.ts`), are rendered by `PrimitiveBlock.tsx`, and have engine tests — but were missing from `KNOWN_SECTION_TYPES`, so shipped practices (`examination-of-conscience`, `friday-mortification`, `morning-offering`) had been failing validation on their own working content. Added the five; error count dropped 111 → 94 and **every remaining error is the single pre-existing rule "manifest.flow must be a string path"**, which rejects the 93 shipped inline-flow prayer manifests that `build-corpus.py` explicitly prefers. Until that rule is fixed the validator can't be a green gate — but it is now clean of false positives on real section types, so the usable check is "no error outside that one rule."
  - **Source discipline on the fixed texts.** The two versicles in `prayer-pope-bishop` were not composed — they're the *Preces feriales* of the Roman Breviary, already in this repo at `content/do/horas/{Latin,English}/Psalterium/Special/Preces.txt:198-203` (Pope's response = Ps 40:3, bishop's = after Mic 5:4), with the Breviary's own rubric that a vacant see drops its versicle. The vernacular renderings match `practice/preces-opus-dei` so the two practices agree word for word. The indulgence note is *Manual of Indulgences* Norms 20 §5 / 23 §§1-3: prayer for the Holy Father's intentions is one of the four standing conditions, an Our Father and Hail Mary suffice, and the conditions may be fulfilled days before or after — which is the whole argument for fixing it to a morning slot.
  - **The struck-through `LA` under a prayer is a bug detector, not decoration.** `BilingualBlock.tsx:31-40` renders `content.secondaryMissing` as the secondary language's label with `textDecorationLine="line-through"` — so a reader whose secondary language is Latin sees a struck `LA` on every block that has no `la` key. On `morning-offering-opus-dei` that flagged two real defects: the heroic minute's `Serviam!` was sitting in the `en-US` and `pt-BR` keys (a Latin word stacked under vernacular labels, so it could never pair), and the closing line was `"Tudo para a glória de Deus. *Deo omnis gloria!*"` — vernacular and Latin mashed into one string with markdown italics, which is exactly what `primitives-guide.md` forbids. Both split into `la`/`en-US`/`pt-BR`. **When auditing a practice, turn the secondary language to Latin and scroll: every struck `LA` is either an honest "no Latin exists for this prayer" or a stacking bug.**
  - **`prayer ref:` defaults to collapsed, which can hide the entire point of a practice.** `morning-offering-opus-dei` rendered its offering — `offering-to-sacred-heart`, the prayer the practice exists to pray — as a one-line chevron row, so praying the norm took a tap to even see the text. Set `defaultOpen: true`. The guide's rule ("omit for memorized standards") is about *prayers inside* a flow; it doesn't mean the flow's own principal text should be collapsed. Sign of the Cross stays collapsed either side of it, correctly. Also dropped a redundant divider so the heroic minute (rise → *Serviam!* → sign yourself) groups above one rule and the offering below it.
  - **Still open (deliberately):** aspirations / presence of God (norm 15, also the `to-source-aspirations` TODO in `collections/base.json`), an Opus Dei variant of `practice/mental-prayer` carrying St. Josemaría's preparation and thanksgiving instead of the St. Alphonsus acts the current flow uses (norm 3), and a daily counterpart to `practice/friday-mortification` (norm 14). All three are recorded as `todo` blocks in `collection/opus-dei`.

- **The offering now reaches every prayer, as one muted line that most users will never see (2026-07-26).** Any prayer can be offered for someone, but an `offering` block in 350 flows would rebuild the wall of text this whole effort removed. `OfferingLine` renders under the practice header — where a Mass intention is announced, before the prayer rather than after — as a single `variant="caption"` line: *"Offered for My godson, and 2 others"*, or *"Offer this prayer for someone"* when nothing is carried. Tapping opens the offering sheet.
  - **Three rules keep it from becoming the papercut.** It renders nothing at all when the user has no active intentions, so it cannot nag someone who never asked for the feature. It is **never gold** — carrying an intention is not a call to action, and gold is preciousness only. And it defers to any flow that authors a real `offering` block, so the morning offerings and the Rosary keep their moment and nobody sees two offerings.
  - **Detecting "the flow authors its own" has a trap.** `hasOfferingBlock` must walk *two* shapes: resolved primitives (nested through container children and each `select` option's children) **and** `rawSections`, because a `select`'s unselected branches are never preprocessed and still carry engine-level `rendered-offering`. Miss the second and the Rosary shows both the line and the block the instant a user switches mysteries.
  - `OfferingPickerSheet` became the single editor behind both entry points — the block's "Carry more today" and the ambient line — listing what is carried (star to make standing, × to drop) above what else could be.

- **A perpetual intention could never be answered, so a granted lifelong petition had no exit but "Stop carrying" (2026-07-26).** `computeActions` gated "Mark answered" on `cadence === 'goal' || 'bounded'`, on the reasoning that "answered" is the wrong verb for something you carry forever. True for "the Holy Souls" — but a conversion, a healing, a reconciliation is *exactly* a perpetual intention, and when God grants it the only available verb filed the grace as attrition. Worse, the petition→thanksgiving bridge hangs off `IntentionAnswered`, so the intentions most worth giving thanks for could never produce a thanksgiving. Every intention can now be answered; perpetual gets gentler wording ("God answered this" / "Deus atendeu") rather than a different mechanism.
  - **The bridge is now a sheet, not a yes/no confirm**, and what the user writes does double duty: it becomes the thanksgiving *in their own words* instead of the robotic `For "{{text}}" — answered`, and it is stored as `IntentionAnswered.notes` and shown under the closed entry. That field had been in the event, the projection and the repository signature since the feature shipped, written by nothing. Answering still closes the intention on any exit path — "Not now", or a swipe-dismiss.
  - **`Movement.subject` was equally dead**: the field, `groupBySubject`, `useRecentSubjects(limit = 8)` and a full `movements.subject.*` i18n block all existed, and no UI ever wrote it, so grouping always returned one untagged bucket. New `SubjectField` (folded away behind "Add subject", with recent-subject chips so the same tag keeps its spelling) is wired into the Altar create sheet and the edit sheet.
  - **The Rosary finally carries intentions.** It is the practice `02-prayer-integration.md` Decision 5 names for `default: 'pinned'`, and it had no offering block at all. Added to all four mystery branches as the first thing in the flow — the Rosary is announced before it is begun.
  - Deleted `QuickCaptureChips` and `IntentionHeart` (exported, never mounted) and the `intentions.*` i18n block that existed only to serve them.
  - **a11y defect found while testing:** `MovementCard`'s inline "Answered" shortcut borrowed the action menu's `markAnswered` label, so a screen reader announced two controls with different behaviour — one opens the menu, one answers — identically. It now matches its visible text.

- **The web build was completely dead, and nobody knew because nothing had ever run it (2026-07-26).** `pnpm start:web` rendered a blank page. Four independent breakages, each fatal on its own, all in code that only native ever exercised:
  - **`@expo/ui` is native-only.** Its components call `requireNativeViewManager`, which throws on web — and `ConfirmHost` mounts a `BottomSheet` at the root of *every* screen, so the throw took the whole app down before first paint. Fixed with web shims (`src/lib/web-shims/`) wired through a `resolver.resolveRequest` branch in `metro.config.js` that only fires for `platform === 'web'`. Three entry points needed cover: `community/bottom-sheet`, `community/segmented-control`, `swift-ui` (+ `/modifiers`, whose modifiers collapse to inert tokens).
  - **`Appearance.setColorScheme`** doesn't exist in react-native-web; the unguarded call in `_layout.tsx` threw out of an effect on every render. It's a native UIKit concern, so it's now `Platform.OS !== 'web'`-guarded.
  - **`AccessibilityInfo.isReduceTransparencyEnabled`** likewise. `test/setup.ts` had *already* stubbed this for the same reason — the gap was known in tests and never fixed in the component. Guarded at the source; glass is an iOS material, so "no reduction" is the right web answer.
  - **`web.output: "static"`** made all of the above fatal at *route-scan* time, so even the error page failed to render. With the shims in, static SSR works again — no config change needed.
  - **Shim gotcha worth remembering:** a `position: absolute` backdrop paints *above* a static sibling regardless of DOM order, so the first version of the sheet swallowed every tap meant for its own content. The panel needs `position: relative`.
  - Still open, both dev-only warnings and both pre-existing: `selectable` (a native-only `Text` prop) reaches the DOM from ~10 components, and something emits `Unexpected text node` five times per screen.
  - **Metro dies with `EMFILE` in a container capped at 4096 file descriptors.** `lucide-react-native` ships ~1600 one-icon modules that the bundler opens concurrently, and `watchFolders = [monorepoRoot]` additionally drags `_site/hearth/v2/blobs` (~51k files after `pnpm build:corpus`) into the watch tree. `--max-workers 1` does not help. A `resolver.blockList` of `/\/_site\/.*/` is the obvious fix and is probably right — but `metro-config/src/defaults/exclusionList` is not an exported subpath in this version, and the plain-array form could not be verified booting here, so it is **not** committed. Raise `ulimit -n` first if you hit this locally.

- **The in-prayer interaction blocks were the only boxed elements on a page of manuscript, and it showed (2026-07-26).** Offering, capture-movement, capture-resolution and review-resolution all rendered as `borderWidth={1}` + `$backgroundSurface` rounded cards with raw `fontFamily`/`fontSize`, dropped into a flow of burgundy rubrics, Cinzel prayer names, justified EB Garamond and gold ornamental rules. Screenshotting the Morning Offering on web made it obvious: a form widget in a Book of Hours.
  - **The fix is mostly deletion.** New `components/prayer/FlowInteraction.tsx` (`FlowInteraction` / `FlowLine` / `FlowAction` / `FlowActions` / `FlowActionSeparator`) draws no box at all. A carried line is the gold `⟢` fleuron + the text, which is exactly the idiom `MovementCard` already used for a petition ("No bordered surface box; the list reads like the chronicle" — its own comment, which the flow blocks had ignored).
  - **A block's label is a `SectionHeading`**, the same italic Junicode as every other named section of the rite, so "Today's intentions" now sits at the same tier as "Acts of Faith, Hope, and Charity" instead of shouting in gold Cinzel caps. Per the Ladder, gold is preciousness only — it was doing label duty.
  - **A prompt is a `rubric`.** "Anything new this morning?" / "One concrete resolution for tomorrow." are instructions to the one praying, never prayed text, so they take the missal's red ink.
  - **Actions are mixed-case body serif in gold**, matching the home screen's "Add practices →" — an invitation should read as an invitation, not as a liturgical label. The full-width gold buttons are gone.
  - Verified in both themes: parchment and Tenebrae.

- **The Morning Offering no longer prints your whole register; the offering block's checkboxes are gone (2026-07-25).** All 12 authored `offering` blocks were byte-identical `default: "all-active"`, so every perpetual intention a user had ever raised was listed inside the prayer *every morning* — a wall of text that grows monotonically and teaches you to skip the section. Root cause was a design residue: `02-prayer-integration.md` Decision 4 specced a **pre-flow picker**, where "pre-select everything, user unchecks" is a defensible default. The picker was dropped during implementation (see the 2026-05-13 entry, "the `offering` block IS the picker") and `all-active` came along unchanged into a context it was never designed for.
  - **`default` is now `pinned` everywhere**, and `usePinnedFor` finally has consumers — before this, *no shipped content used `default: 'pinned'`*, so the entire pin subsystem (`MovementPinned`/`Unpinned`, the projection, `PinPracticeSheet`) was reachable but affected no flow.
  - **The checkboxes were theater and are deleted.** Selection lived in a local `useState<Map<string, boolean>>`, was read by nothing but the `show: 'count'` label, and evaporated on unmount. It cost attention every morning and could never answer "how long have I prayed for this?" (there is still no `IntentionOffered` event — persisting offerings remains unbuilt). Replaced by two gestures that are real: a **star** per row toggles the pin (durable, so it returns tomorrow), and **"Carry more today"** opens `OfferingPickerSheet` for one-sitting additions. Everything rendered is being offered — no state that lies.
  - **Unstarring mid-prayer keeps the line for this sitting.** Yanking text out from under someone praying is worse than a stale row; the id moves into `carriedIds` on unpin.
  - **`morning-offering-sacred-heart` had no offering block at all** while its ten siblings did — pick that variant and your intentions silently vanished. Added.
  - **Orphaned i18n retired:** `movements.picker.*` (the leftover of the deleted picker) is now the "carry more" sheet's copy; `movements.offering.pickHint` is gone with the checkboxes.

- **`cadence: 'bounded'` was unreachable-by-crash in every in-flow capture (2026-07-25).** `RenderedOfferingBlock` and `RenderedCaptureMovementBlock` both rendered the full 3-way `CadenceToggle` and then called `raiseIntention({ text, cadence })` with **no `bounded_until`** — which `movements.ts` rejects. The global `MutationCache.onError` host in `_layout.tsx` caught it and showed the raw developer string *"bounded_until is required for bounded cadence"* in a modal, mid-prayer, and the intention was not saved. Only `AltarCreateSheet` had the date picker that makes `bounded` valid. Extracted it as `BoundedUntilPicker` (+ `defaultBoundedUntil()`) and used it in all three. **Lesson: a control that can select an option must reach every field that option requires — the toggle and the term belong to the same component boundary.**
