- **Intentions had no edit UI for two months while the whole write path sat finished (2026-07-25).** `IntentionUpdated`, its projection arm, `updateIntention`, and `useUpdateIntention` were all implemented, exported, tested — and **called by nothing**. `computeActions` in `MovementActionMenu` only offered answered / pin / retire, so a typo in an intention was permanent, a cadence could never change, and a bounded window could never be extended. Added `MovementEditSheet` (text + `CadenceToggle` + `BoundedUntilPicker`, thanksgivings get text only) behind a new "Corrigir / Edit" action. **Worth grepping for other finished-but-uncalled mutations** — a fully-wired repository function with zero call sites is the cheapest feature in the codebase and the easiest to forget.

- **`expiry.ts` swallowed sweep failures with `.catch(() => {})` ×3 — which `CONVENTIONS.md` forbids using this very feature as its worked example (2026-07-25).** The honest resolution isn't a user-facing dialog: the lazy overlay in `hooks.ts` already reports an overdue bounded intention as closed, so the state the user sees is correct whether or not `IntentionExpired` was written, and the next sweep retries. Interrupting prayer with a modal over a background bookkeeping write would be worse. Replaced with a named `reportSweepFailure` that logs with context and a comment explaining *why* this is the one acceptable non-surfacing path. **The rule is "never swallow silently", not "always show a dialog" — but the reason has to be written down at the catch site.**

- **Two integration-harness bugs that made whole categories of UI untestable (2026-07-25).**
  - **Every `BottomSheet` rendered as `null` in tests.** The `@expo/ui/community/bottom-sheet` stub in `test/setup.ts` keyed on `props.isOpened`, but every call site in the app drives the sheet with `index={visible ? 0 : -1}`. No test had exercised a sheet, so nobody noticed. The stub now accepts either.
  - **`renderApp` had no way to seed pre-existing user state.** The DB is only initialized inside `bootOnce()`, so calling a repository before `renderApp` throws "Database not initialized", and calling it after means the screen has already rendered without it. Added a `seed?: () => Promise<void>` option that runs after `resetForTests` (which wipes events) and before the first render. Use it for anything the screen should open *with* — standing intentions, past completions.
  - **`expo-web-browser` had no stub**, which broke *both* existing `PracticeFlow` integration tests (`dailyLoop`, `examen`) outright on `main` — they were red before this work started, failing at route import with `Cannot find native module 'ExpoWebBrowser'`. Per `CONVENTIONS.md`, a new native dependency needs a stub in the same PR; this one was missed.
  - **`findAllByText` on a first flow resolution needs an explicit `timeout: 5000`.** The 1s default is fine when a heavy integration file runs alone and flakes once the suite runs 74 files in parallel. The `dailyLoop` test already did this for its cross-navigation assertion; its own first assertion didn't.

- **Residual post-load interaction hangs: the in-session catalog re-fetch re-downloaded + re-parsed + re-wrote 522KB every launch; fixed with a conditional GET, and the book warms were deleted (2026-07-04).** After PR #278's three fixes, taps/scroll still stuttered for a beat right after boot. Culprit: the `runAfterInteractions` background refresh (`_layout.tsx`) calls `loadCatalogFromHearth({networkFirst:true})`, which unconditionally downloaded `catalog.json` (**522KB, ~1930 items, growing**), `JSON.parse`'d it, and `setCache`'d it (`JSON.stringify` + SQLite write) **on every launch even when nothing changed** — tens of ms of JS-thread work in the interaction window.
  - **Fix = HTTP conditional GET in `fetchHearth` (`lib/hearth.ts`).** GitHub Pages serves a strong ETag (via Varnish) and honors `If-None-Match` → **304 with no body** when unchanged, so we skip transfer + parse + stringify + write entirely. ETag is stored in the **cache table** under `${key}:etag` (not preferences — `setPreference` fires a cross-tab `broadcastChange`; and the `hearth:` prefix means `clearCache('hearth:')` already wipes it on a hearth switch). Guards: branch on `res.status === 304` *before* `!res.ok` (304 isn't ok); on a 304 with an empty body cache (etag outlived the body), re-fetch once unconditionally. `conditional` is off for `isLocal` dev. Generic in `fetchHearth`, so any mutable named file benefits; hash-addressed blobs (`getJson`) are immutable and don't need it.
  - **The boot "warms" for external books were unnecessary background work.** `warmCccBooks()` deleted — its manifests are static/synchronous, so `registerCccCatalog()` now `rememberManifestBody`s them inline at registration (resident immediately for sync cross-ref anchors; on-demand resolver kept as fallback). `warmEscrivaBooks()` deleted — it fetched **28 TOCs from escriva.org fire-and-forget every boot**; opening a book already resolves the manifest async via TanStack Query → `ensureManifestBody` (book details `index.tsx`). The one gap: `BookReader` read `getBookEntry()` **synchronously**, so a deep link straight into the reader (no details screen first) relied on the warm — fixed by making `BookReader` resolve its manifest via `useQuery`+`ensureManifestBody` with the resident manifest as `initialData` (instant on normal nav, async only on cold deep link). `BookReader.tsx` cross-ref reads stay sync — Hearth books are covered by `warmDeferredManifests`, CCC is now resident, and nothing cross-refs Escrivá books.
  - **Root cause under all book warms:** `getBookEntry → residentItem → getRememberedManifest` is sync/resident-only with no fetch fallback. Anything that needs a book manifest synchronously forces a warm to exist. The durable pattern is to resolve referenced manifests on the async path (`ensureManifestBody`) at the point of use, not warm everything at boot.

- **CCC + Compendium became external books scraped live from vatican.va; the bespoke catechism reader was deleted (2026-06-19).** The full Catechism used to be a third-party `ccc.json` (English-only) read by a custom 3-pane reader (`features/catechism/`, `stores/catechismStore`, `lib/catechism.ts`). Both are gone. Now `book/ccc` and `book/compendium` are **external books** (`BookEntry.source = { type:'external', producer:'producer/ccc-book' }`, same mechanism as the Escrivá books) registered at boot by `content/cccCatalog.ts`, read with the normal book reader, scraped from vatican.va on demand and cached per-device in `external_content`. Hard-won gotchas:
  - **The full CCC IS on vatican.va in Portuguese** — at `archive/cathechism_po/` (note the misspelling "cathechism", same quirk as the Compendium's `_po`). The CCC index (`archive/ccc/index.htm`) lists every language. Don't trust the archive landing page, which only shows languages for the Compendium.
  - **EN and PT are paginated completely differently.** PT = one clean page per chapter, paragraph range in the filename (`p1s1c1_26-49_po.html`) → no crawl. EN (`ENG0015/__P*.HTM`) = ~374 tiny IntraText pages (~9 paragraphs each), base-36 codes (P1…PZ,P10…) chained by "Next" links, **no** paragraph→page map on the site. We crawl the Next-chain once (`scripts/build-ccc-en-index.mjs`) into `sources/ccc/en-pages.json`; the text is frozen (HTML dated 2003).
  - **One canonical TOC, shared across languages** (the reader enforces a single TOC; chapters keyed by `pointRange`/paragraph). We adopt the PT chapter divisions (27 leaves, §1–2865 contiguous, in `sources/ccc/structure.ts`). EN pages straddle chapter boundaries, so a chapter's assembled HTML is `trimToRange`'d to drop leaked neighbour paragraphs — but far-out-of-range embedded numbers (the Ten Commandments enumerated 1–10 at §2051) are kept-but-untagged, not dropped.
  - **IntraText markup is maddeningly inconsistent.** "Next" links are `<a href=__P3.HTM>Next</a>` early and `<A\nhref="http://…__P86.HTM">Next</A>` later. Paragraph numbers are bare (`<p class=MsoNormal>1 …`), bold (`<b>`), or italic with a style attr (IN BRIEF: `<p class=MsoNormal><i style='…'>2857`). The crawler/parser must allow attrs on `<i>`/`<b>` and filter candidate numbers by **monotonic sequence** (else a page enumerating the Ten Commandments looks like it contains "paragraphs 1–10" and gets range `[1,2051]`, polluting the prologue).
  - **The PT source has real typos:** §2217 is printed "2117", §2439 is "1439" (digits transposed). The text is present, just mis-numbered. Two paragraphs out of 2865 — not worth chasing. EN has one merged paragraph (§2077 shares a `<p>` with §2076). Net: completeness is essentially perfect.
  - **Cross-references work** — the Compendium's answers emit `<a data-ref="book/ccc#1-25">`; the foliate WebView (`bootstrap.raw.js`) now posts `data-ref` taps, and `BookReader` resolves `book/<id>#<anchor>` via the target book's `anchors` index (jump within book, or `router.push` across books). Re-run `node bundle.mjs` in `features/books/reader/foliate/` after editing `bootstrap.raw.js`.
  - **`setManifestBodyResolver` was single-slot** — made it an ordered list so Escrivá + CCC resolvers coexist.

- **Bespoke holy cards moved out of app code into a Hearth-served data file (2026-06-18).** Adding a saint card used to mean editing TypeScript in three places — `features/saints/data/saints.ts` (the id + image + i18n-key array), `data/catalog.ts`'s `bespokeFeast` map, and a 4-key `saints.<id>` block in **both** locale files — then shipping an app release. Now the whole catalog is `content/practices/saint-of-the-day/data/holy-cards.json` (`{ version, cards: [{ id, feast:{month,day}, name, patronOf, prayerExcerpt }] }`, strings as `{ "en-US", "pt-BR" }` `LocalizedText`). Adding a card = append an object + drop `content/saints/<id>.webp` + `pnpm build:corpus`. No app code, no release. Gotchas:
  - **It rides the existing saint-of-the-day practice, not a new corpus kind.** The build already emits any `data/*.json` a practice declares in its manifest `data` block as a hash-addressed `dataHashes` entry keyed by logical name (see `build_practices`). So registering `"holy-cards": "data/holy-cards.json"` in the manifest was the entire build-side change — `useHolyCards.ts` fetches it the same way `useSaintOfDayIndex.ts` fetches the day index (look up `practice/saint-of-the-day` → `getRememberedManifest` → find the named `dataHashes` ref → `getJson`). New corpus *kinds* need Python + manifest-type work; new *practice data files* need neither.
  - **The gallery is now async on both inputs.** `useSaintsCatalog` previously rendered the bespoke cards synchronously (imported array) and only the Pictorial Lives index warmed in. Both blobs now warm from Hearth, so `build()` tolerates either being `undefined` and the wall fills in as bytes land — consistent with how the index already behaved. Saints were never part of the offline starter pack, so nothing regressed there.
  - **`id` is the image stem.** `cardImage` is derived as `{ uri: hearthAssetUrl('saints/${id}.webp') }` in the catalog — the data file carries no image URL, so a card's id must match its `content/saints/<id>.webp` filename (snake_case, as before).
  - **The per-saint i18n blocks were deleted, not kept as fallback.** Leaving 66 dead `saints.<id>` entries would have invited the next author to "also" edit them. The `saints.*` UI strings (title, group, encounter, etc.) stay in i18n; only the per-saint name/feast/patron/prayer left. The old `feastDay` string ("October 1") was dropped entirely — it was only ever a fallback for cards without a feast, and every bespoke card has one, so `feastLabel` is always computed from `feast` via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`.

- **Opus Dei daily content — feed + Gospel commentary tab + meditation practice (2026-06-16).** Three opusdei.org surfaces, all scraped at runtime by `apps/app/src/sources/opus-dei/` (mirroring the Vatican News source): a "From Opus Dei" row on Explore (Atom feed), a "Reflection" tab inside `practice/gospel-of-the-day` (between Gospel and Word of the Pope), and a new `practice/opus-dei-meditation` surfaced as an Explore featured-carousel block. Gotchas that bit:
  - **The `lastarticles.xml` feed is bare Atom with no images** (only `xmlns="…/Atom"`; no `media:`/`enclosure`). Card art comes from each article page's `og:image` in a second per-article fetch — the same two-step the Vatican row uses for thumbnails. The CDN image URL takes a `&w=` width param, so we shrink the 1200px social image to 600 for cards.
  - **Article body lives in `<div class="imperavi-body" itemprop="articleBody">`.** Scope all lookups to `<main>` first — the page header carries its own hidden `<h1>Opus Dei` (logo) and sidebars carry "related" modules that would otherwise leak in.
  - **Gospel page = passage + `<hr>` + commentary.** EN uses a literal `<hr>`; both langs head the reflection with a `Commentary`/`Comentário` `<p><strong>` label. We keep only the reflection (the scripture is already the practice's Gospel tab). PT pages don't always have a parseable `<hr>` form, so prefer the label paragraph and fall back to `<hr>`.
  - **Per-run `collapseWs().trim()` mashes inline-citation spacing.** The Vatican parser trims each text node; that's safe for its plain `<p>` markup but on Opus Dei (heavy inline `<em>` scripture + `<a>` cross-refs) it turned `behaviour, <em>the</em>` into `behaviour,the` and `(1 <em>Kings</em> 20:43)` into `(1Kings20:43)`. Fix: collapse but **don't trim** text nodes, then trim only the paragraph's first/last run. A regex assertion (`not.toMatch(/\w,\w/)`) guards it.
  - **Footnotes have two forms.** In-body refs are `<a id="_ftnref1"><sup>[1]</sup></a>` (the `[1]` is stripped as a footnote-ref run); the trailing definition list is separate `<p>`s opened by `<a id="_ftnN">`. The `[n]` marker gets eaten before any text check, so detect definition paragraphs **structurally** (first child `<a id="_ftnN">`), not by leading-`[1]` text.
  - **Native-only.** Pages + feed are CORS-blocked, so the producers/row degrade to a link-out on web and on any failure; `dateScoped: true` keys the per-day cache. The producers' attribution/link-out emit a new **`link` primitive** (`{ type: 'link', text, href }`, rendered by `components/prayer/LinkBlock.tsx`) — added so sources stop embedding raw URLs as prose and external links are tappable (in-app browser native / new tab web), with the open behavior owned by the renderer. Validated against real (trimmed) page markup in `sources/opus-dei/__fixtures__/` via `parse.test.ts` — eyeballing rendered markdown (WebFetch) hid the spacing/footnote bugs; only running the raw HTML through the parser surfaced them.

- **EF display calendar unified onto the Divinum Officium engine (2026-06-16).** The EF half of the calendar (card, month grid, day detail) was the last consumer of the curated `content/liturgical/entries.json` (via `buildYearCalendar`), so it could drift from what the EF Mass/Office actually celebrates. New `buildDoYear` (`@ember/divinum-officium`, `kalendar/year.ts`) loops `resolveDay` — the *same* precedence/occurrence authority the EF Mass uses — across the year and emits a neutral row shape; the app's `buildDoYearCalendar` maps it onto the `Map<string, DayCalendar>` every consumer already expects (mirrors `buildOfYearCalendar`). Branch in `useYearCalendar`: OF→`buildOfYearCalendar`, EF→`buildDoYearCalendar`. Gotchas:
  - **The winner's name is already in `state` after `occurrence` — no section loading needed.** `resolveDay` returns `{ …, state }`; `state.srank[0]`/`state.trank[0]` hold the celebration's Latin name (from the Kalendarium), and `state.sanctoraloffice` says which cycle won. So the calendar never needs the (expensive) officestring text assembly. Added `sections?: boolean` to `resolveDay`: when `false` it returns right after the precedence/duplex block with empty section trees. Also added an optional `directorium?` param so the year loop builds the transfer/version tables **once** instead of 365×. Both are behavior-preserving for existing callers (defaults unchanged; the differential gate still passes).
  - **DO names are Latin, full stop.** The file `Rank` line's *name* field is usually empty (e.g. `Sancti/01-25` = `;;Duplex majus;;4;;…`); the human name comes from the Kalendarium table, which is **Latin only**. So "full-canonical" EF = Latin sanctoral titles (fitting for the EF) and **no descriptions** (DO has no prose). Temporal names are left empty so `useCelebrationDisplay` resolves them via `getLiturgicalDayName` (vernacular) — exactly the OF temporal pattern. `localizeContent` falls back to the first available value, so a `{ la }`-only name renders. The hybrid (curated names over DO precedence) is still possible later without rearchitecting.
  - **Rank scale (Rubrics 1960), calibrated empirically against real data:** I/II-class are 6–7 / 5, III-class the doubles (3–4), IV-class lesser feasts, simplices/commemorations below ~1.5. `normalizeEfRank` maps the DO number (+ rank-text for vigils) to `RankEF`. Don't trust the rank *text* alone — the shared files still carry pre-1960 class words ("Duplex majus") under the 1960 version, so the *number* is the reliable signal.
  - **Surfacing rule:** sanctoral winner → show it; else privileged temporal (rank ≥ 6: Advent/Lent/Passion/Easter Sundays + Lord's feasts) → show it; else a commemorated saint (srank present) → show it so summer ferias still read as a saints' calendar; else skip (ordinary green Sundays rank 5 / plain ferias). Mirrors the OF "drop Ordinary Time Sundays" behavior.
  - **Perf:** a full year resolves in ~0.85s with `sections:false` + shared directorium; query is `staleTime: Infinity`. First cold build still fetches the per-day tempora/saint blobs (the same the EF Mass would), so it's heavier than the OF's two static JSONs — acceptable for a forever-cached query; precompute is a possible later optimization.
  - **`@ember/divinum-officium` stays pure** (no `@ember/liturgical` dep): `buildDoYear` returns a DO-native row (`DoCalendarDay`) and the *app* owns the `RankEF`/`DayCalendar` mapping, so the package keeps its in-package fs-loader tests (`year.test.ts`) with no curated-data coupling.
  - **Env wrinkle:** `@ember/divinum-officium` is a declared app dep but its `node_modules/@ember/divinum-officium` symlink was missing in the fresh container (stale install), so `tsc`/Metro couldn't resolve it (pre-existing — it broke `do-mass.ts` too). `pnpm install` (or recreating the symlink) fixes it.

- **Celebration-of-the-day / calendar unified onto the canonical OF authority (2026-06-14).** The home celebration card, month grid, and day detail used to read a *separate* hand-curated `content/liturgical/entries.json` via `buildYearCalendar`, while the Mass read the canonical MR statics via `resolveOfDay`. They drifted: `entries.json`'s Sacred Heart `anchor_relative` rule resolved to **Easter+61** (Friday after Trinity) instead of the correct **Easter+68**, so the solemnity showed in the Mass but vanished from the card. Fix: a new `buildOfYearCalendar` (`@ember/mass`, `of/calendar/year.ts`) loops `resolveOfDay` across the year and emits the **same** `Map<string, DayCalendar>` shape `buildYearCalendar` does, so no consumer changed. The hooks branch OF→new builder, EF→entries.json (EF kept as-is). Gotchas worth remembering:
  - **Rank separator mismatch.** `@ember/missal-schema` `Rank` is hyphenated (`optional-memorial`); `@ember/liturgical` `RankOF`, the `calendar.rank.*` i18n keys, and `rankColors` use underscores (`optional_memorial`). The builder must `normalizeRank` or badges/colors silently break.
  - **Temporal celebrations carry no title in the *statics* — the name lives in the Mass formulary.** `resolveOfDay` returns a celebration for *every* day (ferias, ordinary Sundays) and `temporal.json` entries have no `title` (only the *formulary* does, on all 390 temporal formularies). So the builder leaves temporal `entry.name` empty and the UI resolves the title from the formulary via `useCelebrationDisplay` (the single source of truth for both title and description), falling back to `getLiturgicalDayName` while it loads / for the month-grid a11y labels + the upcoming banner (which don't fetch formularies). `temporal-notability.ts` is *only* the filter — which temporal days are surfaced (the solemnities/feasts of the Lord by `tempore.*` ref or `getOfLiturgicalPosition().specialDay`; ordinary Sundays/ferias dropped). Christmas's four Masses collapse to one display entry. (Earlier this baked bilingual title strings; that duplicated the formulary titles, so it was removed — names now have one home.)
  - **Descriptions live in the formulary, not the calendar.** `of-data`/the calendar statics carry *no* description — the "Sobre esta celebração" prose is `MassFormulary.description` (`content/of/formularies/**`). The card/detail load it on demand via `useFormularyDescription(ref)`. Coverage is lopsided (**207 pt-BR vs 2 en-US**); we deliberately do **not** fall back to the Pictorial Lives saint reflection (it isn't liturgical) — when the formulary has no description the card/screen simply omit it.
  - **HDO needed a new single source.** The 11 holy-day-of-obligation flags lived only in `entries.json`; once OF stopped reading it, `of/calendar/hdo.ts` became the canonical OF HDO table (`obligations.ts` still reads `principal.entry.holyDayOfObligation`, now set by the builder).
  - **`saintArtMap` was removed.** It bridged calendar ids → the 14 curated holy cards to show art on the celebration card, but it only fired on ~12 feasts/year and duplicated the dedicated Saint-of-the-Day card's Pictorial Lives portrait. `useSaintOfDay` now returns just `{ celebration }`; the celebration card/screen render typographic-on-tone (no holy-card image). (En route it was briefly re-keyed to formulary refs before being dropped.)
  - **OF scope follows content language** (`scopeForContentLang(contentLanguage)`), matching the Mass — *not* the `jurisdiction` pref the EF override path uses.
  - Retired the now-dead generated `content/liturgical/of-calendar.json` + `scripts/build-of-calendar.mjs` (only a stale comment + one test consumed it).

- **Intermittent app-wide touch/scroll freezes = blocked JS thread; the three culprits and the new guardrails (2026-06-12).** Symptom signature: touches and scroll dead but the bottom tab bar still responds — the tab bar is UIKit-native (expo-router `NativeTabs`), everything else needs JS. Diagnosed three independent blockers; all fixed, plus a dev-only stall detector (`lib/stallMonitor.ts`, mounted in `_layout.tsx`) that logs `[stall] JS thread blocked ~Xms` when its heartbeat gap exceeds 500 ms.
  - **expo-file-system's new API is half synchronous.** `bytes()`/`text()`/`base64()` are async, but `write()`, `delete()`, `create()`, `list()`, and the `exists`/`size`/`modificationTime` getters are **synchronous JSI calls** that block the JS thread. Never use them in hot paths or loops: the old `listCachedBlobs` triple-nested `list()` walk was thousands of sync stats for a near-200MB cache (now yields per shard dir), and every downloaded blob used to be `write()`n on the JS thread. For downloads, `File.downloadFileAsync(url, file, { idempotent: true })` is disk-to-disk native (verified in `FileSystemModule.swift`: rejects on non-2xx, network error, and existing destination without `idempotent`) — bytes never enter JS. `store.ts:ensureBlobCached` stages into `blobs-tmp/` and atomically `move()`s into `blobs/` after validation, so a killed app can't leave a truncated blob behind a forever-trusted hash.
  - **`catalogVersion` bump-storms re-rendered the app 3× per launch for nothing.** The post-boot background refresh re-fetches `catalog.json` and re-warms manifests every launch; `setCatalog` used to unconditionally invalidate + bump (one app-wide re-render of every `useCatalogVersion` subscriber), and both warm functions bumped again even with zero new manifests. Now `setCatalog` skips when `generated` matches (non-empty guard keeps test semantics) and the warm functions only notify when they actually loaded something — a no-change refresh causes zero re-renders. Also, the eviction pass (`pinnedHashes()` manifest-tree walk + the sync stat walk) now runs at most once per 24h via `content/cacheMaintenance.ts`. Pattern to keep: anything that "refreshes" on a timer/launch must prove change before notifying.
  - **Practice screens mounted their whole primitive tree synchronously.** Mass/offices render hundreds of nested Tamagui blocks inside a plain ScrollView; mounting them all at navigation time blocked JS through the whole transition. `hooks/useProgressiveCount.ts` chunks the mount (16 first, +24 per post-interaction frame, reset on sections-identity change); applied in `PracticeFlowView` and the chapter reader. Limitation: top-level only — a flow that is one giant `container` doesn't benefit. The book reader is a WebView and needed nothing.
  - Also: `expo-file-system` is now statically imported in `store.ts`/`mediaDownload.ts` (Platform-guarded) instead of a conditional `require` — the package ships a web variant, and bare `require` bypasses vitest's `vi.mock`, making the native path untestable (`store.native.test.ts` forces `Platform.OS='ios'` with a stateful FS mock).

- **OF Mass woven ordinary + the cache/dev-server traps that hid it (2026-06-11).** Rebuilt the OF Mass into `producer/mass-of` (direct-to-primitives, an Ordo spine weaving Order + propers, chip selectors). The model/extractor details live in `docs/mass-rework-journal.md`; the *gotchas* that cost the most time:
  - **A producer's output cache has no TTL and ignores the corpus hash.** `runCachedSource.ts` keys SQLite on `producerVersion` + prefs only. The mass producers read corpus blobs whose hashes change every build, so a static `version: '1'` renders the **old corpus forever** after a rebuild — the app fetched the new `catalog.json` but never the new order blob, and since `_site/blobs/` *accumulates* old blobs (content-addressed, never pruned) the stale catalog's blobs still 200'd with no error. Fix: `get version() { return \`2:${getCatalog().generated}\` }` so the catalog's build stamp busts the cache. Any corpus-reading producer must do the same.
  - **`expo start --web` does not serve native bundles.** Testing on iOS while only the `--web` dev server ran → `index.bundle?platform=ios` is a hard 404, so the device silently kept an old bundle and `fetchHearth` fell back to **production** Hearth (old published corpus) on local-timeout. "As if you did nothing" almost always = the device isn't loading this worktree's JS/corpus, not a code bug. Use `expo start --dev-client` (= `pnpm start`) for native; check the Hearth + Metro access logs for the device IP before suspecting code.
  - **`SelectBlock` only lazily preprocesses non-default branches from `rawSections`.** A `select` whose options carry pre-built `children` (no `rawSections`) rendered **blank** for every option except the default — `SelectBranch` returned `undefined`. Producers that emit final primitives into select options need `SelectBranch` to render `children` directly (fixed).
  - **Drop-caps: the upstream `<span class="cap">` lost the boundary space in the parser**, gluing `O`+`Senhor`→`OSenhor`, `A`+`graça`→`Agraça`. The space is recoverable: `segments-to-lines.ts` keeps the following text node's leading space; `richtext.ts` (baseline already trimmed) uses next-char case (glue a lowercase continuation, space before a new capital). Drop-caps are now eliminated corpus-wide.
  - **`''.join` ≠ the renderer.** Auditing corpus text with Python `''.join(seg.text)` shows `comSenhor` where the renderer's `joinLine` adds the inter-segment space (`com Senhor`). Don't flag "missing space" scannos from a no-separator dump.

- **`select on: "hour"` silently resolved to hour 0 in every practice (2026-06-11).** `useToday()` deliberately returns the *logical day at midnight* (4am-cutoff rule; its doc warns against calling `.getHours()`), but `usePracticeContent` passed it as `FlowContext.date`, and the engine's `hour`/`timeOfDay` context values read `date.getHours()` — so hour-mapped selects (liturgy-of-the-hours, divine-office) always saw hour 0 and defaulted to the night option. Fix at the engine boundary: `FlowContext` gained an optional wall-clock `now` that `hour`/`timeOfDay` prefer, and the practice-content query passes `new Date()` plus the current hour in its query key so the default follows the clock through the day. Any future host that resolves hour-mapped flows must pass `now` — `date` alone reads as midnight.

- **iBreviary LOTH producer — session-cookie date setting, two markup dialects, ora_media splitting (2026-06-10).** `producer/breviary-of-the-day` (`apps/app/src/sources/ibreviary/`) scrapes ibreviary.com/m2 for the `liturgy-of-the-hours` practice. The non-obvious mechanics:
  - **The date can only be set by POST.** `GET /m2/opzioni.php?giorno=…` silently ignores the params; only a form-urlencoded POST (`lang`, `giorno`, `mese`, `anno`, `ok=ok`) updates the `PHPSESSID` session, and `GET /m2/breviario.php?s=<section>` then serves that day. RN's `response.headers.get('set-cookie')` is unreliable, so we never parse cookies — the native HTTP stacks persist the jar themselves. The trade-off: the session is *shared mutable state*, and `SelectBlock` prefetches all hour branches at mount, so concurrent fetches would race the date. `session.ts` serializes every POST+GET pair through a module-level promise chain. **All iBreviary traffic must go through `fetchSectionHtml`** — any fetch outside the queue reintroduces the race.
  - **The two editions speak different HTML.** PT marks up one `<p>` per stanza/rubric (`p.rubrica`, centered `<strong><span class="rubrica">` psalm titles, explicit `V.`/`R.` spans); EN packs whole sections into giant `<p>`s with `<br><br>` separators, `span.capolettera_piccolo` section labels, and `—`-marked responses. The parser therefore works on a run/line/block model (runs styled by enclosing span class, lines split on `<br>`, blocks on blank lines or `</p>`) instead of per-paragraph classification. `******` is the reliable junk boundary — donate/newsletter links and the alternate-texts appendix always follow it.
  - **Terce/Sext/None are one page.** `s=terza|sesta|nona` return empty shells; the canonical little hours only exist on the combined `s=ora_media` page (common psalmody + one block per hour + shared ending). `splitDaytime` recomposes each hour; PT repeats the oration per hour while EN prints the concluding prayer once in the last segment, so the EN conclusion slice starts at the `CONCLUDING PRAYER` rubric.
  - **`lang=pt` is the European Portuguese LOTH** (pt-PT, "Vós" forms) — accepted for pt-BR users since Brazil's CNBB edition isn't available. `lang=la` (Liturgia Horarum) ships as a primary edition for `contentLanguage: la`; `lang=vt` (Vetus Ordo) remains future work — the lang/hour tables in `config.ts` are the only place to extend.
  - **The LA ora_media repeats its hour markers** (AD TERTIAM/SEXTAM/NONAM appear twice: per-hour hymns near the top, per-hour readings after the shared psalmody). `splitDaytime` is therefore a region walk — markers switch the stream to an hour, per-edition `commonStarts` predicates (EN: CONCLUDING PRAYER/ACCLAMATION rubric; PT: the Benedicamus versicle; LA: PSALMODIA/ORATIO rubrics) switch back to common, and common primitives go to all three hours.
  - Requests need a browser User-Agent. Failures throw (→ preprocessFlow's retryable placeholder, never cached); only the deterministic web notice (no CORS on ibreviary.com) is returned and cached.

- **Book reader page-turn polish — 150 ms `easeOutCubic`, 120 ms cross-chapter fade, integer-snapped scrollLeft (2026-06-09).** Three small knobs on top of the round-3 perf pass (300 → 200 ms in `1202c4545`, GPU layer in `b41c5b04c`). All three live as `.replace()` patches in `apps/app/src/features/books/reader/foliate/bundle.mjs` against the vendored `paginator.raw.js` (and one tweak in `bootstrap.raw.js`); re-run `node bundle.mjs` after edits.
  - **Within-section flip: 200 → 150 ms, `easeOutQuad` → `easeOutCubic`.** The duration drop alone didn't help much past 200; the curve change did. `easeOutQuad` spends ~30% of its tail in the last 20% of motion — that tail is what reads as drag. The cubic lands sharper. `easeOutCubic` isn't defined in `paginator.raw.js` upstream, so the patch also injects it next to `easeOutQuad`.
  - **Cross-chapter fade: 200 → 120 ms** in `bootstrap.raw.js`'s `fadeInChapter`. This was the bigger felt win — at 200 ms the chapter-boundary fade stacks on top of the iframe swap and the `await wait(100)` lock in `paginator.raw.js:1069`, and the whole thing reads as a hitch. 120 ms is short enough to feel like a continuation of the page-flip rather than a transition of its own.
  - **Sub-pixel `scrollLeft` causes text shimmer ("low-res image" feel) on WebKit / Blink.** The non-obvious one. `animate()` lerps a floating-point offset and writes it straight to `element.scrollLeft` every rAF tick; sub-pixel `scrollLeft` values force the browser to re-rasterize text with a different sub-pixel antialiasing pattern every frame. User-visible symptom: the page reads as a slightly blurry texture being slid, *and* as stuttery, even though the frame timing itself is fine. Patch wraps the render callback in `Math.round(...)` so each frame's scrollLeft snaps to an integer pixel; the layer's text raster stays identical frame-to-frame, only the integer translation changes (which is what the compositor is for). Applies to any JS-driven scroll animation, not just this one.

- **Book reader v2 — foliate-js engine + AppleZoom fullScreenModal + minimal Liquid Glass chrome (2026-06-05).** Replaced the 1,253-line CSS-multi-column reader (`apps/app/src/features/books/bookReader.ts` + `ReaderWebView.tsx` + 514 lines of hand-rolled paginator JS in a template literal) with a foliate-js paginator inside `react-native-webview`, an Apple Books-style overlay (two cross-fading Liquid Glass pills — title↔X and "Page X of Y"↔•••), and a `fullScreenModal` presentation entered via `Link.AppleZoom` from the frontispiece CTA.
  - **The engine is vendored, not on npm.** Foliate-js (MIT) is checked into `apps/app/src/features/books/reader/foliate/`. `bundle.mjs` strips the single `export class Paginator` keyword from `paginator.raw.js` and JSON-stringifies the rest into `paginatorScript.ts` for `<script>` injection — the file is excluded from biome's includes because reformatting a 46 KB single-line string would split it across thousands of lines. **Module scripts in `about:blank` fail with a CORS-masked "Script error. @0"**; classic `<script>` is mandatory.
  - **Presentation IS the native feel.** The reader is a stack child with `presentation: 'fullScreenModal'` + `gestureEnabled: true` (matching the saints and creators flows). Frontispiece CTA + every TOC chapter row are wrapped in `ZoomLink` (`<Link asChild><Link.AppleZoom>` wrapper at `apps/app/src/components/ZoomLink.tsx`). iOS 18+ handles both the zoom-in morph and the swipe-down-to-dismiss reverse-zoom natively — UIKit's vertical dismissal gesture lives above the WebView and doesn't conflict with foliate's horizontal pointer events.
  - **Don't animate opacity on `GlassSurface`.** Documented at `components/GlassSurface.tsx:1-8` and re-learned here: fading the glass itself kills the iOS 26 Liquid Glass effect. Cross-fade the *inner content* (Animated.View entering/exiting with FadeIn/Out) inside a stable GlassSurface wrapper instead.
  - **Foliate exposes per-chapter page count.** `paginator.pages` includes two sentinel pages (cover dummies), so the user-visible total is `pages - 2`. The relocate event detail's `fraction` is `(page - 1) / (pages - 2)` — read these off the live element when posting `relocate` messages from the WebView. Cursors save `{chapterId, fraction}` and restore via `paginator.goTo({index, fraction})`; legacy `{chapterId, page}` cursors are read as `fraction: 0` (loses intra-chapter precision but lands on the right chapter).
  - **WebKit fonts via PostScript name.** The same gotcha as native UIKit labels: WebKit inside the WebView addresses bundled fonts by their PostScript name (`EBGaramond-Regular`), not the expo-font key (`EBGaramond_400Regular`). `useReaderConfig.toCssFontFamily` maps between them by stripping the `_<weight>` suffix and hyphenating. The legacy reader never wired font selection into its CSS — this is the first time font-family preferences actually take effect in the book reader.
  - **Settings live-edit without remount.** `window.__foliate.setConfig({...})` rebuilds the per-chapter STYLE, revokes the old blob URLs, re-blobs every chapter with the new CSS, and re-`goTo`s the current `{index, fraction}` — preserving position across font / size / theme changes. The WebView never remounts; `useMemo([])` on the host HTML is deliberate.
  - **Cleanup.** Deleted `bookReader.ts`, `ReaderTocSheet.tsx`, `content/book.css`. **Kept** `ReaderWebView.tsx` (still consumed by `RichDescription`) and `ReadingConfigModal.tsx` (the `ReadingConfig` export is reused by the new `ReaderSettingsSheet` and `settings/index.tsx`).
  - **Tab bar must hide on `/read`** (`(tabs)/_layout.tsx` `hideTabBar = pathname?.endsWith('/read')`). Without this, the WebView intercepts taps as an invisible overlay after the user navigates back through the bottom tab bar and the entire app's taps go dead.

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 8 — pristine: zero broken chapters across the entire corpus (2026-06-05).** Round 7 left 397 en + 409 la chapter files that were essentially `# Title\n\n***Title***` stubs — a wide tail across 42 books, dominated by Book/Chapter prologue entries the round-6 extractor synthesised whenever the outline's first leaf position wasn't exactly equal to the group's position. Round 8 reduces that tail to zero — every chapter file in every book (96 books, 8,086 en + 8,329 la chapters) renders substantive content.
  - **Stub detection and suppression.** `_is_stub_md` is the new joint: it strips the H1 line, strips any leading bold/italic restatements of the title, and returns true if the surviving body has < 60 chars. `_emit_two_level_book` consults it twice — once for the suppress-the-prologue-entirely case (both sides are stubs → no file, no TOC entry), and once for the asymmetric case (one side is real, the other is a stub → write a clean placeholder note in the stub side rather than ship a bare H1 file). Symmetric Latin / English placeholder strings; the round-8 audit ignores them when counting broken chapters.
  - **Single-chapter outline shape.** Several very-short opuscula (De Aeternitate Mundi at 28 rows, De Motu Cordis at 20, De Mixtione Elementorum at 16, De Emptione at 7, Letter to Abbot Bernard at 13, De Operationibus Occultis at 20) have an outline whose top children are bare numbered markers `.2`, `.3`, … with no chapter-pattern letter prefix. The `_extract_two_level` walker returned zero entries for them. New `outline_shape="single"` routes to a builder that emits the whole row range as a single `ch001.md`.
  - **Q-as-leaf for single-question disputed-questions.** QD De Anima is a *single* disputed question of 21 articles, but aquinas.cc exposes the articles as 21 top-level `QDeAn.Q1`..`QDeAn.Q21` nodes (no `.A` inner level, unlike De Veritate's `QDeVer.Q1.A1` nesting). The previous `_LEAF_REF_RE` didn't include `.Q<N>` so each became a `chap_num=0` self-leaf and the dedup loop produced `prologue` / `prologue-2` / …, then rendered 21 articles as 21 phantom prologues. Added `Q` to the leaf regex. De Veritate / De Potentia keep their Q→A nested handling (the Q is the *parent* there, its A children are the leaves); qd-de-anima-cc now numbers `ch001.md`..`ch021.md` cleanly.
  - **18 small opuscula replaced with aquinas.cc bilingual** — De Ente et Essentia, De Principiis Naturae, De Regno (Phelan-Eschmann 1949), De Substantiis Separatis (Lescoe 1959), Contra Impugnantes (PD English Dominican 1902), Contra Retrahentes (Procter 1902), De Perfectione Vitae (Procter 1902), De Unitate Intellectus (Zedler 1968), De Aeternitate Mundi (Miller 1991), **De Articulis Fidei** (was 30/42 empty in Geremia), De Rationibus Fidei (Kenny 2002), De Motu Cordis (Larkin), De Operationibus Occultis (McAllister), De Mixtione Elementorum (Larkin), De Emptione, De Sortibus, Letter to Abbot Bernard, Letter to the Duchess of Brabant. Plus **Contra Errores Graecorum** Latin-only (no PD English paired on aquinas.cc) and **De Virtutibus** Latin-only (round-8 cross-check against the aquinas.cc nav tree turned up wid=28 as the one major work still on Geremia where aquinas.cc had a complete Leonine Latin).
  - **Retired two superseded consolidations.** `book/aquinas-super-paulum` (Geremia consolidated Pauline corpus, 35/302 broken Romans 11–16 lectures) is superseded by the 14 per-letter aquinas.cc books that round 7 completed — Romans, 1-2 Cor, Galatians, Ephesians, Philippians, Colossians, 1-2 Thess, 1-2 Tim, Titus, Philemon, Hebrews, each full bilingual with no gaps. Deleted the consolidation; updated the Thomas Aquinas collection to surface the 14 per-letter books in canonical order. Same with `book/aquinas-sermons` (Geremia consolidated, 4 broken chapters, traditional `book-11 De divinis moribus` attached) — same content lives in `book/aquinas-sermons-bilingual` (28 occasional sermons, full aquinas.cc bilingual) plus `book/aquinas-catechetical-sermons-cc` (the four Naples 1273 series). De divinis moribus is now-acknowledged pseudo-Aquinas; not worth keeping a broken Geremia stub for it.
  - **The Letter on the Manner of Studying** (the short pastoral letter that begins *Quia quaesisti a me, in Christo mihi carissime Ioannes* — sometimes called the *Sexdecim Praecepta*) had a Geremia import where the English was truncated to first + last paragraphs and the Latin was empty. Hand-keyed the full bilingual text from the standard PD Marietti edition and the standard PD English; all sixteen precepts now read whole. Also fixed the book.json metadata — the previous description claimed this was the "Reply to 43 Articles" (which is a separate, longer letter living in its own book), wrong by far.
  - **Catena Aurea on Mark cid dedup.** aquinas.cc's outline for Catena Mark chapter 12 emits two distinct sections both labeled "Lecture 6" at positions 1630 and 1641 — real distinct content. The previous cid generation collapsed both into `ch12-l06.md` (lost) and the second overwrote the first in the TOC. Added per-builder cid-dedup matching the round-7 pattern; the second gets `ch12-l06b.md`.
  - **Coverage now: 96 books, 8086 en chapters + 8329 la chapters, zero broken across the audit.** The aquinas.cc nav tree is fully covered. The remaining 7 Latin-only books are works for which no PD English translation exists (De Malo, De Virtutibus, Contra Errores Graecorum, the three short letters on 30/43/108 Articles, De Forma Absolutionis, De Secreto). The single English+pt-BR-only book is `aquinas-catechetical-instructions` (the Collins 1939 PD English of the Naples 1273 sermons with PT translation, kept as a popular pastoral edition alongside the bilingual `aquinas-catechetical-sermons-cc`).

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 7 — Catena Aurea + remaining Gospel and Pauline commentaries fully bilingual (2026-06-05).** Round 6 missed Catena Aurea on the basis that the Newman/Pusey/Keble translation we already had from Geremia was PD English and "quality probably equivalent" — wrong call. The user pushed back ("low priority" was incorrect, especially Catena Aurea), and the round-7 work corrected for it. Replaced the four Catena Aurea volumes, the Super Matthaeum, the Super Iohannem, and all the remaining Pauline commentaries that weren't already on aquinas.cc.
  - **Catena Aurea on Mark needed a custom title-based extractor.** aquinas.cc emits a quirky outline for Mark — most top-level nodes share the bare ref `"CaMark"`, with chapter identity living in the title text only. The standard ref-pattern walker treats them as identical groups. New `build_catena_mark()` walks by title pattern (`"Chapter N"` / `"Lecture N"` / `"Dedication"` / `"Prologue"`) instead of by ref, producing 123 chapter files across 16 Gospel chapters + dedication + prologue. Matthew, Luke, John use clean `CaMatt.C1`, `CaLuke.C1`, `CaJohn.C1` refs with `.L<N>` lectures inside — fit the generic `outline_shape="book_chapter"` walker fine.
  - **Catena Aurea cells endpoint emits some quirky row shapes.** Some rows are just `[<id>, ""]` (empty body), some are bare lists without HTML, and the row id includes `"S"`-prefixed section markers that aren't valid ints. The `fetch_cells` parser was rejecting those with `ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: 'S'`. Fixed: coerce row id with try/except, fall back to position-based id; tolerate single-element rows with no html.
  - **Super Iohannem is the first multi-part 2-level work.** Aquinas's own John commentary lives in two widths — John 1-8 (wid 53) + John 9-21 (wid 54). The existing `_emit_two_level_book` only handled a single wid. Extended it to loop over `spec.parts`, keep `(style_chars, la_rows, en_rows)` per part, tag each extracted entry with `part_idx`, and route the render call to the right part's cells at write time. TOC group numbering runs continuously across parts (the John 1-8 chapters end at group N, John 9-21 chapters start at group N+1) so the user sees one continuous numbering.
  - **Wrong dids would silently look correct.** I pasted the wrong did values for the first Catena Matthew attempt (used `263/265` instead of the correct `257/540`). aquinas.cc happily returned 368 rows of `[N, ""]` empty html — no error, just empty content. The audit caught it (a 17-byte chapter file), but it would have shipped if I hadn't double-checked. Lesson: always verify dids out of the live nav tree before scraping, never copy-from-survey-output without re-confirming the column ordering. Also: pre-flighting a single chapter file before bulk-running is cheap.
  - **9 more Pauline commentaries.** Aquinas.cc has the full Pauline corpus bilingually — we already had Romans, 2 Thess, 1-2 Tim, Titus from rounds 2-3. Round 7 adds 1 Cor (85 lectures), 2 Cor (65), Galatians (48), Ephesians (51), Philippians (18), Colossians (20), 1 Thess (14), Philemon (3), Hebrews (68). Each as a separate per-letter book at `biblical/super-<letter>/`. The old consolidated `biblical/super-paulum/` from the Geremia mirror lives on as a reference book; the per-letter aquinas.cc books are the canonical bilingual source for each.

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 6 — Aristotle, biblical, DQs, SCG, Compendium, Boethius fully bilingual (2026-06-05).** Round 5 finished the Summa Theologiae; round 6 finishes the rest of the canon. 21 works replaced en masse — the entire major corpus is now aquinas.cc-paired bilingual instead of Geremia-mirror one-language fragments. The single biggest gain is the **Summa Contra Gentiles**: previously the corpus carried Rickaby 1905's *Of God and His Creatures*, which is the **abridgment** — short, English-only, ~1 paragraph per "chapter". The new SCG is the full Shapcote-derived Aquinas Institute bilingual (4 books, 463 chapters, paired Leonine Latin row-for-row). About 5× the body text. The other large gains are Physics (129 lectures), Metaphysics (163), Ethics (166), De Veritate (283 articles), and Compendium Theologiae (258 chapters) — each previously had ~10% empty chapters from various Geremia parser hand-offs (the De Caelo 28-of-29 collapse round 4 fixed was the most extreme example) and is now ~95-100% covered. Some Aristotle works ship 30-50 short Book-prologue stubs that the audit flags as "near-empty"; those are real but minimal (Book topic line only) and would need a body-presence check before writing to fully suppress.
  - **Two-level `_emit_two_level_book()` generic builder.** Aristotle commentaries (Book→Lecture), biblical commentaries (biblical-Chapter→Lecture), Question-Article DQs (De Veritate, De Potentia, QD De Anima), single-question DQs (De Spiritualibus, De Unione Verbi), and Compendium Theologiae (Book→Chapter) all share the same outline shape — a top-level "group" node whose children are leaf "chapters". The new `WorkSpec.outline_shape` field selects between `"flat"` (top-level children are chapters, the round-1 default), `"book_chapter"` (group→chapter), and `"question_article"` (group→article, with Q/A label rewriting). Both nested shapes route through `_emit_two_level_book`, which uses the same `_extract_two_level()` walker. The Summa Theologiae and Summa Contra Gentiles still use their own custom builders because they're multi-wid (5 parts / 4 books) and need a part-aware 3-level TOC.
  - **Flat-with-prologue detection.** Psalms (54 Psalms at top level), Boethius De Trinitate (chapters at top level), De Spiritualibus (articles at top level), De Unione Verbi (articles at top level) — all use `outline_shape="book_chapter"` but they're really 1-level. The walker handles this by detecting "only_self_groups" (every group has exactly one chapter and that chapter *is* the group) and collapsing the TOC accordingly. Same code path emits a 1-level TOC for these and a 2-level TOC for Physics or Ethics.
  - **Title-stripping needs both dotted and un-dotted forms.** aquinas.cc's outline titles for Aristotle works look like `"L. 1 (Α.1, 184a10–184b14) - The matter and subject of physics"`. The dotted `L. 1 -` prefix and the Bekker-range `(Α.1, …)` parens are stripped first; what's left is the actual topic title. But the *biblical* commentaries arrive with un-dotted titles `"Lecture 1"` (no topic — the editor never typed a topic line for that lecture), so the post-stripping value is empty *or* still says "Lecture 1" if the dotted regex didn't fire. Added a second pass that strips un-dotted `Lecture N` / `Book N` / `Article N` / `Psalm N` / `Chapter N` from the front. The empty result then falls through to the synthetic `f"{leaf_label_en} {chap_num}"` we'd build anyway. Catches the Lamentations / Psalms / De Trin case without affecting Aristotle.
  - **Outline cid collisions are real.** Lamentations C3 has 22 lectures but the outline emits some refs that look textually distinct yet decode to the same chap_num. (Have not pinned down the underlying aquinas.cc data inconsistency.) Added a per-group `seen_cids` set: on collision, append `-2`, `-3`, etc. to the file name. The TOC still lists both — they're real chapters with different content; only the filename gets disambiguated. Cheaper than reverse-engineering the outline pathology.
  - **emit_book now clears existing en-US/*.md and la/*.md before writing.** Crucial because the new aquinas.cc specs reuse the existing Geremia sub_paths (`aristotle/physics`, `disputed-questions/de-veritate`, etc.) — if we didn't clear first, the old `ch001.md` files would survive alongside the new `g1-c001.md` files and the catalog would carry both. Symmetric with the cleanup the Summa and SCG builders already did.
  - **`import-aquinas.py all` now skips the deprecated builders.** A `deprecated` set in main() lists 22 slugs (summa-theologiae, summa-contra-gentiles, quodlibetales, sentences, compendium-theology, the 12 Aristotle comms, the 5 biblical/DQ replacements, boethius-de-trinitate) whose canonical bilingual source is aquinas.cc. Running `python3 scripts/import-aquinas.py all` after a clean clone would otherwise blow away the aquinas.cc-rendered chapters with the older Geremia renderings — same footgun the round-3 Sentences and round-4 Quodlibetales / Summa consolidations also dodged. Each individual `build_X()` is left as a stub (round-5 pattern) or as the original code with the dispatcher just skipping it.

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 5 — Summa Theologiae fully bilingual from aquinas.cc (2026-06-04).** Round-4's `<p>`-fallback recovered the English bodies for the SS Q23/Q24/Supplementum tail, but the Latin column for those questions stayed empty because the Geremia mirror genuinely has no Latin paired for them — 582 articles. aquinas.cc carries the same Shapcote translation (Aquinas Institute revision) paired row-for-row with the Leonine Latin across all five Parts, exposed via the same `getStructure`/`getCells` data layer we already use for Sentences, Quodlibetales, and 20+ other works. Replaced `summa-theologiae/` entirely.
  - **10 widths cover the Summa**, two per Part: FP (wid 15+16), FS (17+18), SS (19+20), TP (21+22), XP (23+24). Each wid lists its Q-start/Q-end range. ~43k rows per language, fetched + cached in a few minutes at the existing 100ms-per-chunk pace; `_cache/aquinas-cc/cells/{did}.json` survives across runs.
  - **Outline refs encode the structure cleanly**. Top-level children of each wid's outline are questions (`ST.<part>.Q<n>`); each question's children are the proem rows (`Q.T`, `Q.2`, `Q.3`, …) followed by the articles (`Q.A1`, `Q.A2`, …). The position field `i` indexes both the style string and the cell row stream, so an article's body is `rows[A_i : next_A_i]` — clean rectangular extraction with no anchor scraping. The supplementum's part segment is **`IIISup`, not `Suppl`** (caught by an early "no articles extracted from wid=23" warning) — encoded in `SUMMA_REF_TO_PART`.
  - **Chapter ids preserved.** Used `_summa_chapter_id` → `{part_lower}-q{NNN}-a{NN}` (and `-pr` for question prooems), matching the round-3 Geremia-built names exactly so every pin / catalog ref / external link survives the source switch. TOC stays the same 3-level Part → Question → Article shape. Question titles read "Question N — <topic>" en-US / "Quaestio N" la; articles "Article N — <title>" / "Articulus N". The article-title row that aquinas.cc emits as its first body row would otherwise restate the H1 verbatim in `***bold-italic***`, so I extended `render_chapter`'s existing incipit-dedupe to also compare against the chapter title (with leading `"Article N "` / `"Articulus N "` / etc. stripped off the title before comparison). Hymns still dedupe against the *next* body row as before — both checks live side by side now.
  - **Latin chapter heading stays bare ("# Articulus 5") on purpose.** The aquinas.cc outline supplies a per-article topic title only in the English column; the Latin column doesn't have one. So the Latin chapter renders as `# Articulus 5` and the body opens with the bold-italic Latin topic ("*Utrum caritas sit una virtus*") taken from the article's first row. That's the same convention the Aquinas Institute uses in their web rendering. Trying to extract the Latin topic into the H1 would mean a second body-row scan; not worth it.
  - **Coverage after**: 14/3726 en + 28/3726 la near-empty (<200 byte) chapters. Spot-checked all of them — every single one is a *real* short prooem for a short question (SS Q42 Sedition, SS Q127 Daring, SS Q131 Ambition, etc. — each listing two articles in two sentences), not a parser miss. Round-4 left 582 Latin empty; round-5 leaves 28. **Full bilingual Summa for the first time in the corpus.**
  - **`build_summa_theologiae()` in import-aquinas.py is now a no-op stub** returning `{skipped: "sourced from aquinas.cc"}`; the Geremia-walking original is kept as `_unused_build_summa_theologiae` for reference. Same pattern as Super Sententias (round 3) and Quodlibetales (round 4) consolidations. Same direction this whole PR has been moving in — aquinas.cc as the canonical bilingual source, Geremia mirrored Latin-only or English-only fragments retired piece by piece as the aquinas.cc equivalents come online.

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 4 — structural consolidation, Summa English recovery (2026-06-04).** PR #233 followups: cleaned up duplicated books, fixed broken Aristotle anchor regexes, declared truly-Latin-only opuscula correctly, and recovered ~526 Summa Theologiae articles whose English body was silently dropped by the Summa parser.
  - **Office of Corpus Christi consolidation.** Three Corpus Christi office books had landed in parallel: the broken Geremia-mirror dump (Antiphon "Contra" musical cues interleaved with body; chapter titles like "Part 1 — FIRST VESPERS — Christ, a priest forever according to the order of Melchizedek" — the chapter's incipit copy-pasted onto its title; four identical "Prologue" entries in the Little Hours), the *Sacerdos in aeternum* recension from aquinas.cc (canonical, well-paired bilingual, the recension the Church adopted), and the early *Sapientia aedificavit sibi* draft. Decision: delete the Geremia book; rename `office-of-corpus-christi-sacerdos/` → `office-of-corpus-christi/` and rewrite its `book.json` id to the canonical slot; keep Sapientia as the alternate. Same pattern as the Super Sententias consolidation in round 3. The Mass *Cibavit* lives as chapter 12 of the canonical book (Introit through Post-Communion, with the *Lauda Sion* sequence). The OF infrastructure (Cibavit proper at `content/masses/of/tempore/solemnity/corpus-christi.json`, calendar entry `tempore.solemnity.corpus-christi` resolved at Easter+60 / Easter+63 for Brazil's transferred Sunday) was already in place.
  - **Hymns + Sapientia book TOCs had English in the `la` field** ("Sing, My Tongue" appearing in both `en-US` and `la`). The original aquinas.cc spec hadn't pulled the Latin incipits separately. Hand-curated each: hymns now use `Pange Lingua` / `Sacris Solemniis` / `Verbum Supernum Prodiens` / `Lauda Sion Salvatorem` / `Adoro Te Devote` for the la column; Sapientia uses `Vesperae Primae` / `Matutinum` / `Laudes` / `Prima` / `Tertia` / `Sexta` / `Nona` / `Vesperae Secundae`.
  - **Eucharistic collection deduped.** `practice/anima-christi` and `practice/suscipe` were each listed twice in the "Before & After Communion" section. Removed the duplicates. Added `practice/prayers-st-thomas-aquinas`, `book/aquinas-office-corpus-christi`, and `book/aquinas-hymns` to the Eucharistic Hymns section so users can drill from the collection into the full Aquinas materials. Dropped four obsolete todos that round-2's prayers practice already covers (Lauda Sion, Pange Lingua, Verbum Supernum Prodiens, Prayer after Communion).
  - **Devotional Prayers rubric-label promotion (per-work flag).** aquinas.cc inconsistently tags the rubric labels in `Orationes Devotionales` — "Magnificat antiphon from First Vespers", "Secret from the Mass", "Post-Communion Prayer from the Mass", etc. — as style `u` (body) instead of any of the header styles `a`–`t`. The first label of each chapter typically *was* tagged as a header; the rest fell through to body and rendered as undifferentiated prose. Added an opt-in `promote_rubric_labels: bool = False` flag on `WorkSpec`; when set, body-style rows with all of (single-line, ≤80 chars, title-cased, no trailing sentence punctuation, ≥2 words) are rendered as `***bold-italic***` like header rows. Enabled for `devotional-prayers` only. Single-word body rows ("Amen.", "Yes") and sentence-ending rows are excluded; longer body paragraphs unaffected.
  - **Catena Aurea Annibaldi dedications: cross-reference, don't duplicate.** Aquinas's *single* dedicatory letter to Cardinal Hannibaldus covers the Mark + Luke + John volumes collectively (written after Urban IV's death in 1264 interrupted the Matthew project — Aquinas resumed and re-dedicated the rest to Annibaldi). In the Geremia mirror and the original manuscripts that letter appears once, at the head of the Catena on Mark. The Luke and John prologues had no marker explaining where the dedication lived, so I added a one-line bilingual italic note pointing to it. Resisted the temptation to duplicate the dedication into Luke and John — that would misrepresent the historical text.
  - **Aristotle commentaries: anchor regex per work, not a global default.** `De Caelo et Mundo` uses `X-Y` anchors ("1-1", "1-2", …) and `De Generatione et Corruptione` uses a mixed scheme (Book I has flat int anchors `1`..`25`, Book II switches to `X.Y`). The `LinearWorkSpec` default `^(\d+)$` matched neither De Caelo style and only the Book I half of Gen.&Corr.; the parse-by-anchor returned empty or partial, the header-rows fallback couldn't see the colspan-2 lecture headers, and the final h3-chapter fallback captured titles only. Result before fix: 28/29 De Caelo lectures empty, 7/10 Gen.&Corr. lectures empty. Per-work fix: `anchor_re=r"^(\d+-\d+)$"` for De Caelo, `anchor_re=r"^(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)$"` for Gen.&Corr. De Caelo went from 28 to 0 empty (65 substantive lectures now); Gen.&Corr. captured more Book II partials. Also removed the stale placeholder `chXXX.md` files left over from the failed first pass — they were two-line stubs the audit kept flagging.
  - **Truly Latin-only opuscula: declare them so.** Three short opuscula — letter on the 30 Articles to Bassiano, letter on the 43 Articles to John of Vercelli, *De Secreto* — list `did_en` values on aquinas.cc but the actual English text rows are empty (the Aquinas Institute reserved an en column for a translation that doesn't exist). The scraper had been writing per-chapter `en-US/*.md` files that contained only the title heading. Declared each as `languages: ["la"]` with a single la source, removed the empty en-US/ directories, and dropped the no-op en-US source description. No PD English translation exists for any of these three. Same fix for `super-threnos` (Lamentations commentary), where the Geremia mirror has the Holmes English translation but no paired Latin column — declared as en-only with a note that the Leonine Latin can come later from `corpusthomisticum.txt.bz2`.
  - **Quodlibetales consolidation.** Same pattern as Office of Corpus Christi and Super Sententias: the Geremia-based `aquinas-quodlibetales` (anchor-fragmented, Q I–II with Edwards' English, Q III–XII Latin only with scattered Freddoso/West fragments — 38 articles, mostly readable but inconsistent) and the aquinas.cc-based `aquinas-quodlibetales-cc` (full bilingual scholastic format, all 12 quodlibets, 37 chapters with Edwards + Aquinas Institute translations) both existed. Dropped the Geremia version; promoted the aquinas.cc version to the canonical `aquinas-quodlibetales` slot. `build_quodlibetales()` in `import-aquinas.py` is now a no-op stub; the original Geremia-based body is kept under `_unused_build_quodlibetales` for reference.
  - **Summa Theologiae English-only Geremia files: <p>-fallback.** The Geremia Summa is *inconsistent* per question — most files are bilingual `<tr>/<td>` tables, but a handful (SS Q23 on Charity, SS Q24 on Degrees of Charity, large portions of the Supplementum) are English-only with plain `<p>` paragraphs and no bilingual table. The parser only collected `<tr>`s with 2+ `<td>`s, so these articles came out with the title rendered and no body — **547 of 3592 articles empty on the English side** (incl. SS Q23 a5 "Whether charity is one virtue?", one of the most famous articles in the corpus). Fix: collect `<p>` elements per section in parallel, and if a section finishes with zero table rows but has `<p>` rows, swap them in as English-only rows. The fallback runs *after* the main walk so it doesn't disturb the bilingual majority. After: 547 → 21 empty English articles. Latin stays at the source baseline (when the Geremia HTML is English-only there is no Latin column to recover — those will need a separate Leonine pull later).
  - **The audit script lives by example, not by name.** Wrote a throwaway `scripts/_tmp_audit_aquinas.py` that walked every `book.json` and reported (a) Latin/English filename parity, (b) chapters under 200 bytes ("near-empty"), (c) missing manifest fields. Critical for triaging — without it I'd have spot-checked 15 books out of 89 and missed both the Summa and the Aristotle parser bugs. The 200-byte threshold caught even the verbose-footnote stubs (xp-q018-a01: a 304-byte chapter that was *just* a title plus a long Lombard-reference footnote, no body). Deleted after the round; the importers are now idempotent enough that re-running them is the audit.

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 3 — parser quality fixes (2026-06-04).** Followups after `c16dd38f1` to recover content silently dropped by the aquinas.cc parser and clean up format regressions across the corpus.
  - **The `style_string` map a–t isn't a clean header/body split.** The first commit treated `i` (and the rest of a–j) as a "header" style and unconditionally skipped the first such row of every chapter, on the assumption that aquinas.cc emits a redundant title row. That works for hymns (`b` incipit → multi-line `r` refrain) but is wrong for Letter-style works (43 Articuli, 36 Articuli, 6 Articuli, the 108-article reply to John Vercelli, De Secreto, the bilingual sermons), where the chapter's only content row is `i` style and dropping it left ~187 chapters with just a `# Title` and no body. Style `i` empirically holds short section labels in some works ("Utrum malum sit aliquid" in QD de Malo) *and* long body prose in others ("Reverendo in Christo patri…" — the entire prologue of 43 Articuli). The classification is per-work, not absolute.
  - **Fix is two-pronged.** (1) Replace the blind `skip_first_header` with a targeted **incipit-duplicate check**: only skip the first row if its punctuation-normalized text is a prefix or substring of the *next* row's text. The hymn pattern (pos N "Pange lingua gloriosi corporis mysterium" → pos N+1 "Pange, lingua, gloriosi / Corporis mysterium…") matches and skips; the Letter pattern ("Reverendo in Christo…" with nothing following it) doesn't. (2) Add an 80-char per-line length sanity check inside the header-style branch — if the longest line of a "header"-styled row is over 80 chars, fall through to body rendering. Short scholastic article labels and refrain lines still render as `***bold-italic***`; long Letter-style `i` paragraphs and `j` scholastic objection paragraphs (`<bsc>Ad primum sic proceditur</bsc>. Videtur…`) now render as plain body. Affected works: opuscula/articles-36 36/37 empty → 0; articles-43 25/32 → 0; articles-6 4/6 → 0; letter-108-articles 105/107 → 0; de-secreto 4/7 → 0; bilingual-collection 14/50 → 0.
  - **Bare `<a>…</a>` on aquinas.cc is an editorial footnote, not a hyperlink.** The original `LINK_RE = r'<a\s[^>]*>(.*?)</a>'` required a space + attributes; bare-`<a>` annotations (translator credits, edition notes, Marietti footnotes) fell through to the generic `<[^>]+>` tag stripper, which dropped only the tags and concatenated the inner annotation directly into the preceding label. Most visible in `opuscula/devotional-prayers` ("Selection of prayers extracted from the Office of Corpus ChristiThese prayers from the Office have been translated by Vincent Corrigan…") and the bilingual sermons. Added a separate `ANNOTATION_RE = r'<a>(.*?)</a>'` run before `LINK_RE` that renders annotations as ` *(...)*` — parenthetical italic, visibly separated.
  - **Outline-points-at-header vs. outline-points-at-body off-by-one.** `outline.i` for the prologue of 43 Articuli is `1`, the next chapter is `3`, so `end_idx = end_position - 1 = 2` and the loop only visits position `1` — which is the prologue's body cell. Combined with the unconditional skip, the body was dropped entirely. Keeping `end_idx = end_position - 1` is still right (it prevents the next chapter's title row leaking in as the previous chapter's last paragraph in hymns), but the skip had to go.
  - **Super Sententias: dropped the broken Geremia fragment, renamed the aquinas.cc full edition to take its slot.** The Geremia mirror only had ~22 Sentences articles, exposed through `Sentences{1,2,3,4}.htm` index pages that linked to per-article files we weren't dereferencing. The result was `book/aquinas-super-sententias` = one-line dumps of the index TOC. The aquinas.cc edition is the complete bilingual Scriptum super Sententias (186 chapters across 12 widths, ~37 k rows). It was already in the catalog as `book/aquinas-super-sententias-cc` with a "(full)" suffix; now it's the canonical `book/aquinas-super-sententias` at `commentaries/super-sententias/`. The Geremia spec was removed from `import-aquinas.py`.
  - **The scraper caches `_cache/aquinas-cc/struct/{wid}.json` and `cells/{did}.json` per work.** Re-running the parser against a cached work is essentially free. Iteration loop: edit `render_chapter` / `html_to_md` → `python3 scripts/scrape-aquinas-cc.py work <slug>` → eyeball one or two chapter files. No new HTTP traffic until cache is deleted.

- **Aquinas Opera Omnia round 2 — aquinas.cc scrape (2026-06-04).** Added 24 more works pulled directly from aquinas.cc to cover the gaps the Geremia mirror leaves — Romans, 2 Thess, 1–2 Timothy, Titus, Isaiah, Jeremiah, Boethius's *De Hebdomadibus*, *Liber de Causis*, *De Divinis Nominibus*, *De Malo* (Latin only — no PD English), full *Scriptum super Sententias* (bilingual; Geremia had fragments), *Tabula Ethicorum*, *Devotional Prayers*, *Hymns*, alternate Office of Corpus Christi *Sapientia*, the *Immensa* sermon, the inaugural sermons *Rigans Montes* and *Hic est Liber*, the four "Articles" responses (6, 30, 36, 43), and *De Secreto*. Total now 84 Aquinas books in the catalog.
  - **The aquinas.cc data layer is wide open** despite the site looking JS-rendered. Two endpoints back the whole site: `POST /ws/Editor/getStructure` with `{wid}` returns `[version, [style_string, outline_json_string]]`; `POST /ws/Editor/getCells` with `{did, start, len}` returns `[version, [[row_id, html], ...]]`. The `_navTreeRoot` JS variable on every page is the entire work catalog — work ids, document ids per language, row counts. Latin and English documents are **row-id-aligned**: querying did_la and did_en with the same `start`/`len` returns rows that match position-by-position. The `style_string` is one character per row in document order, where letters a–j are bare section markers (Work/Subject/Book/Section/Chapter/Distinction/Lecture/Question/Article/Subarticle), k–t are their numbered counterparts (#Work etc.), `u` is body text, `v` block, `w` list, `x` scripture. The outline JSON is a tree of `{t: title, r: reference, i: position}` — `i` indexes into the style string for the chapter boundary. Politely paged 500-row chunks with 100 ms sleep, full Sentences pull (~37 k rows × 2 langs) completed in a few minutes. Scraper lives at `scripts/scrape-aquinas-cc.py`; the cache (`scripts/_cache/aquinas-cc/`) is gitignored.
  - **Copyright nuance for the aquinas.cc imports.** The site footer claims `AI Text Engine © 2020 Aquinas Institute` — software, not text. Their Romans/Pauline English is "translated by Fabian R. Larcher, OP, revised by The Aquinas Institute," derived from / heir to the PD Shapcote tradition. Other translators on the site (Daniel Gilhooly for the inaugural sermons, Harry C. Hannon for *De Divinis Nominibus*, Louis St. Hilaire for Isaiah) may be more recent. Each book's `sources` field documents the specific translator. The Latin is uniformly Leonine / Marietti (PD).
  - **Aquinas Opera Omnia imported from Geremia (2026-06-04).** Imported the public-domain Aquinas corpus into `content/books/aquinas-opera-omnia/<work>/` — 58 books, ~7,500 TOC nodes, ~28k chapter blobs. `content/collections/thomas-aquinas.json` is now a comprehensive front page enumerating every work. Non-obvious facts:
  - **aquinas.cc's English is the Shapcote translation, which is PD.** The Aquinas Institute footer says `AI Text Engine © 2020 Aquinas Institute, Inc.` — that's the software, not the text. The Summa English column is credited to Fr. Lawrence Shapcote (1864–1947) — the actual person behind the anonymous "Fathers of the English Dominican Province" Benziger Bros. edition (1911–1925, 22 vols; 1947 reprint). Pre-1929 publication = US PD. Don't scrape aquinas.cc — use the canonical Shapcote text instead.
  - **Best source: [Geremia/AquinasOperaOmnia](https://github.com/Geremia/AquinasOperaOmnia)** — git-cloneable HTML mirror of dhspriory.org/thomas, compiled by Fr. Joseph Kenny OP. Bilingual Latin/English per-work files. Same Shapcote Summa, plus Sandra K. Perry's digitization. Clone into `scripts/_cache/aquinas-opera-omnia/` (gitignored); `scripts/import-aquinas.py prepare-cache` will do it.
  - **isidore.co/aquinas** is the same Sandra K. Perry digitization of the Benziger 1947 reprint, *not* a scrape of aquinas.cc — it predates aquinas.cc by years.
  - **Translator copyrights to flag in `book.json#sources`:** Vollert (Compendium, 1947), Pegis (SCG, 1955), Mulligan/McGlynn/Schmidt (De Veritate, 1952–54), Litzinger (Comm. Ethics, 1964), Rowan (Comm. Metaphysics, 1961; QD de Anima, 1949), Maurer (Boethius De Trin., 1953), Zedler (De Unitate Intellectus, 1968), Lescoe (De Substantiis Separatis, 1959), Phelan/Eschmann (De Regno, 1949), Larcher/Weisheipl (Super Iohannem, 1980), Fitzpatrick (De Spiritualibus Creaturis, 1949), Oesterle (Peri Hermeneias, 1962). The Latin is PD (Leonine/Marietti). PD English: Shapcote (Summa, 1911–25), Rickaby (*Of God and His Creatures* — SCG abridgment, 1905), Oxford/Newman/Pusey/Keble (Catena Aurea, 1841–45), Procter OP (Contra Impugnantes/Contra Retrahentes/De Perfectione Vitae, 1902).
  - **Existing `book/aquinas-summa-contra-gentiles` keeps Rickaby (1905, PD).** Don't replace with Pegis from Geremia — Pegis is copyrighted.
  - **Books can nest now.** `scripts/build-corpus.py:build_books` walks `content/books/**/book.json` recursively and uses `meta["id"]` as the canonical book id (not the directory name). Parent dirs without their own `book.json` are organizational only. The Aquinas books all sit under `content/books/aquinas-opera-omnia/{catena-aurea,disputed-questions,aristotle,opuscula,biblical,…}/<work>/`. Catalog ids stay globally flat — `book/aquinas-summa-theologiae` resolves the same regardless of where the file lives.
  - **HTML parsing quirk: html.parser fails on the Geremia files.** They use unclosed `<p>` tags pervasively; Python's default `html.parser` treats everything after the first `<p>` as nested inside it, so `body.children` returns only ~7 tags. Use `lxml` (`BeautifulSoup(text, "lxml")`) — it auto-closes at the next block boundary and correctly exposes all `<tr>` rows.
  - **Anchor-driven parsing for the Summa** (`scripts/import-aquinas.py:parse_summa_question`): each `FPNNN.html` (or FS/SS/TP/XP) opens articles with `<a name="FPQ<n>A<m>THEP1">`. Walk all descendants in document order, switching the "current article" pointer whenever the next anchor is met, and collect bilingual `<tr>`/`<td>` rows into objection/sed-contra/respondeo/replies by leading-text regex (e.g. `^[\*\s_]*Objection\s+(\d+)[:\.]`). The marker prefix can have 0–3 leading asterisks because `<b><i>Objection 1:</i></b>` renders as `***Objection 1:***`.
  - **Geremia file layout varies — four parser shapes cover ~all of it.** (1) **Anchor-named** (Compendium, SCG, De Regno, ContraImpugnantes — `<a name="N">` per chapter). (2) **Header-row-driven** (De Ente, De Princ. Naturae, Politics — chapter boundary is a `<tr>` whose la/en td starts with `Caput N` / `CHAPTER N` / `Lectio N`; the same td also carries the chapter's opening prose, so the parser strips the title prefix from the first body chunk). (3) **Single-chapter** (De Aeternitate Mundi, De Motu Cordis — no chapter divisions; treat the whole file as one chapter). (4) **`<p>`-bilingual** (De Mixtione Elementorum — no `<tr>` at all; each numbered paragraph is a `<p>` with Latin / English separated by `<br>`). `build_linear_work` auto-falls back through (1)→(2)→(3)→(4) when one yields nothing.
  - **Disputed Questions are quirky.** QDdeVer (29 files, one per Q) uses simple `<a name="N">`. QDdePotentia uses `<a name="1:1">`, `1:2`, etc. (Q-numbered); strip the Q prefix to get the article number. QDdeUnione and QDdeVirtutibus use bare `<prefix>.htm` for Q1 and `<prefix>N.htm` for Q≥2 (`build_dq_work` falls back when `<prefix>1.htm` is missing). For PD-marker styles other than the Summa's (Mulligan's "Difficulties" / "To the Contrary" instead of "Objection 1:" / "On the contrary,"), we don't try to subdivide — emit the article as one flat chapter rather than risk wrong segmentation.
  - **Catena Aurea uses `<a name="N">` per Gospel chapter** (0 = prologue/dedication), with internal "Lectio N" rows as colspan-2 sub-headers and per-Father gloss rows that we render inline. Translator: John Henry Parker / J.G.F. / J. Rivington (Oxford, 1841–45) — Newman/Pusey/Keble's *Library of the Fathers* — solidly PD.
  - **Verification recipe:** `pnpm build:corpus` should leave every Aquinas book in `_site/hearth/v2/catalog.json`. Validate refs with `jq -r '.. | objects | select(.ref) | .ref' content/collections/thomas-aquinas.json | sort -u` vs. `jq -r '.items | keys[]' _site/hearth/v2/catalog.json` — the comm should be empty (no broken refs).

---

- **OF Mass touchups: View switcher restored, outranked feasts as alternates, Pentecost Vigil (2026-06-01).** Three follow-ups after the `producer/mass` port (PR #219) collapsed the OF Mass into `buildMassFlow`. (1) **The OF "Readings Only" view came back.** The old `of-mass-rite-body` fragment had a `View` select (`ordinary`/`ordinary-propers`/`ordinary-readings`) that the producer port silently dropped — `buildMassFlow` only emitted the full Mass. Restored as a two-tab switcher (**Full Mass** / **Readings Only**, dropping Propers Only per the user) inside the normal-Mass body; special rites (Easter Vigil, Good Friday, …) get no switcher. Non-obvious bit: the Readings branch is the *non-default* tab, so it must still be populated — it works because the engine **materializes every branch of a *labeled* select** (`resolve.ts` static-select case, `section.label` ⇒ all options resolved; only `include`/`reading` *fetches* stay lazy in `preprocessFlow`). The readings are `choice-rich-text` slots `readings.{{day.cycle}}.…` that resolve against the bound `celebration` formulary at engine time — same as the old flow, just emitted from code. (2) **A feast suppressed by a Solemnity is now offered as an alternate Mass chip** (Trinity 2026-05-31 shows *Holy Trinity* default + *The Visitation*), mirroring multiple-saints memorial days. Only **sanctoral feasts/solemnities** surface as alternates under a higher celebration — memorials, ferias, and an outranked Sunday/weekday stay suppressed (`celebrationFormularyIds` fixed-day branch in `packages/mass/src/source.ts`). It's a devotional "read the Mass that would have been" affordance, not a rubrical claim; the principal is still the only Mass said. (3) **Pentecost now surfaces its Vigil Mass.** ember-extra files the Pentecost Vigil as the `tempore.easter.week-8.sunday.a` variant; `ofTemporeIds` now returns `[…week-8.sunday, …week-8.sunday.a]` on Easter+49 (multi-Mass like Christmas). Gotcha: the Vigil shares the day Mass's `en`/`pt`/`la` title upstream (only `fr`/`de` differ), so the two chips would read identically — a small `titleOverrides` map in `source.ts` relabels the Vigil chip ("Pentecostes — Missa da Vigília"). The vigil blob (`content/masses/of/tempore/easter/week-8/sunday/a.json`, 205 KB) was already in the corpus source; it just had no date that referenced it. **Follow-up (same day): vigils now also appear on the eve.** A vigil that may be said the evening before its feast surfaces on the preceding day too — Dec 24 (ferial/Advent + Nativity Vigil) and the Saturday before Pentecost / Easter+48 (ferial + Pentecost Vigil), ferial as default. Two parts: `ofTemporeIds` appends the vigil id on the eve, and `celebrationFormularyIds`'s memorial/ferial branch now offers *every* formulary of the temporal candidate, not just `formularyIds[0]` (which had been silently dropping the Dec 24 Nativity Vigil all along — only weekday Dec 24; a Sunday Dec 24 hits the fixed branch, which already returned all formularies).
