- **Select tabs switch client-side now — no whole-flow rebuild (2026-05-31).** Tapping a tab in a `select` block used to flash the full-screen "Oremus" threshold: `selectOverrides` was part of `usePracticeContent`'s React-Query key, so any tab change re-resolved the *entire* practice flow, dropped `data` to `undefined`, and `derivePracticeFlowStatus` fell to the loading branch. The engine had deliberately materialized **only the selected** option's body (`resolve.ts` static + `select.ts` from-data, "for responsiveness"), forcing that re-resolve to fetch the other branch. Fix is **lazy + prefetch**, hinging on one insight: engine resolution (`resolveSection`) is synchronous and cheap — it emits a `RenderedSection` tree still holding unresolved `include`/`reading` markers; the **expensive network work is in `preprocessFlow`** (resolving those markers via ContentSources). So: (1) the engine now materializes **every** branch's *structure* in its single walk, each with its own `templateVars`/`flowData[as]` binding — cheap, no network; (2) `preprocessFlow`'s `select` case preprocesses **only the selected branch** into `children` (so the default paints with the flow) and carries the raw `RenderedSection[]` for all options on a new `ContainerOption.rawSections`; (3) `selectOverrides` is **removed from the main query key** (`usePracticeContent` resolves with `{}` = engine's auto/default), so a tab change never touches the main query; (4) the renderer switches client-side — `SelectBlock` holds local `activeId`, and `SelectBranch` either renders the default's `children` or runs a per-branch `useQuery(['select-branch', practiceId, overrideKey, optionId, lang, translation, todayKey], …)` that preprocesses `rawSections`, showing a `Skeleton` only while that one branch resolves. `SelectBlock` `prefetchQuery`s the non-default branches on mount, so a tap is usually instant. The branch runtime (`queryClient`/prefs/date/programDay) reaches `SelectBranch` via a `PreprocessProvider` context wrapped around `PracticeReady`'s body — the one sanctioned exception to "blocks never fetch", localized to the inherently on-demand `select`. **Completion gotcha:** `findTrackIds`/`walkRendered` (extracted to `PracticeFlow/findTrackIds.ts`) walk *all* select options; with every branch now materialized that would advance reading cursors for branches never prayed, so they now descend only the effective `selectOverrides[overrideKey] ?? selectedId`. **Known limitation (acceptable):** because switching is client-side and the main flow resolved with the default pick, sections *outside* a select that template off its choice (`as: view` referenced elsewhere) won't live-update on a tab change — no current content does this; selects are self-contained pickers.

- **Gospel of the Day + Word of the Pope, both from Vatican News (2026-05-31).** The `gospel-of-the-day` practice is now two tabs (Gospel | Word of the Pope), both sourced from the daily Vatican News "Word of the Day" page (`https://www.vaticannews.va/{en/word-of-the-day|pt/palavra-do-dia}/YYYY/MM/DD.html`). Two producers in one folder `apps/app/src/sources/vatican-news/`: `producer/gospel-of-the-day` (citation + passage) and `producer/word-of-the-pope` (the reflection). Tabs are a static `select` → `SelectBlock`, the same segmented control the Mass uses for OF/EF (no new renderer). Page-parsing gotchas: (1) **403 without a browser `User-Agent`** (like the CCC Compendium fetcher) — but these pages are **UTF-8** (no Latin-1 decode). (2) Each block is a `<section class="...section--evidence">` with a per-language `<h2>` (`Gospel of the day`/`Evangelho do Dia`, `The words of the Popes`/`As palavras dos Papas`) and paragraphs in the sibling `section__content`. Match by **h2 text**, not the brittle class — verified on the full live page each heading matches exactly one section. The gospel block's **first `<p>` is the citation**, the rest the passage. (3) PT pages are **HTML-entity-heavy** (`&atilde;`), so parse with `{ decodeEntities: true }`; drop the trailing empty `<p>&nbsp;</p>`. (4) Both tabs hit the **same URL** and can resolve concurrently (the active branch + the background prefetch of the other tab — see the select-tabs entry above) — a module-level in-flight `Map<url,Promise>` in `fetchPage` collapses them into one GET.
  - **Gospel fallback = offline `mass-of`.** Web is CORS-blocked (no proxy yet), so on web/any failure the gospel producer falls back to the corpus-computed `mass-of` Gospel (extracted `loadGospelOfDay`/`pickGospel*` into `apps/app/src/lib/mass-of/gospelOfDay.ts`, shared with Explore's `useGospelOfTheDay`). The producer **never throws** → the Gospel tab is always populated. The Pope producer **does** throw on native failure (no offline equivalent) → degraded to a placeholder by the per-include guard. Explore's "Evangelho do Dia" card (`useGospelOfTheDay`) now prefers the same Vatican gospel on native (mass-of fallback) so the card matches the practice tab — it uses `fetchVaticanGospelText` (plain text) + a `compactCitation` ("João 3,16-18" from the full incipit) since the card title wants the short reference, not the liturgical incipit the tab rubric shows. Note: `include` params *can* bind loaded context via `{{path}}` (whole-string match returns the raw value), but they can't compute a dynamic path, and binding the whole `readings` object would bloat the cache key — so the producer calls the `mass-of` DataSource directly (via `getDataSource`) instead of taking the gospel through params. Also learned: `readings` is keyed by lectionary cycle (`A`/`B`/`C` Sundays, `I`/`II` weekdays, `default` fixed feasts) and `mass-of` does **not** pre-filter to the day's cycle — `readings.default.gospel` only resolves on fixed feasts, so iterate the keys (what `pickGospelText` does); `choice-rich-text` handles this via a `readings.{{day.cycle}}.gospel` template + default fallback.
  - **Two generic gaps fixed:** **(a) date-scoped caching** — `cacheKeyFor` (React-Query key + SQLite `external_content` row) keys on `prefsDeps + params` only, **not `ctx.date`**, so a date-specific producer would serve the first cached day forever. Added opt-in `dateScoped?: boolean` on `ContentSource`; `preprocessFlow` stamps `params.date = local YYYY-MM-DD` when set. **(b) per-include resilience** — `preprocessFlow`'s `Promise.all` meant one throwing `include` failed the whole practice; the `include` branch is now wrapped in try/catch → placeholder text primitive. Also: the flow **validator's `KNOWN_SECTION_TYPES` allowlist was missing `include`** — it had been silently erroring on every existing flow that uses it (compendium, dwdo, divine-office, little-office-bvm); added it (+ a `ref` check).

- **Seasonal app theming deleted; only the home season hero still shifts color (2026-05-30).** The app used to swap to a per-season Tamagui sub-theme (`light_advent`, `dark_lent`, … composed in `tamagui.config.ts` from `liturgicalSubThemes`), so the gold `$accent`, walls, etc. retinted across the year — driven by `useLiturgicalTheme()` feeding `<Theme name>` in `_layout.tsx`. It didn't earn its keep (the whole-app shift "didn't make an impact"), so it was removed wholesale: deleted the 18 `light_*`/`dark_*` theme entries, both `liturgicalSubThemes*` objects, the `useLiturgicalTheme` hook, and the two `<Theme name>` wrappers. The palette is now season-neutral all year (base `light`/`dark`/`illuminated` only). The **one** thing kept: the Fraktur season hero in `features/home/components/LiturgicalHeader.tsx` still tints by season — it reads a focused `seasonalAccent` color map (`config/themes.ts`, 8 seasons + `rose` for Gaudete/Laetare, light/dark) via the new `useSeasonAccentColor(season, date)` hook and applies the color directly to that one `<Text>`. Note the old `martyr` accent was dropped: `useLiturgicalTheme` only ever emitted `season | 'rose'`, never `martyr`, so the `light_martyr` theme was dead. Pattern worth remembering: to make *one element* honor a signal that used to drive a global theme, resolve the color locally from a small map keyed by the signal — don't keep a whole theme alive for it.

- **The flow `liturgicalSeason` context now follows the user's calendar form (2026-05-28).** `getContextValue` in `packages/content-engine/src/engine/context.ts` used to hardcode `getLiturgicalSeason(date, 'ef')`, even though the user's selected calendar (`of`/`ef`) was already on `FlowContext.liturgicalCalendar` (typed `string`, hence ignored). The visible bug: the Angelus practice (`content/practices/angelus/flow.json` — `select on liturgicalSeason`, `easter → regina-caeli`) kept showing the Regina Caeli for ~6 days past Pentecost Sunday for OF users (the default). Reason: EF Paschaltide intentionally runs through the **Pentecost octave** — `Easter + 55` = Saturday after Pentecost (`getEfSeason`, `packages/liturgical/src/season.ts`), per the traditional rubric (Regina Caeli replaces the Angelus until None of that Saturday). OF Eastertide ends at Pentecost Sunday (`Easter + 49`). The `season.ts` boundaries were correct all along; the engine just wasn't reading the form. Fix: type `FlowContext.liturgicalCalendar` as `LiturgicalCalendarForm` and pass it straight to `getLiturgicalSeason` (which already defaults `undefined` → `of`). So both reckonings are honored: EF keeps the octave-long Regina Caeli, OF returns to the Angelus the day after Pentecost.

- **Per-tab notch banners — `PageFlourish` (2026-05-28).** Explore/Library/Search/You now wear a rectangular memento-mori banner in the notch with the page title below, mirroring the Today corner flourish but without its baked-in cutout. New `apps/app/src/components/PageFlourish.tsx`: a full-bleed `expo-image` rendered as a *direct child of `ScreenLayout`* (above the content `YStack`), pulled into the notch with `marginTop={-insets.top}` (cancels `ScreenLayout`'s `paddingTop={insets.top}`) and broken out of the `$lg` side padding with `marginHorizontal="$-lg"` — the same negative-token idiom `PageHeader` uses. Width is left at `'100%'` + `aspectRatio` so it auto-fits the 640 column (no `windowWidth` math needed, unlike the Today flourish which clamps explicitly). One transparent PNG per tab (`assets/textures/notch_{tab}_dark.png`); component is theme-aware (`dark` required, `light` optional, `light ?? dark` fallback) so the light variants drop in as a one-line `light={…}` per screen when generated. Search renders it only in the non-searching branch (`{!isSearching && …}`) so an active query keeps the autocomplete at the top. Status bar/clock are already hidden app-wide, so the banner bleeding fully behind the notch is fine.

- **Explore rebuilt as "The Almanac" — illuminated editorial front page (2026-05-27).** Replaced the collection-icon `DiscoverySections` Explore with `features/explore/` (`AlmanacMasthead` + `ExploreFeed`): a masthead, a peek-snap featured carousel (Saint of the Day → weekday devotion → seasonal → featured reading), the existing `GospelOfTheDay`, then cover-card rows (Library/Voices/Devotions/Traditions). Spec in `docs/features/explore.md`. Non-obvious gotchas hit while building:
  - **`hearthAssetUrl` always points at the *remote* corpus, even in dev** (by design — the dev hearth serves only the catalog + hashed blobs, not the static tree). So any newly-sourced art under `content/art/` or `content/saints/` **will not render until `pnpm build:corpus` + deploy**; there's no local preview of static assets. This is why Explore leans on solid jewel-tone fallback blocks (`bgColor.ts`) and an app-side `artMap` (id → `art/…` path) rather than catalog `image` fields — book catalog hints have no `image` (it lives in the deferred `BookEntry` manifest, and 0/30 covers are populated).
  - **Calendar saint ids ≠ holy-card art ids.** The liturgical calendar uses `st-therese-of-lisieux`; `features/saints/data/saints.ts` uses `therese`. `features/explore/saintArtMap.ts` is the bridge (extend it as more saint art lands); unmapped days fall back to a category/season-tinted block via `bgColor.toneForCelebration`.
  - **`expo-linear-gradient` is NOT a dependency** — don't import it (a corrupted file read suggested otherwise). Use solid `backgroundColor` + an `rgba` scrim panel for legibility instead.
  - **iOS clips glyphs when `lineHeight` ≤ `fontSize`.** A versal initial with `lineHeight === fontSize` cropped top/bottom (a "C" read as a cog). Give large display letters `lineHeight ≈ 1.4× fontSize`.
  - **Reuse, don't rebuild:** the Gospel of the Day already exists end-to-end — `useGospelOfTheDay` (`lib/mass-of/use-gospel-of-the-day.ts`, via the `mass-of` DataSource) + the `GospelOfTheDay` component. The dies-domini weekday devotion cycle is a static map in `pickFeatured.weekdayDevotion` (Mon→Holy Souls, Thu→Eucharist, Fri→Sacred Heart, Sat→Our Lady).

- **Vatican News, integrated natively rather than embedded (2026-05-27).** Explore's "From Rome" first used the official `<vaticannews-widget>` embed (a WebView/iframe), but it always looked like a foreign white Bootstrap card. Replaced with a native render (`FromRome`): the widget is just `widget.js` fetching one server-rendered fragment — **`https://www.vaticannews.va/widget.{lang}.html`** — which is **CORS-open (`access-control-allow-origin: *`)** and cacheable (`max-age=900`), so we fetch the same fragment and parse it with **`htmlparser2`** (`vaticanContent.ts`). The markup carries everything: videos as `data-video-id`/`data-title`/`data-date`/`data-thumbnail` (YouTube → reuse `YouTubePlayer`), `news-featured` + `news-list vaticannews` items (date/title/href), `news-list vatican_va` = acts of the Holy See (audiences, encyclicals, Regina Caeli), and `custom-link-item` outlet links. Rendered as stacked editorial sections; links open the in-app bottom-sheet WebView browser (native) / new tab (web). The embed (`VaticanNews`) stays as an **automatic fallback** when parsing returns empty. Gotchas: (1) the embed widget uses an **open Shadow DOM**, so click interception must walk `e.composedPath()`, not `closest()`; it has no dark-mode attr (its `type="grey"` is the dark variant) and no intrinsic height (it must postMessage `scrollHeight` back). (2) **macOS case-insensitive FS**: a `vaticanNews.ts` data module next to the `VaticanNews.tsx` component collides in TS ("differs only in casing"); renamed the data module to `vaticanContent.ts`. After such a rename the editor tsserver caches a phantom "no exported member" error — `tsc -p` is authoritative (it was clean).

- **Galleries: one primitive, three displays, two markdown directives (2026-05-23).** Practice/chapter `type: "gallery"` JSON used to be exploded into individual `image` primitives by `apps/app/src/content/preprocessFlow.ts`, dropping the `title` field and orphaning the unused `GalleryBlock` carousel component. Spec'd in `docs/features/galleries.md` and rewired: `GalleryPrimitive` now has `display: 'carousel' | 'stack' | 'row'` (default `carousel`), an optional shared `caption`, optional `weights` for `row`, and items with optional `alt | title | attribution | caption`. The preprocessor passes the primitive through; `GalleryBlock` branches on `display`. Row layout measures its container at runtime — if `count * 140px + (count-1)*10px > containerWidth` it switches from CSS-grid to a peek-and-snap FlatList so off-screen bleed becomes the swipe affordance. Books use the same shape via a new marked container-directive extension (`:::gallery`, `:::row`) in `apps/app/src/features/books/markedGalleryExtension.ts`. The extension is the first non-footnote marked plugin in the project — pattern for future ones: define a block-level tokenizer with a `start` hook returning the first regex-match index, a `tokenizer` that consumes the full block (and stashes inner caption tokens via `this.lexer.inlineTokens`), and a `renderer` that uses `this.parser.parseInline(tokens)` for those inner tokens. CSS in the reader WebView reads `[data-display]` and `[data-overflow]` attributes (set by JS at measure-time) to pick layout. Touch/click handlers in `paginationScript` short-circuit page-flip when the target is inside `.ember-gallery`, dispatching a `galleryImageTap` message that the React host hands to `ImageViewer`.

- **PDF book ingestion: `My Catholic Faith` (Bp. Morrow, 1949) end-to-end pipeline (2026-05-18).** First book imported directly from a PDF source rather than transcribed HTML/text. The source is a 423-page two-column pictorial catechism with 193 numbered lessons. Pipeline lives at `scripts/import-my-catholic-faith.py` and is fully re-runnable: it downloads the PDF to `.cache/`, wipes `content/books/morrow-my-catholic-faith/{en-US,images}/`, and rebuilds everything from page bounds detected in the PDF itself. Validation: `scripts/validate-my-catholic-faith.py`.

  **Layout-aware extraction beats `pdftotext`.** The PDF has 11pt body text, 9pt commentary expansions, 11pt-bold questions, italic image captions, and a 12pt+ bold lesson heading per chapter. Plain `pdftotext` flattens the 2-column layout in physical order (left-then-right within each line band → reads wrong). The pipeline uses pymupdf's `get_text("dict")`, splits each line into runs of consecutive spans sharing `(size, bold, italic)`, classifies via font reference (`Type3 (428 0 R)` = bold, `Type3 (598 0 R)` = italic, `Type3 (504 0 R)` = regular — flags=0 on every span, useless), then sorts left/right columns by `(int(y0 // 7), x0)`. **Floor division, not round, is critical:** `round(y0/5)` for adjacent fragments at y=522.515 vs y=522.488 put them in *different* buckets (105 vs 104) because they straddle the .5 rounding boundary — that's what was scrambling bold-question and regular-answer fragments across many lessons. Column split is at x=247 (left col tails end ~245, right col starts cluster at 250+; gutter is sparse at 246-249), not 250 — that fix unscrambled the 2-up Q&A layout in lessons 80/82/130.

  **Whitespace artifacts are baked into the PDF text stream**, not extraction-method artifacts — `pdftotext`, pymupdf's `dict` mode, and `words` mode all emit `ofthorns`, `ifwe`, `endofthe`, `BlessedTrinity`. Fix: `split_glued(token)` with three paths gated on confidence. (1) CamelCase boundary `[a-z][A-Z]` + nested wordninja per piece; (2) ALL-CAPS path that lowercases, runs wordninja, re-uppercases, handles trailing `'S`; (3) general wordninja with at-least-one-glue-word requirement; (4) binary fallback for tokens with zipf≈0 (recovers `ofsin` that wordninja over-splits to `of s in`). Each path validates pieces via `wordfreq.zipf_frequency` (≥3.0, or 2.5/2.7 in the camel+glue and all-caps paths). Tested against 25 cases including proper nouns (`LaRavoire`, `Krishnagar`, `DeVico`) and rare ecclesiastical terms (`Indefectibility`, `Sacramentals`, `Manichaean`) — none mis-split. Closed-class glue whitelist + a small `CATHOLIC_VOCAB` (saints, places, biblical verbs) covers the residual long-tail.

  **Section dividers are not chapters.** The printed TOC italicizes ~10 sub-section labels like "APOSTOLICITY OF CATHOLIC DOCTRINES" between numbered lessons — visually they look like headings (12pt bold), but they aren't separate chapters. Two consequences: (1) lessons including their following divider can appear as 3-page rather than 2-page spans, which is correct — the divider stays with the previous chapter per user direction; (2) when a divider appears at the top of a lesson's first page (e.g. "Eminent Catholics" on lesson 65's page), `find_heading` must search past it for the actual `N. TITLE` line. Solved by tagging non-numbered 12pt-bold lines as `section` (rendered as `## H2`) instead of `heading` (`# H1`), and emitting the H1 first in markdown regardless of physical order.

  **Lesson 61's heading mixes 12pt and 13pt** in the same line ("**61.**" is 13pt, "THE POPE, BISHOPS AND COUNCILS" is 12pt). Since the fragment splitter keys on size, the two halves landed in different fragments; the heading detector saw only "61." Fix: when scanning for the heading line, merge adjacent same-line bold≥12pt fragments before pattern-matching.

  **Running-header band y-coordinate is razor-thin.** Page numbers + "MY CATHOLIC FAITH" / running title sit at y≈18; lesson 66's heading sits at y≈24. Cutoff of y<30 dropped lesson 66's heading entirely. Final rule: only drop y<30 if the line's font is *not* 12pt+ bold — a lesson heading near the top of the page is real content, not a header.

  **193/193 chapters, 189/193 header images.** Four lessons (14, 51 originally — 51 recovered, 58, 65) and one appendix have illustrations rendered as Type3 vector glyphs which `pdfimages` can't extract. Fallback: rasterize the page region above the heading via `page.get_pixmap(clip=Rect(0, 30, width, heading_y-4))` at 2× zoom and convert via ImageMagick → WebP. Recovered 3 of the 7 originally missing; the remaining 5 are genuinely imageless (the heading sits at the top of the page with no room above for an illustration).

  **Auto-generated TOC mirrors source.** `book.json`'s TOC titles are regenerated on each import run from the extracted lesson headings, so the manifest can't drift from chapter content (Judgement/Judgment, Neighbour/Neighbor, Colors/Colours — the PDF uses British spelling intermittently; trying to hand-type a "canonical" TOC produces 21 mismatches against the actual H1s).

  **Lesson 193 + appendix split.** The PDF runs the final lesson + Church Year + Most Important Prayers + Index as one continuous text from page 406 to end. `APPENDIX_OVERRIDE` clamps lesson 193 to pp 406-407; `APPENDICES` carves Church Year (408-411) and Most Important Prayers (412-415) into their own chapter files; the index (p416+) is dropped — it's a printed-book navigation aid that doesn't translate.

  **Sub-agent audit loop.** After the initial import, two rounds of sonnet sub-agents (7 + 4 parallel) reviewed chapter batches and surfaced fixable artifact classes beyond the initial ones: glued-on-2-char artifacts (`ofa`, `Iam`), pronoun-glued verbs (`Himselfgave`/`Himselftaught`), space-split words (`Via ti cum`, `dir i ment`, `A men` → `Amen`), bold-question mid-sentence with `?` typeset in regular weight, line-break hyphens not re-joined (`im- pediments`), numeric glue (`of350`), and the `Chruch` source typo. Each got a targeted fix; the regex-driven `SPLIT_FIXES` table feeds into a `PROTECTED_REJOINED` set that prevents `split_glued` from immediately undoing the rejoin (e.g. wordninja sees `Amen` and wants to re-split to `A men`; the guard short-circuits). The `clean_text` second pass runs on the rendered markdown (skipping image-ref lines) to catch typos that only manifest after title-casing converts the all-caps source heading.

- **Content Producers — spec'd as a small extension point for runtime-computed content (2026-05-17).** Three real pressures forced the design: (1) the Mass flow is split-brain — `packages/mass-of/src/source.ts` owns precedence/Gloria/preface logic in code while `content/practices/mass/flow.json` + 37 fragments mirror those decisions in JSON, so a single liturgical-rule change touches both sides; (2) several books we want (CCC on vatican.va, iBreviary, Lírio Católico, Escriva works) can't be bundled in Hearth for copyright reasons and need runtime fetching with sub-document addressability (cite `book/ccc#507` from a prayer card and either open the reader there or render the paragraph inline); (3) daily snippets like today's gospel, word of the pope, today's office reading want to compose into existing static practices at block level, not replace whole practices. See `docs/features/producers.md` for the design.

  **Three things load-bearing in the design, three explicitly deferred.**

  *Locked in:* (a) **Producer** = built-in code function in its own package, registered by id, returning either `FlowBlock[]` (flow consumers) or cleaned HTML + anchor sidecar (reader consumers) — no forced unification. Catalog entry is just `{ id, kind }`; attribution and cache policy live in producer code, not declarative fields. (b) **`include` primitive** in the flow DSL (`{ type: "include", ref: "producer/...", params: {...} }`) usable at any depth — same primitive handles whole-flow producers (Mass: one `include` at the root) and composable snippets (gospel-of-the-day inside a morning routine) and anchored book excerpts (`book/ccc#507-509`). (c) **Anchored refs** (`kind/id#anchor[-anchor]`) extend the ref grammar; resolver returns `(chapter, anchorRange)`; reader uses native `scrollIntoView` and HTML subtree extraction. No new rendering pipeline — tappable refs work via `<a data-ref="...">` caught by a WebView click handler.

  *Deferred:* (a) **Declarative-recipe runtime** — premature; extract only after 3-4 built-in producers converge on the same shape. (b) **Reader IR / unified Block type** — today's WebView+HTML is sufficient; IR pays off only when we want multiple render targets or structured highlights. Doing it later is cheap because IR would be internal. (c) **Web parity for external fetching** — CORS proxy vs. native-first decision deferred until a real user need bites.

  **What the user vetoed during planning (worth remembering for future similar designs):** I initially proposed an IR refactor as a prerequisite, a unified `Block` type across flow and reader, declarative adapter recipes from day one (`html-extract` / `json-api` / `widget-embed` kinds), a `producer` field on `PracticeManifest` as a whole-flow swap, and producer manifests carrying `{ attribution, license, lawfulUse, cachePolicy }`. Each was overengineering. The user pushed back: producers compose at block level not practice level (otherwise you can't drop today's gospel into a morning routine), built-in code first (declarative recipes only when shared patterns prove themselves), HTML is sufficient (IR is internal and can be added later cheaply), and config fields belong inside producer code (built-in producers don't need configuring). The final spec is much smaller — one doc, one extension point, two small additions to existing data shapes (`source`/`anchors` on `BookEntry`, `include` in the flow DSL).

  **External cache stays on-device.** The legal load-bearing decision: Hearth never holds third-party bytes. Cache lives in a new `external_content` SQLite table on each device, with the existing 200MB LRU at `apps/app/src/content/store.ts:204-231`. Even transient server-side caching arguably crosses from "Safari Reader" into "republishing" for the copyrighted sources, so we don't.

- **Merged `prayer` and `practice` into a single `practice` kind (2026-05-15).** Two reasons: scheduling a Pater Noster at 3pm was impossible (only practices were schedulable), and the data shape was already 95% the same — a prayer's `body: FlowSection[]` is functionally just a flow's `sections`. Migration moved ~99 `content/prayers/*.json` into `content/practices/<id>/manifest.json` with inline `flow: { sections: <old body> }` — most short prayers now skip `flow.json` entirely.

  **The resolver alias is the key trick.** All 1,099 existing `{ type: 'prayer', ref: 'our-father' }` refs in practice flows keep working unchanged because `fetchPrayerSync(id)` now passes `'practice'` as the hint to `canonicalize()`, which finds `practice/our-father` and returns its `flow.sections` as the prayer body. The `prayer` section type in the flow DSL is preserved (it's a rendering hint — collapsible block with title); only the catalog kind disappeared. `resolveCanticle` works the same way; the 3 canticles (Magnificat / Benedictus / Nunc Dimittis) keep their `subtitle` / `source` as optional `PracticeManifest` fields.

  **Five name collisions** existed where the same id had both a `prayer/` and a `practice/` entry (act-of-reparation, anima-christi, memorare, regina-caeli, suscipe). The practice was a ritual wrapper (sign-of-cross → prayer text → sign-of-cross) and the prayer JSON held the canonical body. Resolution: substitute the prayer's body inline where the practice's flow had a self-ref or held a duplicated text block, then convert the practice's `"flow": "flow.json"` legacy string to inline `flow`. See `scripts/migrate-prayers-to-practices.py`.

  **Collections** got a one-shot rewrite: 128 `ref: "prayer/<id>"` → `ref: "practice/<id>"` across 11 JSONs. Refs *inside* practice flows stayed bare and Just Work via `canonicalize`.

  **UI fully erased the distinction:** `PrayerModal`, `EssentialPrayersRow`, `AllPrayersList` deleted. Browse, search, and collection ItemCard collapse to one render path — every item opens a practice page. The hardcoded "Essential Prayers" curation is now a separate concern (can be rebuilt as a featured collection if we miss it).

  **Note:** `tsc -p apps/app` from the worktree reports `Cannot find module '@ember/content-engine'` / `@ember/liturgical` errors because the workspace's TypeScript path resolution requires the packages built. Pre-existing, not the merge.

- **Creator metadata pre-computed by the deploy workflow, not the client (2026-05-14).** The Catholic Creators feature had two expensive YouTube discoveries running per device, per refresh: scraping the channel page's `og:image` to get a creator avatar (Atom feed has no channel image), and HEAD-probing `/shorts/<videoId>` for items in the channel feed that weren't in the UUSH playlist (UUSH only surfaces the last ~15 shorts, so older shorts in the channel feed get misclassified as long-form videos until you probe). For 20 creators × ~15 ambiguous items each that's ~300 HEAD requests per device per refresh. Also: YouTube has started rate-limiting `/feeds/videos.xml?playlist_id=…` calls aggressively per-IP — a single dev workstation can easily land in a 404 hole after a few minutes of testing.

  Solution: do the discovery once, in CI, on a fresh runner IP, write a static JSON file per creator into the Hearth corpus, and have the client just consume it.

  **Pipeline:** `scripts/build-creator-meta.py` runs after `build-corpus.py` in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`. Walks `content/creators/*/manifest.json`; for each YouTube channel scrapes the og:image and discovers shorts (UUSH + HEAD-probe of ambiguous channel-feed items, parallelized via `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor`); for each podcast/RSS feed extracts the channel-level `<itunes:image>`/`<image>`/`<icon>`. Writes `_site/hearth/v2/creator-meta/<slug>.json` with `{creatorId, generatedAt, channelImage, shortVideoIds[]}`. Stdlib only — no extra Python deps in CI.

  **Schedule:** the workflow now also fires on `cron: '15 * * * *'` (hourly at :15). The Pages deploy is fast since the existing corpus is mostly cached; only the creator-meta files change between runs. Shorts classifications lag at most an hour for new uploads — acceptable since a video never *changes* kind once published.

  **Client:** `apps/app/src/features/creators/feeds/fetcher.ts` tries `hearthUrl('creator-meta/<slug>.json')` at the start of each refresh. When present, the YouTube branch skips the og:image scrape, the UUSH fetch, AND the per-video HEAD probe — uses the prebuilt `channelImage` + `shortVideoIds` directly, only fetching the channel_id Atom feed for the current item list. When absent (e.g. a creator added between CI runs), the live discovery path runs as a fallback. The `defaultPrebuiltLoader` dynamic-imports `@/lib/hearth` — that module transitively pulls in `expo-sqlite` via the cache repo, which vitest/jsdom can't parse; the dynamic import means the fetcher tests don't touch it as long as they inject their own loader (typically `async () => null`).

  **Why this instead of a Cloudflare Worker / Edge proxy** (the journal-noted TODO at `apps/app/src/features/creators/feeds/fetcher.ts`'s `feedUrl()` for CORS): operational simplicity. The Pages deploy is already running on each content change; layering one more Python step that costs ~10s in CI and emits ~20 small JSON files is strictly free. A Worker remains the right answer if/when we need real-time freshness or a JSON-normalized feed API; for now, a CI-built static pack covers the actual pain (avatar discovery + shorts classification) without standing up new infrastructure.

- **RNTL + Vitest integration harness — agent-runnable e2e without a simulator (2026-05-13).** Maestro covers iOS but needs a sim — too slow and too heavy for AI agents to iterate against. Vitest unit tests only cover pure logic. New middle layer: `*.test.tsx` files driven by `@testing-library/react` rendering through `react-native-web` in jsdom, against **real SQLite** (`better-sqlite3` `:memory:` behind a thin `expo-sqlite`-shaped adapter at `apps/app/src/test/sqlite-better.ts`) and **real Hearth content** (a `fetch` interceptor at `apps/app/src/test/hearth-local.ts` that serves blobs from `_site/hearth/v2/` on disk — produced once by `pnpm build:corpus`). Same content the live site serves; nothing mocked at the data layer.

  **Harness:** `apps/app/src/test/renderApp.tsx`. Mirrors the production provider tree (QueryClient → TamaguiProvider → Theme) but skips the heavy boot sequence — runs `resetAllTestDbs → openDatabaseAsync → migrations → createEventsTable → replayAll → store.hydrate → registerDataSources → loadCatalogFromHearth → warmCriticalManifests → warmDeferredManifests → resetForTests` imperatively. Routes are opt-in: each test registers only the screens it exercises (`{ pattern: '/pray/[practiceId]', loader: () => import('@/app/pray/[practiceId]') }`). Reuses the same `resetForTests()` Maestro hits via `/dev/reset`.

  **Worked example:** `apps/app/src/features/practices/components/PracticeFlow.test.tsx` mirrors the Maestro `practice-with-choices.yaml` flow — opens grace-meals, asserts both `select-option-*` testIDs render, clicks Before, asserts the prayer title shows, clicks After, asserts the alternate prayer title shows. End-to-end runtime: ~3s including catalog warm. If this test passes, the harness can support any practice/pray flow.

  **Mocks live in `apps/app/src/test/setup.ts`.** What's stubbed (and why): `react-native-reanimated` (the official `mock.js` does `require('./src/mock')` which only resolves under Metro/Jest — hand-rolled stub instead); `react-native-gesture-handler` (its `jestSetup.js` pokes globals jsdom rejects); `react-native-svg` and `react-native-safe-area-context` (ship CJS with TS syntax); `lucide-react-native` (3000+ icon files = 30s import — listed each icon used in app code); `fake-indexeddb/auto` (RN content-cache calls `indexedDB.open` on web; jsdom doesn't ship it). Also: `window.matchMedia` / `ResizeObserver` / `scrollTo` polyfills for Tamagui Select. SQL files (`db/migrations/*.sql`) load via a one-shot rolldown plugin in `vitest.config.ts` that wraps file contents as a default-exported string — Metro lets you `import sql from './x.sql'` directly; vite/rolldown don't.

  **Router fake** (`apps/app/src/test/router-fake.tsx`): tiny Zustand-backed registry mapping `[paramName]` route patterns → screen modules. `useRouter`, `useLocalSearchParams`, `Stack`, `Redirect` mirror the surface app code actually uses (only 4 exports across the whole codebase). One gotcha: Expo Router lets `practice/grace-meals` (containing `/`) match a single `[practiceId]` segment — the final dynamic segment must match `(.+)` not `([^/]+)`. Without that fix the route silently doesn't resolve and the test sees an empty `practiceId`.

  **What stays Maestro's job (the ceiling):** Reanimated worklets, gesture-driven UI (swipes, scrubbing), native bridges (haptics/notifications/native image decoding), real WebViews (book reader), responsive breakpoints keyed off `Dimensions.get('window')` (jsdom is 1024×768), font-dependent layout (custom fonts don't load), real Stack back-stack semantics. Assert on accessibility labels and DOM presence, not measured pixels.

  **Speed:** ~2-3s per integration test file (boot + catalog warm dominates; subsequent renders are sub-ms). Full app suite (172 unit + 1 smoke + 1 integration) runs in ~9s. Drop-in for `pnpm test`; no split. Setup: `pnpm install` (better-sqlite3 needs a native compile — `cd node_modules/better-sqlite3 && npm run install` if pnpm skipped the install hook), then `pnpm build:corpus` once before the first integration run.

- **Spiritual threads — daily-only resolution loop shipped (2026-05-13).** Phases 1–3 of `docs/features/spiritual-threads/` plus a daily-only Plan-of-Life Resolutions panel and the petition→thanksgiving bridge from 6a. Phases 4 (higher-level pieces), 5 (periodic reviews), and the `'liturgical-event'` schedule rule are **deferred** — non-daily windows had real defects (post-Pentecost OT collapse from `addDays(start, 100)`, missing logical-day cutoff) and we chose to defer over partial fix. PRD `04-plan-of-life.md` / `05-periodic-reviews.md` are marked deferred and remain the design-of-record.

  **Architecture decisions worth remembering:**

  - **One Movement projection, two event streams.** Petitions and thanksgivings share `Map<id, Movement>` discriminated by `kind`, but their events are distinct (`IntentionRaised`/`Answered`/`Expired`/`Retired` vs `ThanksgivingOffered`/`Updated`/`Retired`). Hooks expose kind-bound wrappers (`useActiveIntentions`, `useActiveThanksgivings`); selectors share a `matching()` generator.
  - **Cadence-aware closure.** `state: 'active' | 'closed'` is the boolean every query needs; `closure_kind: 'answered' | 'expired' | 'retired'` is the analytic detail. Action-menu verbs gate on `cadence` (`perpetual` → only "Retire"; `goal` → "Mark answered"; `bounded` → both + auto-expiry).
  - **Bounded petition expiry uses sweep + lazy fallback.** `useExpirySweep` fires on app foreground + 1h interval and emits `IntentionExpired`; read hooks defensively skip overdue active items so a missed sweep doesn't show stale state. Pure logic in `findExpired.ts` so vitest doesn't have to load `react-native`.
  - **Engine stays pure.** `EngineContext.resolutions` / `windowFor` / `supportsMovements` are read-only injections. The four new DSL primitives (`offering`, `capture-movement`, `capture-resolution`, `review-resolution`) resolve to rendered shapes; **writes happen in the player**, not the engine. `withSpiritualThreads(ec)` is the host wiring that snapshots Zustand store state at resolve time and closes over the host's "today" for `windowFor`. Lives in `apps/app/src/content/engineContext.ts`.
  - **Engine doesn't know about clocks.** `ec.windowFor(level, forward)` takes only level + direction; the host closes over its anchor. There is no `ec.logicalDay()` — the engine asks "what window?", the host decides what "today" means.
  - **Offering is in-flow only.** No separate `OfferingPickerSheet`; the `offering` block IS the picker. Renders the active intentions (or a quick empty-state CTA) inline at the liturgical moment the practice asks for them.
  - **Flow resolution is a snapshot.** When a user captures a daily resolution mid-Examen, the *running* flow doesn't reshuffle — `withSpiritualThreads` reads state once at resolve time. Backing up to a prior block shows the same data the user saw on first pass.
  - **Capture-resolution pre-fills from the projection.** `ec.resolutions.inWindow(level, starts_at)` looks up an existing resolution whose window matches the block's target window. If found, the block opens in display mode with a Change affordance instead of an empty form; tapping Change reopens the form pre-filled and `Save` calls `reviseResolution` (preserves id, emits `ResolutionRevised`). Stops users from accidentally creating duplicate resolutions across multiple Examen runs in the same day.
  - **No "Skip" controls.** Both `capture-movement` and `capture-resolution` are *optional by default*; the way to skip is to not submit. An explicit Skip button would be irreversible (once tapped, the form is gone), which is hostile when the user just wants to scroll past. The `optional` prop was removed entirely from the DSL.
  - **Explicit-open everywhere.** Capture forms (offering, capture-movement) open only on a "+ Add" tap. Auto-opening a textarea the user didn't ask for is the same anti-pattern; one consistent fix.
  - **One "today" everywhere, with a 4am cutoff (configurable).** `useToday()` / `getToday()` apply `logicalDay(new Date(), 4)` in live mode — a new day doesn't start until 4am. Examen done at Wed 1am still reviews R(Tue) and writes R(Wed); the day carousel still shows Tuesday; completion stamps still go to Tuesday. The resolutions / day carousel / completions / wall all agree on what day it is. Time-travel honors the user-picked date as-is (no cutoff applied — picking May 12 means May 12, not "depends on the hour"). The journal previously documented an asymmetry where only resolutions honored the cutoff — that was rejected in favor of consistency.
  - **Resolution lifecycle: window stored, state derived.** A `Resolution` has `starts_at` / `ends_at`; "active" / "closed" are computed from `now` vs window. Reviews are separate (`ResolutionCheckin` accumulates N times; `ResolutionReviewed` is the canonical close). `pending-<level>` = most-recent unreviewed; `active-<level>` = the one whose window contains now.
  - **Two checkin events vs one with a mode field.** `ResolutionCheckin` and `ResolutionReviewed` are separate events because their cardinality differs (N vs 1) and only `ResolutionReviewed` marks a resolution as canonically closed. A resolution with three Checkins but no Reviewed is still pending.
  - **`ResolutionLevel` is `'daily'`.** The type union is intentionally a singleton to keep the call sites typed for when non-daily resumes. `REVIEW_TARGETS` in `engine/resolve.ts` only registers `'active-daily'` and `'pending-daily'`.
  - **`group` DSL block for conditional sections.** `{ type: 'group', skipIfEmpty: true, sections: [...] }` — collapses entirely when its children resolve to only structural primitives (subheading / divider / heading). Verificatio in the Examen wraps subheading + review-resolution + divider this way: on first run with no prior resolution, the whole section disappears cleanly instead of leaving an orphan header. Useful anywhere a chrome-only section needs to gate on a single conditional child.
  - **Greenfield deletion.** Old `features/intentions/`, `features/gratias/`, the hardcoded `app/examen.tsx`, their repos, and 6 old event types are *gone*. No migration code, no backward-compat shims, no `_v1` event tags. Per `CLAUDE.md`: solo project, lightweight changes; the new `applyEvent` switch only handles new types and any old events left in a local DB are inert.
  - **Cross-cutting cleanups.** `<BottomSheet>` absorbs ~500 lines of duplicated `<Modal>` scaffolding. `<PrayerTextInput>` consolidates the multiline-text-with-EBGaramond styling that was duplicated across five capture surfaces (sm/md/lg + optional `surface` token). New `$colorDestructive` theme token replaces hardcoded `#B4322A`. Use these for any new sheet / capture surface.

  **Watchpoints when non-daily resumes:**
  - Forward-walk season detection (don't `addDays(start, 100)` — post-Pentecost OT is ~180 days).
  - Apply `logicalDay` cutoff uniformly to weekly/monthly/seasonal/annual windows, not just daily.
  - Add seasonal `windowFor` test coverage from the start.

- **`mass-of` typed corpus accessors — `fetchAsset` legacy router gone (2026-05-11).** `mass-of` used to build path strings (`of/masses/tempore/...json`, `of/library/preface/...json`, `of/calendar/...json`) and hand them to `EngineContext.fetchAsset` / `SourceContext.fetchAsset`; the app implemented that as `fetchOfAsset.ts`, a path→corpus-id router. Both sides already understood corpus ids — the path-string contract was pointless indirection left over from the v1→v2 migration. Replaced it with a typed `MassOfDataSource` interface (`fetchMassProper(id)`, `fetchOrdinary(id)`, `fetchPreface(id)`, `fetchOfData(id)`) exported from `@ember/mass-of` alongside a new `createMassOfSource(data: MassOfDataSource): DataSource` factory. The source closes over its data accessor at registration time; `register.ts` builds the host impl with `createCorpusMassOfDataSource(getLangs)` (`apps/app/src/lib/mass-of/dataSource.ts`) which calls `loadMassProper(id, langs)` / `ensureManifestBody` / `normalizeLangKeys` directly. Removed: `apps/app/src/content/fetchOfAsset.ts`, `formularyPath` export from `@ember/mass-of`, `fetchAsset` from both `EngineContext` and `SourceContext`, `EngineContext.fetchAsset` from `engineContext.ts`, the path-routing in `use-gospel-of-the-day.ts`. `mergeLangs.ts` + `langAliases.ts` stay — they have other callers in `resolver.ts`. **Why a getter (`() => string[]`) for langs**: `registerDataSources()` runs once at boot before the user has settled on a content language; the accessor reads the current preferences-store snapshot on each fetch so runtime language changes flow through without re-registering. Tests: 6 obsolete `formularyPath` test cases dropped; `source.test.ts` / `integration.test.ts` updated to populate the typed accessor (integration test now maps corpus ids back to ember-extra filesystem paths to keep using the vendored fixtures). All 60 mass-of tests + 142 content-engine tests + 171 app tests green.

- **Catholic Creators — Phases 1-3 substrate + UI + offline (2026-05-10).** First three phases of the Creators feature landed end-to-end: catalog plumbing, Browse UI, and Home/Follows/Offline. v1 ships after Phases 4-5; this PR is the foundation everything else stacks on.

  **What works now.** Browse → Creators surfaces seed creators (Padre Paulo Ricardo pt-BR, Pints With Aquinas en-US). Tapping a profile lazy-fetches RSS/Atom + YouTube Atom feeds (no API key, no quota), parses chapter markers from podcast `<description>` timestamp lists, and renders Listen / Watch / Read tabs based on which channels the creator has. Audio plays in-app (`expo-audio`), the persistent Now-Playing mini-bar follows users across screens, YouTube videos play via the official IFrame API (web `<iframe>` + native WebView — no audio extraction, no proxying, ToS-compliant). Articles render in summary-by-default with a per-channel `fullText: true` allowlist for known-good feeds. Following a creator surfaces their newest items in a Home `Latest` row; pinning an episode downloads media + image to the content-addressed blob cache and protects them from LRU eviction. Wi-Fi-only is a hard gate (default ON) — pin requests on cellular enqueue to `pending_pins` and drain on Wi-Fi reconnect. Offline chip floats top-right when disconnected.

  **Key decisions.**
  - **Live items live in SQLite, not the corpus.** Episodes/videos/articles are too volatile for hash-addressed storage — a daily podcast would force a corpus rebuild every day. `feed_items.item_id = sha256(creator_id + ':' + guid)` is deterministic so pinned blobs survive reinstalls when the documents directory is restored from device backup.
  - **Audio backend is injected into the store, not imported by it.** `creatorsStore.ts` holds transport state; `audioPlayer.ts` wraps `expo-audio` and is wired once at boot via `installAudioBackend()`. Tests substitute a stub backend and run without React Native. Backend lives at module scope (not in immer state) because immer freezes everything it touches and `expo-audio` owns mutable handles that must stay live.
  - **Pure auto-pin reconciler.** `computeReconcile(recentItems, autoPinnedSet, count)` returns `{toPin, toUnpin}` deltas — idempotent, survives RSS shuffles, and never touches manual pins (caller scopes the input set). Phase 1 fetcher's `onPostRefresh` callback drives it.
  - **One pinning manager, two protected-set sources.** Pinned creator media would have been silently evicted by GC because `pinnedHashes()` walked only the corpus pinned array. Solved with `registerExtraProtectedHashes(provider)` — creators registers `pinnedFeedItemHashes` at boot and the union flows through the existing `evictTo(budget, protected)` call. **Critical bug caught in simplify pass; verify any future feature that adds its own pinning calls `registerExtraProtectedHashes`.**
  - **Sealed dependency injection for `feedItemPin`.** All three deps (`network`, `preferenceGet`, `download`) are required. Wire defaults at the install site (`pinning/install.ts`), not module-level — eliminates the boot-race "half-installed" footgun where `pinFeedItem` would silently treat undefined `prefProvider` as Wi-Fi-only.
  - **Wi-Fi-only persists via raw preference.** `creators.wifiOnly` lives in the `preferences` k/v table rather than being added to the typed `usePreferencesStore` schema. Trade-off: avoids growing the typed store for a single feature flag; cost is the local `useState` mirror in `WifiOnlyToggle`. Revisit if more Creators preferences accumulate.
  - **Offline-first Wi-Fi gate is a hard rule, not a hint.** When the toggle is on and the user is on cellular, `pinFeedItem` returns `'deferred'` and writes to `pending_pins`. A "preference" that downloads anyway when convenient is a bug, not a feature.

  **Quirks worth knowing.**
  - **fast-xml-parser CDATA + namespaces.** `<itunes:image href="..."/>` parses as `{'@_href': '...'}` — needs `attrOf`, not `textOf`. CDATA blocks land under `__cdata` (configured via `cdataPropName`). Empty-string fall-through bit me: `attrOf(...) ?? textOf(...) ?? channelImage` returns `''` because `??` only short-circuits on null/undefined; switched to `||` for the image fallback chain. The shared parser instance plus `isArray` whitelist (item, entry, enclosure, media:content, media:thumbnail, podcast:chapters) keeps the three parsers seeing the same shapes.
  - **Inline chapter parsing requires ≥2 timestamps and monotonicity.** Single `00:00 Intro` lines aren't useful chapter docs and they appear inside lyric/schedule blocks; the regex hits `^(\d{1,2}:)?\d{1,2}:\d{2}` per line plus a strict-ascending filter to drop false positives. PPR-style descriptions reliably parse to per-question chapters.
  - **Vitest can't parse react-native.** Any test file that transitively imports `expo-sqlite` / RN modules fails with `Flow is not supported`. Workaround: split fetcher into pure `fetchCreatorDrafts(manifest, opts)` (testable) + DB-touching `refreshCreator` (not). The reconcile logic is similarly factored into a pure `computeReconcile` plus a DB-binding `reconcileAutoPins`. Don't dynamic-import DB repos from `pinningManager.pinnedHashes()` — that's how the GC fix originally broke the test suite. Use registration callbacks instead.
  - **Immer freezes objects placed in store state.** Backend handles, network listeners, and similar live objects must be held at module scope, not inside `create<T>(immer(...))`.
  - **`expo-audio` (not `expo-av`).** SDK 55 deprecates `expo-av`; the spec called for `expo-av` but `expo-audio` is the supported path. API surface: `createAudioPlayer({uri})`, `player.play()/pause()/seekTo()/setPlaybackRate()/remove()`, `currentTime`/`duration` properties. No `setNowPlayingInfoAsync` yet — lock-screen artwork + scrubbing deferred to Phase 5 polish.
  - **`onTick` rerender storm.** 1Hz position polling fires `useCreatorsStore.getState().onTick(currentTime)`; without a guard, every subscriber to `positionS` (slider, mini-bar, anything else watching the store) re-renders every second. Added a sub-second threshold inside `onTick` so the store no-ops when delta < 0.5s. Apply the same pattern to any future polling that writes to a hot-subscribed store.
  - **Memoize WebView `html`/`source`.** `ReaderWebView` and the YouTube iframe remount whenever the parent re-renders if the `html` string is rebuilt inline. Wrap in `useMemo([videoId])` / `useMemo([summary])` — otherwise switching tabs or scrolling the parent restarts video / reloads the article.
  - **Solo-project DB convention.** Per `CLAUDE.md`, no migration churn — all Phase 1 tables (`creator_follows`, `feed_items`, `media_progress`, `search_history`, `practice_voice`, `pending_pins`) appended to `0001_initial.sql` with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`. `feed_items.upsertFeedItems` chunks at 70 rows × 14 cols = 980 bound params (under SQLite's 999 cap).
  - **Build-script `build_creators` walker.** `content/creators/<id>/{manifest.json,avatar.webp,banner.webp}` → catalog entry with `creatorRole`, `creatorLanguages`, `hasQa` (derived from any `format: 'qa'` channel). Verified `pnpm build:corpus` emits both seed creators with avatar/banner blobs flowing through the existing image pipeline.

  **What's left.**
  - **Phase 4 — Global Search v1.** FlexSearch over `feed_items` titles/summaries/chapter markers + corpus titles (books/prayers/saints/CCC headings/collections/practices/Mass propers). Per-language indexes, kind-pluggable `IndexableSource` registry so Phase 6 + 8 slot in without rewrite. Per-question deep-linking via parsed chapter markers. Q&A boost, kind priors, recency tie-break. Localized empty-state with starter questions. `search_history` repo wired (created in Phase 1, ready). Privacy-preserving: queries never leave the device.
  - **Phase 5 — Polish.** Lock-screen artwork + scrubbing (likely a small Expo config plugin or `expo-music-info`), sleep-timer end-of-episode via `didJustFinish`, skeletons mirroring final layouts, pt-BR parity (≥40% of seed creators lusophone — currently 1/2 = 50%, holds for now), accessibility pass (VoiceOver / TalkBack labels, 44pt touch targets, color contrast), background download via `expo-background-fetch`. Dark-mode CSS in `ArticleReader` (currently hard-coded `#222` → unreadable in dark mode); `useReadingStyle` integration for font-size/line-height parity with Bible/CCC/books. Audio polling lifecycle on AppState change (pause polling when backgrounded).
  - **Phase 6 — Series (playlists).** `playlist` corpus kind, `build_playlists` walker, validator, transitive pin collector, Browse Series row with seasonal tag awareness, profile Series tab, playlist detail route, search adapter. Sets up Phase 7 (`practiceBinding` for guided prayers) and Phase 8 (canonical-question redirects, multilingual synonyms, full CCC paragraph indexing).
  - **Cross-cutting cleanups (deferred from simplify passes):** consolidate `bareId(ref)` across the 8 pre-existing call sites in collections/practices code (matched the existing pattern within Phase 2 to keep diff focused; codebase-wide cleanup is its own PR). Promote `FilterChip` from `app/creators/index.tsx` to `components/` once a second consumer needs it (e.g. saints filtering). Per-creator AppState lifecycle for `audioPlayer`'s 1Hz polling. Replace `deriveItemId`'s async `crypto.subtle.digest` with a sync hash if the per-refresh `await` storm becomes measurable (currently ~150 awaits per typical refresh, masked by `Promise.all`).
  - **Native build verification still pending.** All UI/audio paths typecheck and unit-test green, but lock-screen integration, background playback, and `expo-audio` audio-mode behaviour need device testing — the simulator won't flush all the iOS background-modes paths. App Store metadata `app.json` doesn't yet declare `UIBackgroundModes: audio`; add when validating Phase 5.

- **Collections refactor — coherent set + sourcing backlog (2026-05-08).** The 14 inherited library-era collections were a mix of kitchen-sink dumps (`devotions` had 17 unsorted items in one flat section), thin shells (`breviary` and `carmelite` had 2 items each), import-source labels (`ccel-classics`), and a personal scratchpad (`gustavo-personal`). Hearth v2 makes refs global, which means cross-listing is free — collections are now curated reading lists, not units of distribution. Rewrote the set to 19 user-facing collections (1 daily autoseed, 8 themes, 4 schools, 3 genres, 2 patrimony, 1 master liturgical-year scaffold) plus the still-hidden `examples`. **Deleted:** `devotions`, `breviary`, `ccel-classics`, `gustavo-personal`, `ave-maria-claretiano` (renamed). Two personal practices removed with `gustavo-personal`: `practice/mental-prayer-coloquio` and `practice/rosary-perpetual-novena`. **New:** `marian`, `eucharistic`, `way-of-the-cross`, `holy-spirit`, `for-the-dead`, `confession-and-conversion`, `mental-prayer`, `spiritual-classics`, `anglican-patrimony`, `liturgical-year`, `devocionario-claretiano` (replaces ave-maria-claretiano). **Cross-listing is the design**: `practice/litany-sacred-heart` lives in `litanies` AND `sacred-heart`; `practice/seven-sorrows` in `marian` AND `way-of-the-cross`; `practice/intimita-divina` in `carmelite` AND `mental-prayer`; etc. `seeAlso` arrays point sideways without duplicating annotations. Schema unchanged — refs are global strings, the 2-level section/sub-section depth limit handles everything. All 19 collections build clean against the corpus; verified every ref resolves to a catalog id.

  **/browse upgraded to a sectioned card discovery page** (the "Pray" home shortcut). Today-in-the-Church hero card driven by `pickSpotlight(season, date)` (Lent → Way of the Cross, Easter → Divine Mercy, Advent → Marian, June → Sacred Heart, May/Oct → Marian, November → For the Dead, fallback Catholic Daily Prayers). Sectioned rows: daily, formation, themes, genres, schools, patrimony (filtered by user locale via `entry.languages`), liturgical-year strip. Footer link routes to `/browse/all` which preserves the old flat alphabetical list as a power-user fallback. Section-to-collection mapping lives in `apps/app/src/features/collections/sectionLayout.ts` as plain TS — no `archetype` field added to manifests; the page is a hardcoded layout over a known set of collection IDs, which keeps JSON authoring concerns out of the data model.

  **Sourcing backlog** (collections that ship as scaffolds — rendered with placeholder prose flagging what to author/import next):
    - High-priority cross-cutting: Brown Scapular enrollment + Promise (marian, carmelite); Stabat Mater (way-of-the-cross, liturgical-year/Triduum); Pentecost & Easter sequences — *Veni Sancte Spiritus*, *Victimae Paschali Laudes* (holy-spirit, liturgical-year); Penitential Psalms practice (lent, confession); Litany of Christ the King; Funeral / In Paradisum prayers; Novena for the Faithful Departed (Nov 1–9).
    - Carmelite expansion: Teresa of Ávila (*Way of Perfection*, *Interior Castle*, *Autobiography*); John of the Cross (*Ascent*, *Dark Night*, *Spiritual Canticle*); Thérèse of Lisieux (*Story of a Soul*); Elizabeth of the Trinity (*O My God, Trinity Whom I Adore*).
    - Spiritual classics: Cassian *Conferences*, Scupoli *Spiritual Combat*, Granada *Sinner's Guide*, de Caussade *Abandonment*, Chautard *Soul of the Apostolate*, Brother Lawrence *Practice of the Presence*.
    - Liturgical-year scaffolds: O Antiphons (Dec 17–23), Christmas Novena, Stations of the Crib, Rorate Coeli, Tenebrae, Improperia, Pange Lingua, Adoration of the Cross liturgy, Marian-month devotions for May & October.
    - Catechetical chapters needed: St. Alphonsus (alphonsus-liguori prologue), Carmelite tradition (carmelite intro), Anglican Use/Ordinariate (anglican-patrimony intro), praying for the dead, the Seven Gifts of the Spirit, Stations history & variants, Divine Mercy image meaning + novena context.

  Plan: `~/.claude-personal/plans/our-current-collections-were-federated-adleman.md`.

- **v1 backwards-compat layer burned + content/ simplification (2026-05-08, follow-up to the corpus migration).** Hard migration: deleted everything that existed only to keep v1 callsites alive on the v2 corpus. Gone: `content/registry.ts` (façade), `features/libraries/{libraryManager,hooks,constants}.ts` (~350 lines of v1 install/remove shim), `db/migrations/0002_corpus.sql` + the `hearth-v2-purged` flag, `content/manifest-types.ts` (v1 type file). `EngineContext.fetchAsset` and `SourceContext.fetchAsset` lost their unused `libraryId` first param; `mass-of` calls `ctx.fetchAsset(path)` directly and the `HOST_LIBRARY = 'base'` constant is gone. `createEngineContext(chapterId?, languagePrefs?)` no longer takes a `libraryId`. `getChapterManifest`/`loadChapterContent`/`loadBookChapterText`/`resolvePrayer`/`getBookEntry`/`getProseText` all dropped the unused `_libraryId?` params. The browse UI (`/browse/index.tsx`, `/browse/[libraryId].tsx`) was rewritten directly against `contentIndex` — no install/download UI, every collection is openable. Followed up with a focused simplify pass on `apps/app/src/content/`: collapsed v1 `PracticeManifest`/`ChapterManifest`/`BookEntry` shims (resolver was returning v1 shapes via `as never` stubs); renamed `PracticeItemManifest` → `PracticeManifest`, `ChapterItemManifest` → `ChapterManifest`, `BookItemManifest` → `BookEntry`; moved `ProgramConfig`/`SlotDefault`/`AlternativeToRef`/`TocNode` into `manifestTypes.ts`. Dropped dead exports (`clearSources`, `rememberProse`, the wrong `getCatalogVersion` returning the literal `2`, `setCatalog` re-export, `search` re-export). Fixed `store.getBlob` race window — cache check now lives inside the inflight-keyed promise so concurrent callers for the same uncached hash share one fetch+write pipeline instead of double-fetching. Extracted `ensureManifestBody(hash)` into `contentIndex.ts` (replaces inline `getRememberedManifest ?? getJson + remember` patterns in `fetchOfAsset` and `loadMassProper`). Collapsed `loadPracticeData`/`loadPracticeTracks` into a shared `loadHashedRecord<T>`; replaced the repeated `canonicalize → residentFor → null-check` pattern with a single `residentItem<T>(id, kind)` helper. Net: `resolver.ts` shrank 25% (490 → 367 lines); `manifest-types.ts` (62 lines) deleted; ~185 lines net removed.

- **Hearth v2 book reader on the blob store (2026-05-08).** `features/libraries/bookReader.ts` `loadBookContent` now takes a corpus `bookId` instead of a `bookDirUri` (which was always undefined in v2). It looks up the book item manifest from `contentIndex`, fetches each `(chapter, lang)` markdown blob via `store.getText`, and inlines referenced images by hash → base64 data URI (matching the existing WebView shell expectations). Verified on web with `book/catechism-of-trent` — full WebView pagination + chapter rendering works. With this in place, the only remaining v1-coupled module flagged in the journal is gone. Bonus: 659 lines of dead code dropped (`content/sources/{filesystem,idb}.ts` deleted, type-only imports moved to `@/content/resolver`).

- **Hearth v2 — corpus-first content distribution (2026-05-08).** Replaced the monolithic `.pray` library bundling model with a content-addressed corpus served from `https://ember.dpgu.me/hearth/v2/`. Triggered by base library hitting ~70MB; every typo fix re-shipped the whole bundle. Rough numbers: first launch dropped from ~70MB to ~150KB (catalog + manifests + embedded starter pack); typo-fix update from ~70MB to ~5KB (single blob). Per-language Mass-proper splitting saves ~85% per Mass for single-language users. Shape: `content/{prayers,practices,books,chapters,masses,of-library,of-data,collections,checkup}/` flat by kind, hashed into immutable `blobs/{ab}/{cd}/{full-sha256}` by `scripts/build-corpus.py`. Client (`apps/app/src/content/{store,contentIndex,resolver}.ts`) lazily fetches blobs and warms a small set of always-resident manifests (~133 blobs critical, ~144 deferred) at boot. Pinning is the only persistent personal-content state besides plan-of-life slots — `features/pinning/pinningManager.ts` walks an item recursively and marks all referenced blobs for offline; pinned-items list lives in `preferences['pinned-items']` (no new DB tables). The legacy library install/remove shim that bridged v2 to the old `library/` UI was burned in a follow-up — see the v1 backwards-compat entry above. Invariants worth remembering: (1) `canonicalize(id, hintKind)` is now a HARD filter — passing a hint kind disables fallthrough to other kinds (otherwise wrongly-typed manifests leak through and crash the engine — see commit `70e00265`); (2) async resolver functions return `undefined` for missing items, but TanStack Query rejects `undefined` from queryFn — coerce to `null` at the queryFn boundary and unwrap at the consumer; (3) `getCollectionsForItem` and `getEntriesByKind` are cached and invalidated via a `catalogVersion` counter exposed by `useCatalogVersion()` so practice cards re-render when deferred manifests warm. Spec: `~/.claude-personal/plans/we-have-a-huge-dynamic-sloth.md`. Branch: `hearth-v2-corpus`.
- **Maestro E2E suite, iOS-first (2026-05-05).** Integration tests live under `apps/app/.maestro/` (config + flows + a shared reset subflow). `pnpm --filter @ember/app test:e2e` runs them against a running iOS sim with a dev build (`pnpm ios` first, then `pnpm test:e2e`). Three flows: home → practice → complete (daily loop), add a practice to plan, and a `select`-DSL practice (grace-meals, Before vs After). Setup: `brew install maestro`. Local-only for now; no CI runner.

  **Determinism via `/dev/reset` deep link.** Production app has no test mode, so we added a dev-only Expo Router route at `apps/app/src/app/dev/reset.tsx` (guarded by `if (!__DEV__) return <Redirect href="/" />` — never reaches a release build). Note: an underscore-prefixed folder (`_test`, `__test`) would have been excluded from Expo Router; `dev/` is just a regular route. Maestro hits `ember://dev/reset?date=YYYY-MM-DD&enableSlots=<csv>` at the top of every flow. The route calls `resetForTests()` (`apps/app/src/db/test-fixtures.ts`) which: truncates writable SQLite tables (events, user_practices, user_practice_slots, completions, cursors, preferences, cache, cached_translations) via a single `execAsync` (multi-statement), resets the in-memory event store, re-runs `seedPractices` + `seedCursors`, emits `SlotUpdated` events to enable the requested slots, and applies a fixed time-travel date via `usePreferencesStore`. We also `queryClient.clear()` so TanStack caches don't keep stale data. No app reload needed.

  **Why not reuse `resetDatabase()`.** The existing `apps/app/src/db/client.ts:resetDatabase` deletes the SQLite file and calls `expo.reloadAppAsync()`. That works for the Settings UI but is hostile to Maestro: after the reload the deep-link query params are gone, so we'd need a persisted "pending test seed" flag interleaved with prod boot. The in-place reset is contained in dev code and skips the reload entirely.

  **Selectors.** ~6 `testID`s added at the surfaces tests touch: `slot-row-{practiceId}`, `slot-toggle-{practiceId}` (TimeBlockSection), `practice-card-{manifestId}` (catalog), `add-to-plan-button` (catalog detail), `practice-home-button` (PracticeFlow), `select-option-{id}` (SelectBlock), and `shortcut-{labelKey}` (AppShortcuts). Everything else relies on the existing `accessibilityLabel`s — Maestro reads them natively, so we only added IDs where labels were dynamic, i18n-interpolated, or absent on critical hit targets. `AnimatedCheckbox` had to be widened to accept `testID` (it didn't pass-through).

  **Fixture practice: `base:grace-meals`.** Has a manifest (so it's seeded with a disabled slot by default), a minimal `select` flow (Before/After Meals), and `completion: manual` so it exercises the home-screen toggle path rather than a "Mark complete" button. Slot key is `base:grace-meals::1` — practice IDs are qualified `<libraryId>:<practiceId>` and the seed code uses `String(i + 1)` for slot ids (`apps/app/src/db/seed.ts`).

  **Test date: `2026-01-14`.** A quiet ferial Wednesday — no major celebration banners cluttering the home screen. Avoid Easter-week / Solemnity dates: they re-flow the home layout in ways tests don't need to assert on.

- **CCEL ThML importer scaffolded for the Wisdom track (2026-04-27).** New `content/libraries/ccel-classics/scripts/ccel/` Python module + `content/libraries/ccel-classics/scripts/ccel-import.py` shim convert a CCEL ThML XML into an Ember book directory (`book.json` + per-language Markdown chapters), wired to the existing `pnpm build:libraries` pipeline with no changes to it. Uses `lxml` (`pip install lxml`); `scripts/build-libraries.sh` is unchanged. New library bootstrapped at `content/libraries/ccel-classics/`. Reasoning, mapping table, and pilot list live in `docs/content/ccel-import.md`.

  ThML parsing quirks: (1) CCEL files declare `<!DOCTYPE ThML PUBLIC "...thml.dtd">` and use HTML named entities (`&nbsp;`, `&mdash;`, `&eacute;`, etc.) inside XML — without the DTD, lxml errors. The importer strips the DOCTYPE before parsing and pre-substitutes a curated set of named entities (`content/libraries/ccel-classics/scripts/ccel/thml.py:_NAMED_ENTITIES`); unknown entities are silently dropped. (2) `<pb/>` (page break) is a void element whose tail whitespace would otherwise leak as a stray `  ` line between paragraphs — the markdown walker skips whitespace-only tails on void elements and on unknown void blocks generally. (3) `<scripRef>` v1 strategy: drop the `passage="…"` attribute, preserve only the visible text. Live scripture linking is deferred (book-format.md "Future Directions"). (4) Cross-document `<a href="…">` links are dropped (count logged); the visible citation text remains. v2 path: rewrite to in-Ember links once the WebView reader can handle deep links — currently it can't.

  Chapter granularity is auto-picked: deepest divN whose median word count lands in [500, 5000] words. Override with `--chapter-level div1|div2|div3`. Ancestor-qualified ids (`book-1-chapter-1`) prevent collisions when the same `n="1"` appears under multiple parent divs. Run `python3 -m unittest ccel.tests.test_importer -v` from `content/libraries/ccel-classics/scripts/` for the full importer test suite (15 tests, no extra deps beyond `lxml`).

  Licensing reasoning: CCEL underlying texts are largely US public domain; CCEL claims copyright only on its formatting/encoding work. By emitting fresh Markdown derived from the public-domain text we don't redistribute their formatting. We still credit CCEL in `book.json` `sources[]` and the library `description`. Per-work rights statement still needs manual review before publishing — a small number of CCEL works are translator-restricted.

  Network note: in this dev environment, both `ccel.org` (sandbox host blocklist) and CCEL itself (returns 403 to scripted clients) were unreachable, so the importer was developed and tested against a synthetic ThML fixture (`content/libraries/ccel-classics/scripts/ccel/tests/fixtures/sample.xml`) that exercises every element in the mapping. End-to-end smoke against the live `ccel-classics` library succeeded; pilot conversions (Imitation, Devout Life, Confessions, Summa Contra Gentiles) need to be run on a machine with CCEL access.

- **CCEL pilot conversion learnings (2026-04-28).** Ran the importer against all four pilot ThMLs end-to-end on a machine with CCEL access. Final shipped library: `ccel-classics-0.1.0.pray` (1.5MB, 656 files, 4 books / 638 chapters total). Several real-world quirks the synthetic fixture missed:

  (1) **DC fields use both element and HTML form**: CCEL ThML 1.04 uses `<DC.Creator scheme="short-form">Thomas à Kempis</DC.Creator>` (element), not the HTML `<meta name="DC.Creator" content="…"/>` form. The original extractor only looked at `<meta>` and pulled nothing. Fixed `_meta_value` to scan element form first, then HTML; added `_meta_values_with_attr` to disambiguate by `scheme`/`sub`. Author preference: `scheme="short-form"` → `scheme="file-as"` → first DC.Creator. Skip `scheme="ccel"` (the bare slug).

  (2) **`<DC.Date sub="Created">` is the digital-edition timestamp, NOT composition**. Imitation showed `1998-12-10`, Devout Life `2002-08-08`. Importer now prefers `<firstPublished>` / `<published>` from `<generalInfo>`; only accepts `DC.Date` if it's a pre-1900 4-digit year. For modern translations / works without `firstPublished`, pass `--composed` explicitly.

  (3) **Source URL fallback**: not every CCEL file has a `DC.Identifier` URL; many only have the slug `ccel/kempis/imitation.htm`. Synthesize `https://www.ccel.org/ccel/<authorID>/<bookID>/` from `<authorID>`/`<bookID>` in `<electronicEdInfo>` when no http(s) DC.Identifier exists.

  (4) **`DC.Language` may be ISO 639-3** (`eng`, `por`, `lat`, `grc`). Extended `_LANG_MAP` to handle 3-letter codes.

  (5) **Skip CCEL navigation indexes/contents**: every CCEL file ends with a `<div1 title="Indexes">` (or `<div1 title="INDEX.">` for older files) wrapping `<div2 title="Subject Index">`, `<div2 title="Index of Scripture References">`, etc. Some also start with a print-edition `<div1 title="CONTENTS.">` table of contents. These are auto-generated reference data and produce mangled output. Filter by title (`index`, `indexes`, `contents`, `index of …`) at root level + at chapter level — covered by `_is_index_section`.

  (6) **CCEL chapters open with print decorations**: `<h4>The First Chapter</h4>` then `<h3>{title}</h3>` before the first paragraph. We already inject our own `# {title}` H1 from the section attribute, so any leading sub-heading inside a chapter body is redundant — strip everything until the first non-empty, non-`#` line.

  (7) **`<p> </p>` whitespace-only spacers** are common in CCEL files for visual layout. Drop them entirely instead of emitting blank "  " lines.

  (8) **Whitespace doubling around `<pb/>`**: `<p>…about\n\n<pb/>\n\nthe Trinity…</p>` produced `learnedly about  the Trinity` (double space). Added `re.sub(r" {2,}(?!\n)", " ", body)` in `render()` — collapses 2+ spaces to 1 except when they form a Markdown hard break (`  \n`).

  (9) **`<verse>` in Augustine's Confessions** is a poetic line group (children: `<l>`) — same as `<lg>`. Added to the `lg/verse` handler.

  (10) **Recursion bug in unknown-block fallback**: `<table>` was in `_BLOCK_TAGS` but had no handler in `_walk_block`, so it fell through to a `_walk_inline(el, b)` call that routed back to `_walk_block` based on `_BLOCK_TAGS` membership → infinite recursion. Fixed by walking children directly in the fallback rather than re-dispatching the same element.

  (11) **CCEL `<div1 type="Book" title="Preface">`**: Summa Contra Gentiles has a Preface div with `type="Book"`. Original slug logic preferred `type` over title, producing `book.md`. Re-prioritized: title takes precedence over bare type when no `n` is present.

  (12) **Long titles → filesystem errors**: Devout Life's `<div1>` titles include the full part-summary, then chapter titles add another sentence — concatenated, the slug exceeded 255 bytes (macOS filename limit). Slug now detects "PART/BOOK/CHAPTER N" prefixes and uses just the prefix (`part-i`, `chapter-vii`); falls back to the first six words of the title.

  (13) **Augustine Confessions has no `<div2>`** — the whole text is `<div1>` per book with `<p class="chapter">CHAPTER I</p>` paragraph-markers inline. `--chapter-level auto` correctly picks `div1`, producing 13 large book-length files (43–150KB each). The print-edition CHAPTER markers stay as plain paragraphs in the output; promoting them to `## H2` based on the `class="chapter"` attribute is a future refinement.

  (14) **The pilot test repo**: `timothyhartzog/theology-corpus` (small Reformed-evangelical CCEL mirror) has Aquinas's Summa Theologica and Augustine's Confessions/City of God, but most Catholic pilots had to be fetched directly from `ccel.org`. Pilot inputs live under `tmp/ccel-sources/` (gitignored).

  After the pilot pass, the regex usage in `content/libraries/ccel-classics/scripts/ccel/` was simplified — several string-pattern checks were converted to plain set/startswith/word-split tests; only narrow text-parsing regexes remain (named-entity scan, DOCTYPE strip, date-format coercion, slugify, whitespace normalization).

- **Intimità Divina liturgical-map + TOC overhaul (2026-04-26).** User reported that on the 4th Sunday of Easter the practice surfaced "Patrocinio di S. Giuseppe (Mercoledì della II sett. dopo Pasqua)" instead of the proper Sunday meditation. Root cause: the temporal Easter block of `liturgical-map.json` was anchored at `easter/1/0 → giorno-142` (which is actually Easter *Tuesday*), so every Easter-season slot was off by 2 chapters and the breakage cascaded through Pentecost and the first weeks of post-Pentecost (Trinity Sunday — `giorno-196` — was even sitting in `reserves`). All `fixedDates` were similarly broken: 02-02 "Purificazione" pointed at `giorno-138` which is the Good Friday meditation; the December O Antiphons (12-22..12-25) were off by 4. Fix anchored each season to the explicit liturgical labels embedded in the chapter files themselves (e.g. giorno-140 has `*Domenica di Risurrezione*`, giorno-189 has `*Domenica di Pentecoste*`, giorno-196 has `*I Domenica dopo Pentecoste*`). After the rewrite the temporal block is a complete 8×7 Easter grid, post-Pent has all 25 weeks with the Corpus Christi/Sacred Heart octaves modeled (giorno-200 "Maria Mediatrice" is the lone floating chapter; giorno-324–327 are post-Pent week 18 leftovers). Added `fixed/12-26..01-06` and `christmas/1/0` temporal entries so Christmas/Epiphany octave meditations surface as primary instead of a reserve fallback. Major feasts (Immacolata, Assunzione, Natività di Maria, Tutti i Santi, Defunti, etc.) moved into `feasts` so they override temporal as the primary meditation; lesser ones stay in `fixedDates` as additionals. Added `isLastSundayOfOctober` to the resolver and a `feasts['movable/last-sunday-october']` slot so Cristo Re works year-round (the 4th-vs-5th Sunday issue can't be solved by `weekdaysOfMonths` keys alone). Verification harness: `scripts/verify-intimita-divina-map.ts` walks 32 anchor dates Nov 2025 → Nov 2026 and asserts the right meditation surfaces — full year passes.

  Companion fix: the production `book.json` TOC was missing 21 chapter files (10 `festa-vol*-*` feasts, 9 `giorno-368..376` "amore" series, plus `presentazione`/`prefazione`) and had Vol II/III boundaries off by 2 (giorno-140 Easter Sunday was filed under Vol II "Settuagesima al Sabato Santo"). A draft `intimita-divina-v2/book.json` had the right structure but was Italian-only. Adopted v2's 9-section TOC (front-matter + 6 volumes + 2 feste sections) with full en-US/pt-BR localization re-using v1's existing translations for overlapping chapters and translating fresh for the 9 new + 4 structural nodes; deleted the v2 directory. Added `vatican-letter.md` (Segreteria di Stato letter, 12 Feb 1957, signed Mons. Angelo Dell'Acqua, plus Imprimatur) and `abbreviazioni.md` (the abbreviation key — G.C. for S. Giovanni della Croce, T.G. for S. Teresa di Gesù, etc.) in all three languages — both transcribed from `intimita_divina_originals_pdf/ID_I.pdf` pages 8 and 20. Library `languages` field updated to `["it", "en-US", "pt-BR"]` (was `["it"]` despite the book having translations).

  Lessons: (1) when a temporal map looks "off by N", check the chapter files' italic label lines first — they're authoritative anchors and cheap to scan; (2) the resolver tries `reserves` *before* `fixedDates` as a temporal fallback (`liturgical-day-resolver.ts` lines 183–191), so any feast whose `pos.key` isn't in `temporal` will surface a random reserve as primary unless you also add the `pos.key` (e.g. `christmas/1/0`, `fixed/12-26`) to `temporal` directly; (3) `weekdaysOfMonths` keys are 1st–5th by `Math.floor((date-1)/7)` — there's no "last X of month" so liturgical anchors like Cristo Re need a movable-feast helper.

- **Liturgical calendar + Mass: one source-driven authority (2026-05-31, OF half).** A user report — on 2026-05-31 the Mass showed the *Visitation* (a Feast) instead of *Holy Trinity Sunday* (a Solemnity), with "bifurcated" readings offering both Masses' gospels — exposed that the app carried **three independent date→celebration→id derivations** across **three id namespaces**, plus two precedence implementations: `packages/liturgical` (calendar brain, `entries.json`), `packages/mass-of` (OF Mass; its own `calendar.ts` + a string-heuristic `applyPrecedence`), and `packages/mass-propers` (EF; Divinum Officium file-ids).
