The Trinity bug was a literal symptom: `applyPrecedence` detected a privileged temporal day only by an id ending in `.sunday`, but movable solemnities use `tempore.solemnity.most-holy-trinity` (no `.sunday`), so a coinciding feast wrongly suppressed the solemnity and survived as a per-slot alternate.
  - **`content/liturgical/entries.json` is not generated from anything.** Git archaeology (`7cfeb767` → `175f49cf`): it began as a hand-authored `liturgical-entries.jsonl` (an LLM research agent gathered it — the prose descriptions are the tell), was transformed by a pure jsonl→ts script (since deleted), and flattened into a minified blob whose readable source + tooling were thrown away. No link to the canonical propers — which is *why* it silently drifts (wrong precedence; missing celebrations like Mary, Mother of the Church on the Monday after Pentecost). Decision: **generate the calendar from the canonical upstreams** (ember-extra for OF, Divinum Officium for EF) and retire the hand-authored file. Two per-form calendars, no cross-form identity matching — each stays in lockstep with its own propers.
  - **The win that makes the bridge free: ember-extra's calendar ids *are* the Mass-proper ids.** `scripts/build-of-calendar.mjs` reads `content/of-data/calendar/{sanctorale,tempore/solemnity}` (the synced ember-extra calendar — ranks, dates, movable codes, regional variants) and emits `content/liturgical/of-calendar.json` as `LiturgicalEntry[]`. Because the entries keep ember-extra ids (`sanctorale.05-31`, `tempore.solemnity.most-holy-trinity`), a resolved celebration *is* its proper id (`mass/of/sanctorale/05-31`) — no second lookup, no namespace split. Movable celebrations carry no date upstream; a small code→rule table in the generator supplies them (Trinity=Easter+56, Mary Mother of the Church `movable.05-35`=Easter+50, Immaculate Heart `movable.05-32`=Easter+69, etc.).
  - **One precedence authority: `resolveOfDay` (GIRM Table of Liturgical Days).** New `packages/liturgical/src/of-day.ts` merges the temporal cycle (from `getOfLiturgicalPosition` + the ported `ofTemporeIds`) with the generated sanctoral entries and ranks them on a single 1–13 scale. Replaces both the calendar's old Sunday-only `applySundaySuppression` *for the Mass path* and `mass-of`'s `applyPrecedence` (deleted). `mass-of`'s `load()` now just asks `resolveOfDay` which celebration wins and builds *only* that Mass (self-contained, no cross-Mass alternates → no bifurcation). Gotcha worth keeping: ember-extra doesn't distinguish a **Feast of the Lord** (Presentation 02-02, Transfiguration 08-06, Holy Cross 09-14, Lateran 11-09 — these outrank an Ordinary-Time Sunday) from a saint's feast (which loses to it); both are rank `feast`, so the four are named explicitly in `feastOfTheLordIds`.
  - **Two calendars coexist *by purpose*, and `entries.json` is not going away.** `resolveOfDay` (computed temporal + generated sanctoral) drives the **Mass** — it needs the exact Mass-proper id. `buildYearCalendar` over the curated `entries.json` drives the **home card + month grid** — a *display* calendar that needs the temporal feasts (Christmas, Easter, Epiphany, Ascension, Pentecost, Mary Mother of God…) and the **holy-day-of-obligation** flags, neither of which ember-extra's data carries. A first attempt to point the home at the generated `of-calendar.json` (sanctoral-only) silently dropped every temporal feast and HDO badge from the home — reverted. Mary, Mother of the Church was instead added to `entries.json` directly (one curated entry) so it shows on the home, while the Mass gets it from ember-extra's `sanctorale.movable.05-35`. `resolveOfDay` skips any `tempore.*` entry so the sanctoral list can't double-count a computed temporal day. Lesson: the propers calendar and the display calendar answer different questions; deleting `entries.json` is *not* free — it would require regenerating the temporal-feast display set + an HDO overlay (jurisdiction-dependent), which ember-extra doesn't provide. **EF precedence is now data-driven too** (`build-ef-ranks.mjs` → `content/propers/ef-ranks.json` → `chooseProperSourceByRank`): rather than the hand-authored category, the EF Mass picks tempora-vs-sancti by Divinum Officium's own `[Rank]` occurrence number (Feria 1, Duplex 3, Sunday 6.9, Duplex I classis 6.5; the `rubrica 1960` variant is taken for 1962). No invented rubrics — DO's data.
  - **Then the user reversed the two "won't-do" conclusions below** — directing that the Mass assembly be pulled out of `flow.json` into a code-built `producer/mass`, and that the Mass packages be consolidated. What was done: `mass-of` + `mass-propers` merged into one **`@ember/mass`** (cycle-free — only `liturgical` itself can't fold in, since `liturgical → content-engine → liturgical` would cycle, so it stays a separate package), exposing `buildMassFlow(day)` which builds the Order-of-Mass section tree in TypeScript and references the liturgical text by fragment id. The app's `producer/mass` ContentSource (`apps/app/src/sources/mass-flow.ts`) loads the day via the `mass-of` DataSource, fetches the merged `content/liturgical/mass-fragments.json` (built by `scripts/build-mass-fragments.mjs`), resolves `buildMassFlow` through the engine, and preprocesses to primitives. `content/practices/mass/flow.json` collapsed to a single form `select` (OF → `include producer/mass`; EF still `call ef-form-body` until the EF producer lands). The fragments stay declarative content — only the *assembly/branching* moved to code.
  - **Circular-import gotcha (the producer is the first to resolve a full sub-flow).** `producer/mass` needs `preprocessFlow` to resolve its sub-`include`s, and `preprocessFlow` imports the source registry — so a *static* `import` created `registry → mass-flow → preprocessFlow → registry`, leaving `massFlowSource` undefined at `registerSource` time (`undefined.id`). Fix: lazily `import()` `preprocessFlow` + `engineContext` inside `fetch` (they're only needed there), so `mass-flow` has no static cycle. Verified by `apps/app/src/sources/mass-flow.test.ts` (boots the corpus + registries, calls `massFlowSource.fetch` directly, asserts the OF Mass assembles end-to-end into primitives).
  - **The prior (now-superseded) argument, kept for the record:** the close originally read that a producer was *won't-do* because `flow.json` already made no decisions — it binds to data (`celebration.rite`, `includeGloria`, `season`, `day.celebrations`) with the decisions in code (`resolveOfDay`/`deriveIncludeGloria`/`chooseProperSourceByRank`), so rewriting the readable fragments as imperative `FlowBlock`-building TS would remove zero duplication and hurt maintainability. The user weighed that and chose the programmatic assembly + package unification anyway.
  - **EF half: `producer/mass` is now form-aware (2026-06-01).** The same producer assembles both forms, dispatched by the include `params.form` (`'of' | 'ef'`), so `flow.json` is a thin form `select` of `{ include: producer/mass, params: { form } }` — the EF branch's old `{ call: ef-form-body }` is gone. New `buildEFFlow()` in `@ember/mass` is the EF analog of `buildMassFlow`: it emits the view switch (Full Mass / Propers Only / Readings Only) and the Order-of-Mass sequence — i.e. it replaces the four assembly fragments (`ef-form-body`, `ef-extraordinary-view`, `ef-extraordinary-propers-view`, `ef-extraordinary-readings-view`, now deleted) — while the EF *content* fragments (`ef-kyrie`, `ef-canon-of-the-mass`, …, still in `mass-fragments.json`) stay declarative `call` targets and the day's propers stay `proper` slots resolved per-slot against Divinum Officium at render (`ProperSlot` unchanged — EF is slot-centric, no day object, so this is the right seam to keep). The producer's EF path skips the `mass-of` load entirely (`flowData: {}`). Producer `version` bumped to `2` to drop stale OF-only caches. Verified by `buildEFFlow.test.ts` (assembly order + slots) and a second `mass-flow.test.ts` case (EF resolves to primitives with `proper` interactions and no OF `celebration-banner`). **Not ported (deliberately):** the per-slot proper *resolution* still happens in `ProperSlot`/`useProperForSlot` at render, not in the producer — moving it server-side (emitting resolved prose, retiring `ProperSlot`) is a larger, riskier change (DO commemoration/concurrence) left as a follow-up; the producer port already delivers the unification (one producer, thin flow.json).

- **The Novenário — a year-round novena collection (2026-06-19).** Imported a Brazilian *Novenário* book's list as `collection/novenario`: **74 new bilingual novenas authored** + 12 existing ones reused = **86 entries**, organized by calendar month (Janeiro–Dezembro + *Móveis & Livres* + *Novenas Preciosas*). Source-selection groundwork (FishEaters vs PrayMoreNovenas, scraped + cross-matched + scored against a rubric) lives in `docs/content/novenario-sources.md`. Findings worth keeping:
  - **Every Ember novena is one uniform house template — and the content is *original*, not scraped.** `st-joseph-novena`/`perpetual-help-novena`/`st-therese-novena` are all: static opening prayer → `cycle` over `data/days.json` (`{dayTitle, meditation, intention}`) → Our Father/Hail Mary/Glory Be → closing prayer, with `program: {totalDays, progressPolicy:'continue', completionBehavior:'offer-restart'}`. The day meditations are concise original compositions *in the saint's spirit*, not copied from any site. So the right move was to **author original bilingual content in that voice** (grounded in each saint's real life/virtues, with obscure ones web-verified) rather than reproduce FishEaters/PMN text — which also sidesteps PMN's copyright. The earlier FE-vs-PMN evaluation still paid off: it set each novena's authentic emphasis and confirmed which devotions warrant distinct-day richness.
  - **Atom correctness: the day's *intention* is prayed text, not a rubric.** The existing novenas rendered the per-day intention as `{type:'rubric', text:'{{intention}}'}`, but `primitives-guide.md` is explicit that a "For X" petition (its own *"For purity of body"* example) is **prayed text → inline `prayer`**, not a stage direction. Fixed it to `{type:'prayer', inline:'{{intention}}'}` in the new ones and **harmonized the 12 reused flows** so the whole collection is consistent. This works inside a `cycle` because `engine/vars.ts` `deepSubstitute` returns the *raw* value for a whole-string `{{path}}` match — so `inline: "{{intention}}"` resolves to the day entry's `LocalizedContent` object, exactly like a literal inline prayer. The `"Today's intention:"` label stays a `rubric` (it genuinely *is* an instruction).
  - **`manifest.subtitle` carries the feast window** (e.g. `"May 11–20"` / `"11–20 de maio"`), bilingual, so the calendar collection reads naturally; added to new manifests and back-filled on the reused ones.
  - **Shape variations:** the Seven Sorrows is a **7-day septenary** (`totalDays:7`, one sorrow per day); the Surrender Novena (Fr. Dolindo) repeats the *"O Jesus, I surrender myself to You; take care of everything"* refrain in each day's intention; and there are two **generic template novenas** (`any-saint`, `any-marian-feast`) with `[Saint N.]`-style placeholders the user fills in.
  - **Process:** built via a throwaway generator (`scripts/novenario/{plan.py,generate.py}`) fed by parallel authoring agents writing to `.nov-build/<slug>.json`; both the generator and scratch were **removed after generation** — the flat `content/practices/*` + `content/collections/novenario.json` JSON is the source of truth, per the repo's content model. Corpus builds clean; all 86 collection refs resolve.
  - **Quality audit + fixes — because it's AI-composed, it had to be adversarially checked.** The day-content is original (AI-written, not scraped), so I ran an independent audit: 13 skeptical reviewer agents (separate from the authors) fact-checking every novena against authoritative sources, plus orthodoxy / Portuguese / slop passes. **Caveat that matters:** live WebSearch was largely *down* during the audit, so reviewers leaned on model knowledge — an AI "PASS" on a fact it couldn't actually web-verify is *not* verification. So I re-checked the date-sensitive claims live afterward: Frei Galvão's memorial is **Oct 25** (pt.wikipedia — the English infobox's "11 May" is his canonization date, a common artifact), Anchieta **Jun 9**, Josemaría **Jun 26**, All Souls **Nov 2**. ~24 fixes applied: **wrong feast windows** (Christ the King is movable → "9 days before the feast", not Nov 11–19; Holy Souls must end Nov 2 not Nov 1; Josemaría Jun 17–25 not "Jun 18"); **Portuguese errors** (a meaning-*inverting* mistranslation in St. Rita Day 4 — "prayed they be taken rather than stain their souls" had become "…to stain their souls"; gender "a arauto"→"mensageira"; "conceição"→"concepção"; a stray Latin "Hauri" left in an Aquinas day title); and **theological/precision softenings** (Assumption Day 2 over-asserted beyond the dogma's deliberate silence on whether Mary died; Corpus Christi history miscounted the *Adoro te devote* as part of the proper office; St. Clare "twice… by the Sacrament"; the Franciscan peace prayer kept as "attributed to" St. Francis; private revelations recast as "recounted"). The other ~50 novenas passed. Lesson: original devotional content can be made trustworthy, but only with an *independent* check whose factual claims are themselves verified live — and a final human read is still wise.
  - **Daily-depth pass — each day gained a prayer.** Review feedback: the days felt thin next to PrayMoreNovenas (~46 words/day vs their ~150). For the 74 new novenas, every day was enriched — meditation expanded to ~70 words (preserving the audited facts) and a NEW ~50-word day-specific **prayer** added — lifting each day to ~115–150 words. Schema: `days[].prayer` (bilingual), rendered in the flow `cycle` as an inline prayer between the meditation and the intention. The 12 reused practices were left at their original concise depth (scope choice). A bad-pattern re-grep confirmed none of the audit fixes regressed in the rewrite.

## Architecture Decisions

- **Unified flow system replaces variants, forms, and multiple flows (2026-04-12).** The content engine accumulated four content-selection mechanisms (variants, forms, multiple flows, setKeyOverride) each built for specific practices. Decision: collapse all four into one `select` section type — a conditional branch that reads context (day of week, time of day, user preference) and picks an option. One flow per practice. Different meditation traditions become separate practices grouped by `alternativeTo`. Full spec: `docs/features/unified-flow-system.md`. Key insight: all four mechanisms were just conditional content selection with different indirection layers. The `variant` DB column was never actually written — every user was on the default.

- **DSL completeness: no practice should need custom app code (2026-04-12).** The flow JSON must be expressive enough that the app is a pure reader. The `useLiturgicalMeditation` hook (118 lines, meditacoes-ligorio only) was the clearest violation — calendar→chapter resolution hard-coded in React. Fix: `resolve` field on `FlowDefinition` with pluggable strategies (first: `"liturgical-day"`), and dynamic `prose` sections that load book chapters by template-variable chapter IDs. The resolution algorithm stays in `@ember/liturgical` but is invoked declaratively from the flow JSON, not from a practice-specific hook.

- **Visible `select` blocks require explicit app-side rendering + override wiring (2026-04-14).** After migrating practices to unified flow, the "title-only, no body" symptom appeared only on practices whose main content lived under top-level visible `select` sections (e.g., Mass). The content engine was already emitting rendered `select` sections, but `SectionBlock` had no `select` case, so those branches rendered as `null`. Fix: add `SelectBlock` UI renderer and thread `selectOverrides` state through `PracticeFlow` so selector changes re-resolve flow content live. Silent/static patterns (e.g., Way of Light, Bible+Catechism) were unaffected.

- **Large-flow `select` switching is fast only when non-selected branches are lazy (2026-04-14).** For visible `select`, eagerly resolving sections for every option on each pass caused noticeable delay on heavy flows like Mass. The UI only needs labels for all options and sections for the active option, so the engine now resolves sections only for the selected option and emits empty section arrays for non-selected options. This keeps behavior identical while removing most of the per-toggle work.

- **Unified flow completed migration path for dynamic Liguori meditations (2026-04-14).** The old `useLiturgicalMeditation` hook was removed and replaced with declarative flow resolution: `resolveFlowAsync` executes flow-level `resolve` strategies (starting with `liturgical-day`), injects template vars, preloads dynamic prose chapter text, and then renders sections without practice-specific React logic. This made Liguori content a pure flow JSON concern and aligned slot naming previews to show the first visible select choice in Plan of Life.

- **Legacy slot flow/variant plumbing removed from runtime writes (2026-04-14).** Slot creation/update no longer writes a `variant` field, and the unused `changeSlotFlow` mutation path was removed from repositories/hooks. The database column remains for backward compatibility, but runtime behavior now treats slots as practice-scoped schedules with unified-flow selection handled by the engine.

- **Dynamic prose must support book-language fallback to avoid false-empty renders (2026-04-14).** `meditacoes-ligorio` is PT-BR-only content, so strict active-language loading can render empty prose when app/content language is EN. The fix was to make dynamic prose language behavior explicit (`langPolicy`) and preload chapter text across candidate languages (active/content/book languages), then localize from the merged payload. This keeps the flow declarative while keeping side-effect handling inside bounded source adapters.

- **Flow contract now supports explicit versioning (`flowVersion`) (2026-04-14).** The engine accepts legacy flows with no version and validates `flowVersion: "1"` for explicit-contract flows, while rejecting unsupported future versions early. This gives us room to evolve semantics safely without silent behavior drift.

- **Content loader migration phase used canonical `flow.json` preference without practice IDs (2026-04-14).** During unified-flow rollout, filesystem loading preferred top-level `flow.json` over legacy manifest declarations to avoid practice-specific branches. This transitional behavior was later tightened into strict `manifest.flow` loading once content migration was complete.

- **Legacy flow migration paths removed from filesystem loader (2026-04-14).** The temporary runtime adapters (`migrateLegacyLiturgicalMeditationFlow`, `flows[]` manifest fallback, canonical-vs-declared flow scoring) were removed. Loader contract is now strict: every practice must declare `manifest.flow`, and content was migrated accordingly.

- **Legacy Liguori sidecar flow removed (2026-04-14).** `practices/meditacoes-ligorio/flows/daily.json` was removed to avoid split truth and stale fixed-slot placeholders (`feastLabel`, `meditation-feast`). Canonical `flow.json` is the single source of truth.

- **Liguori resolver now includes monthly supplemental meditations on day 25 (2026-04-14).** `resolveLiturgicalDay` emits supplemental entries for the 25th-day monthly set, and the content-engine strategy includes those chapters in the `meditations` array. This restores expected multi-meditation days on the 25th while keeping logic in the liturgical kernel instead of app-layer hooks.

- **Day-25 rendering fix required reserve fallback for temporal holes (2026-04-14).** Some 25th dates (e.g., during Pentecost-transition gaps) had no temporal chapter in the map, so only the monthly supplemental meditation appeared. `resolveLiturgicalDay` now falls back to `reserves` when temporal lookup fails, preserving the expected “temporal + monthly 25th” pairing.

- **Liguori recurrence keys now use generic `weekdaysOfMonths` + fixed-date keys (2026-04-14).** The old family keys (`first-friday/*`, `march-wednesday/*`, `25th-of-month/*`) were migrated to two generic mechanisms: fixed-date keys (`MM-DD`, e.g. `04-25`) and `weekdaysOfMonths` keys (`1st-friday-of-january`, `3rd-monday-of-february`). The resolver now computes recurrence keys directly and no longer branches on practice-specific key families.

- **Appendix map section removed in favor of `fixedDates` additive entries (2026-04-14).** Monthly 25th meditations now live in `fixedDates` directly, and the resolver merges fixed-date entries as additional content alongside temporal resolution (deduping identical chapters). The former `virtue/*` set was moved into `reserves` in declared order so it can still be used by fallback rotation without preserving practice-specific key families.

- **Resolve steps now support calendar override from JSON (`resolve[].calendar`) (2026-04-14).** The engine accepts `calendar: "ef" | "of"` on resolve steps and uses it for liturgical label generation instead of user preference when provided. Liguori flow now declares `"calendar": "ef"` in content, so EF behavior is encoded in static JSON rather than implicit runtime state.

- **54-Day Rosary Novena modeled as content-only program in a dedicated library (2026-04-13).** The implementation uses a new `novenas` library that depends on `base` for shared prayer refs, with a single practice `rosary-54-day-novena`. No app runtime changes were needed: `programDay` + `cycle` + `select` in flow JSON were enough to express petition/thanksgiving phases and the traditional Joyful→Sorrowful→Glorious 3-day cadence.

- **Novenas consolidation direction: one library for all novenas (2026-04-13).** We decided `novenas` should become the single destination for novena practices. Existing novenas in `devotions` are planned for migration first, then a broader expansion backlog (Divine Mercy, St. Joseph, St. Therese, Immaculate Conception, Christmas, and additional Marian/saint novenas) will be added in phased content-only batches.

- **Divine Mercy Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `divine-mercy-novena` includes `program` config (`totalDays: 9`, `progressPolicy: continue`, `completionBehavior: offer-restart`), day-indexed cycle data with EN/PT-BR parity, and a flow that combines day-specific intention/petition content with the chaplet structure using existing shared prayer refs from `base`.

- **St. Joseph Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `st-joseph-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR day-by-day parity (`data/days.json`) and a cycle-based flow using standard opening/closing prayers plus a St. Joseph closing invocation.

- **Our Lady of Aparecida Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `our-lady-aparecida-novena` uses the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR parity in `data/days.json`, plus a cycle-based flow with opening and closing prayers in both languages.

- **St. Jude Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `st-jude-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR day-by-day parity in `data/days.json`, plus a standard cycle-based flow with opening/closing prayers and daily intentions.

- **St. Therese Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `st-therese-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR day-by-day parity (`data/days.json`) and a cycle-based flow (opening prayer, day meditation/intention, closing invocation) focused on the Little Way.

- **St. Michael Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `st-michael-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR day-by-day parity (`data/days.json`) and a cycle-based flow using opening/closing prayers with daily meditation and intention.

- **St. Anthony of Padua Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `st-anthony-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR parity in `data/days.json`, plus a standard cycle-based flow with opening/closing prayers and daily intentions.

- **Our Lady of Perpetual Help Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `perpetual-help-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR parity in `data/days.json`, using a standard cycle flow with opening prayer, day meditation/intention, and closing prayers.

- **Our Lady of Guadalupe Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `guadalupe-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR day-by-day parity in `data/days.json`, using the standard novena flow (opening prayer, day meditation/intention, and closing prayers).

- **Our Lady of Mount Carmel Novena added to novenas as a content-only 9-day program (2026-04-13).** New practice ID `mount-carmel-novena` follows the same program policy (`continue` + `offer-restart`) with EN/PT-BR day-by-day parity in `data/days.json`, using the standard novena flow (opening prayer, day meditation/intention, and closing prayers).

---

## Infrastructure

- **Hearth replaces the broken Expo web deploy on GitHub Pages.** The original `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` built and deployed the full Expo web app to GitHub Pages but broke after the monorepo restructure (missing `baseUrl` config). Instead of fixing it, we repurposed GitHub Pages as a static content server called Hearth. It copies Bible, propers, catechism, and saints images to `_site/hearth/v1/`, converts PNGs to WebP, and generates a `manifest.json` with SHA-256 hashes. No npm install or Metro — runs in under a minute. The `v1/` URL prefix allows future breaking changes without breaking old app versions. Base URL: `https://ember.dpgu.me/hearth/`.

- **The Hearth deploy was dead for 47 days and nothing said so (2026-08-05).** Every run of `deploy.yml` failed from 2026-06-19T19:54Z through 2026-08-05, so `ember.dpgu.me/hearth/v2/` served a corpus frozen at mid-June while eleven PRs of content merged to main believing they were live. Cause: #313 ("CCC + Compendium: external books scraped from vatican.va") deleted `scripts/fetch-ccc.ts` — correctly, since the CCC is now scraped from vatican.va per device and `content/catechism/` is no longer generated — but left the workflow's `npx tsx scripts/fetch-ccc.ts` step in place. `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on step 4 of 8, identical on the first failing run and the last. Worth naming the failure mode rather than the typo:
  - **The break didn't land on the commit that caused it.** #313 touched no `content/**` or `apps/hearth/**` path, so the workflow's `paths:` filter meant it never ran on its own merge. The first red run was the *next* push (#314, Mass Times / geohash) — an unrelated PR, on unrelated files, which is exactly the shape that gets dismissed as flaky. Deleting a file a workflow references is a change to the workflow, and `paths:` will happily hide that from you.
  - **The hourly schedule turned one failure into 1,100 identical ones.** `cron: '15 * * * *'` keeps creator-meta fresh, but it also meant ~25 red runs a day, every day, for six weeks. High-frequency red is indistinguishable from background noise; a daily-digest or a failure alert on `schedule` runs is the missing piece, not more logs.
  - **Nothing downstream noticed a stale corpus.** The app fetches Hearth at runtime and a months-old catalog is perfectly valid, so it degrades to "new content silently missing" — no error, no version skew, nothing a test can see. A freshness check (deploy writes a build timestamp; something asserts it's recent) is the only thing that would have caught this within a day.
  - **Fix:** dropped the `Fetch CCC` step, and with it `actions/setup-node` — it existed only to run that one script, so the build is now pure Python + shell (which also clears the Node 20 deprecation warning). Removed the dead `catechism` entry from the aux-dir list in both `deploy.yml` and `scripts/copy-hearth-aux.mjs`. Verified by running the post-CCC steps locally against a clean stage: corpus builds 2,010 items / 51,346 blobs, creator-meta exits 0.

- **App fetches all large content from Hearth on demand.** Bible (DRB), Catechism (CCC), EF Mass propers, and saints images are no longer bundled in the app. They're fetched from Hearth via `apps/app/src/lib/hearth.ts` (thin `fetch()` + SQLite cache wrapper) and cached in the `cache` table. This saves ~63MB from the app bundle. Source files live in `content/` at the repo root. First launch requires network — accepted trade-off. The `@ember/mass-propers` package's `PropersDataSource` interface is fully async to support this.

## Content & Licensing

- **DRB is the only viable bundled English Catholic Bible.** The Douay-Rheims (1749–1750) is fully public domain. Every other major Catholic English translation (NABRE, RSV-CE, NJB, etc.) is under active copyright. Source: `xxruyle/Bible-DouayRheims` on GitHub, MIT license.

- **Bolls.life DRB is incomplete — do NOT use it.** The Bolls.life API's Douay-Rheims translation only has 66 books — all 7 deuterocanonical books are missing. For Catholic use, always use the bundled version from xxruyle. Non-Catholic translations (66 books) work fine but need fallback to bundled DRB for deuterocanonical content.

- **ICEL copyright blocks free OF collects and antiphons.** The Ordinary Form's Collect, Entrance/Communion Antiphons, and other variable prayers are copyrighted by the International Commission on English in the Liturgy. No free API provides them in structured format. This is a hard blocker for complete OF daily Mass propers. The EF has no such problem — everything is available via Missale Meum/Divinum Officium.

- **CCC usage requires Vatican attribution.** The Catechism JSON comes from scraping vatican.va. Attribution required: "Catechism of the Catholic Church, copyright Libreria Editrice Vaticana." Vatican generally permits non-commercial educational use.

- **Divinum Officium hymns use a custom bracket format.** The source files aren't JSON — they use `[SectionName]` brackets. Needs a one-time parse step to convert to JSON. Already done for MVP hymns.

## APIs & Integrations

- **Bolls.life API: free, no auth, generous rate limits.** Base URL: `https://bolls.life`. No documented rate limits, but don't abuse it. Responses cached indefinitely in SQLite since Bible text never changes.

- **EF propers bundled from Divinum Officium, not Missale Meum API.** Missale Meum wraps DO data but only supports English + Polish. We need Portuguese too, so we parse the raw DO files directly. The bracket-based format (`[Introitus]`, `[Oratio]`, etc.) is parsed at build time by `scripts/parse-do-propers.ts` into JSON. Portuguese coverage is partial (207 vs 480 Tempora files) — the app falls back to English via `localizeContent`. Cross-references (`@Tempora/...`, `@Commune/...`) point to `missa/` or `horas/` Commune directories. Prayer macros (`$Per Dominum`) expand from `Ordo/Prayers.txt`. The Commune files for Mass sections live in `horas/{lang}/Commune/` (same files serve both Office and Mass).

- **Evangelizo: OF reading text but with quirks.** `https://feed.evangelizo.org/v2/reader.php?date={YYYYMMDD}&lang={LANG}&type=all` provides full daily reading text. Limitations: response may contain HTML that needs stripping, date range limited to ~30 days from current date, no CORS headers (may need proxy for web), and does NOT provide collects/antiphons.

- **Catholic Readings API: OF calendar + references only.** GitHub Pages hosted (free, MIT). Provides Scripture references (not full text) and liturgical day metadata. The `psalm` field sometimes references non-psalm books (e.g., Jeremiah). Cross-chapter ranges use em-dash (—). Coverage: 2025–2026 data available.

- **Universalis JSONP: potentially has OF propers but unreliable.** Claims to provide full Mass propers via JSONP. Documentation is vague and incomplete. Legacy JavaScript callback pattern doesn't fit React Native well. Worth investigating further but don't count on it.

- **Bible Gateway and API.Bible are not viable.** Bible Gateway discontinued its API and prohibits scraping. API.Bible has FUMS tracking requirements implying online-only usage, unclear Catholic translation availability.

## Technical

- **ProperSlot retries share one OF query key.** Each slot on the Mass screen (Introit, Collect, Antiphon, etc.) has its own `useProperForSlot(slot, 'of')` call, but all slots read from a single `['of-propers', dateKey, language]` query. When a user taps "Try again" on any failed slot, `ofQuery.refetch()` triggers one network request and TanStack Query re-derives every slot's section from the refreshed data — no fan-out, no per-slot request storm. Same approach for EF but each slot has its own key (EF reads from bundled/Hearth propers, independent slots).

- **Global mutation error handler uses TanStack Query's MutationCache, not per-hook wiring.** Across 39+ mutations in 10 features, none had `onError` handlers — failures were silent. Rather than retrofitting each hook, `_layout.tsx` configures `QueryClient({ mutationCache: new MutationCache({ onError }) })` once. The global handler opens `ConfirmSheet` in single-action mode with `i18n.t('error.somethingWrong')` + the error message (or `error.tryAgainLater` fallback). Per-mutation `onError` still wins: the global handler guards with `if (mutation.options.onError) return`. Trade-off: every mutation error now shows a dialog unless explicitly suppressed — acceptable because the fallback is far better than silent failure.

- **The original `getLiturgicalSeason()` was an antiphon scheduler, not a season calculator.** Its boundaries mapped to the Marian antiphon switching dates (Advent→Feb 1, Feb 2→Holy Wednesday, etc.), which don't correspond to actual liturgical seasons. The antiphon schedule is a separate traditional system with its own date ranges — decoupled from seasons in the rewrite.

- **OF and EF seasons share Easter and Advent but diverge everywhere else.** Key differences: EF has Epiphanytide (Jan 14 to Septuagesima) and Septuagesimatide (3 pre-Lent Sundays using violet), neither of which exist in OF. EF has "Time after Pentecost" where OF has "Ordinary Time." Christmas ends at Baptism of the Lord (OF, ~Jan 11) vs Octave of Epiphany (EF, Jan 13). A single `LiturgicalSeason` union with 8 values covers both forms cleanly — each form uses a subset.

- **Year-boundary edge case in season calculation.** Dec 25 is after the start of Advent, so a naive "if date >= Advent start, return advent" incorrectly classifies Christmas Day. The fix: check Christmas (Dec 25+) before checking Advent. Similarly, Jan 1 is still Christmastide in both forms.

- **Divine Office migrated to practice content format.** The custom engine (`divine-office/engine.ts`) and `PrayerFlow` component were replaced by JSON flow files + the shared content engine. Three key infrastructure changes accompanied this: (1) `practice_completions` event log replaces `practice_logs` — supports multiple completions per day and per-hour detail tracking; (2) `reading_tracks` replaces `reading_progress` — named cursors with `id` PK instead of `type` PK, enabling future track sharing between practices; (3) reading tracks are auto-advanced on practice completion whenever `tracks` are defined in the manifest. The `daily_office` and `practice_logs` tables still exist but are deprecated.

- **Unified hours/forms/flow into a single `flows` model.** Three previously separate manifest fields (`flow`, `hours`, `forms`) were replaced by a single `flows: FlowEntry[]` array. Each flow is a named, schedulable prayer sequence. Scheduling (tier, day-of-week) moved from the `Variant` type into `defaults.slots[]` in the manifest — each `SlotDefault` maps a `flowId` to a schedule. The `Variant` type was simplified: `selector`, `schedule`, and `setNames` fields were removed; the engine now receives the active flow ID as `setKeyOverride` in `FlowContext` and picks `variant.data[flowId]` directly (falls back to the first key). This made the Rosary manifest explicit: 4 flows (joyful/sorrowful/glorious/luminous) with day-of-week slots, instead of an opaque `selector: "day-of-week"` schedule inside the variant. Simple practices get a single flow with `id: "default"`.

- **Tamagui sub-theme nesting for liturgical seasons.** Wrapping the app in `<Theme name={season}>` inside `<TamaguiProvider defaultTheme={resolvedTheme}>` causes Tamagui to resolve `light` + `advent` to the registered `light_advent` theme automatically. This means any component reading `$accent` gets the seasonal color with zero changes to those components. Important: `useThemeName()` now returns the sub-theme name (e.g., `'dark_lent'`), not just `'dark'` — any code checking theme name equality must use `.startsWith('dark')` instead of `=== 'dark'`.

- **Backward-compatible wrappers for reading tracks.** `getReadingProgressByType('ot')` maps to `getReadingTrack('default-ot')` so existing consumers (settings screens, reading progress hooks) work without changes. The `ReadingTrack` type is a superset of `ReadingProgress` (adds `id` and `label`).

- **Typography is a "Ladder of Reverence", and the app is all-serif.** The old system had four font families (`$display`/`$heading`/`$body`/`$script`) each doing 2–4 jobs, so type didn't separate interface from reading from prayer. The rebuild maps the families onto seven semantic rungs (interface → rubric → apparatus → reading → prayer → label → sacred-title → peak), expressed as the `variant` prop of a single `Typography` component in `components/typography/` (see the consolidation note below). One added typeface: a medievalist **`$title`** (Junicode/IM FELL) for sacred titles. **A quiet sans (Alegreya Sans) was tried for the interface rung and removed** — it "sucked the soul out", reading as generic-app chrome against the manuscript character. So chrome stays EB Garamond and recedes via size/weight/color/case, never a sans (`variant="interface"` = quiet `$body`). The discipline that prevents a repeat of the old `$script` overuse: default to the lowest adequate rung; one rung-7 peak per screen; gold/burgundy are *sacred-only* color signals, UI stays neutral; never set raw `fontFamily`/`fontSize`. See `docs/design/design-system.md` § Typography and `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` § Typography.

- **Junicode is not on Google Fonts — bundle the OFL TTFs locally.** IM FELL English ships as an `@expo-google-fonts/*` package, but Junicode has no such package; the full normal-width family (Light→Bold + italics, from the `psb1558/Junicode-font` v2.224 release) is committed under `apps/app/assets/fonts/` and loaded via `require(...)` in `_layout.tsx`, same as `UnifrakturMaguntia`. (Historical: the two medievalist faces once both loaded every boot behind `flags.sacredTitleFace` for live A/B — see the resolution below.)

- **`$title` A/B resolved: Junicode wins; IM FELL English removed entirely.** After comparing on real screens, Junicode was kept — warmer, and it carries the full weight range (IM FELL ships only 400 + italic, so every other weight flattened). Dropped the `@expo-google-fonts/im-fell-english` dependency, the `sacredTitleFace` flag, and the dual-wiring in `fonts.ts`; `$title` now points straight at Junicode. The A/B-via-flag pattern was cheap and worth repeating for future face decisions.

- **The Ladder is one `Typography` component with a `variant` prop, not N intent-named components.** The first build gave each rung its own export (`UiText`, `Rubric`, `SacredTitle`, `SectionLabel`, `CeremonialMark`, …). That was reconsidered: the rungs are *roles* but their differences are mostly font/size/color/case, which is exactly what a Tamagui `variants` block expresses — so the whole ladder collapsed into one `styled(Text)` whose `variant` is the rung (`interface` default, `rubric`, `annotation`/`reference`/`verse-number`/`caption`/`whisper`, `label`/`marker`, `sacred-title`, `ceremonial`/`drop-cap`, plus `screen-title`). One orthogonal `tone` variant (`default`/`muted`); everything else is a pass-through `Text` prop. The apparatus rung is where the old N-component approach was most bloated (5 near-identical exports). Two deliberate carve-outs stay separate: **`PrayerText`/`PrayerLines`** (rungs 3–4) because they call `useReadingStyle` — a styled component can't run hooks — and the thin prayer-domain wrappers `SectionHeading`/`SectionMarker`, which now just render `<Typography variant="label|marker">`. Gotcha re-confirmed: `styled(Text)` infers the `fontSize` type from the default `$body` font (tokens 1–5), so hero/peak sizes inside variants must be **numeric** (`fontSize: 48`/`46`), and call sites needing `$6`/`$7` (oversized `$title`/`$display` tokens) keep a `'$6' as any` cast.

- **Reading leading must be a ratio, not absolute px.** `useReadingStyle`'s line-height was an absolute-px array indexed independently of font size, so at large font sizes it collapsed toward ~1.1 (cramped). It now selects a *ratio* (`readingScale.leadingRatio`, default ≈ 1.5) applied as `round(fontSize × ratio)`, so leading scales with size. `preferencesStore` steps stay 1–7 (no migration; the persisted integer just selects a ratio bucket). The book-reader WebView already derived a unitless ratio (`bookReader.ts buildConfigCss`), so only the native path changed. `useReadingMaxWidth()` adds a ~34em measure cap consumed by reading *containers* (not per `<Text>`, which would fight flex / bilingual side-by-side).

- **RN `<Text>` won't switch face on inherited weight/style.** With `fontFamily` pinned, a nested `fontWeight="700"` doesn't pick the bold face — you must resolve the concrete face name from the font's `face` map (see `ProseBlock` `emphasisStyle`). Only EB Garamond ships bold/italic faces; the other user-selectable reading fonts load Regular only, so non-EB fonts fall back to synthetic weight/style. Also: a `styled(Text)`'s `fontSize` token type is inferred from the *default* `$body` font (tokens $1–$5) regardless of its own `fontFamily`, so `$6`/`$7` sizes on `$title`/`$display` components need a numeric value or a cast.

- **Rubrics restyled from section headers to Missal-style italic.** The `RubricLabel` component was originally uppercase, letter-spaced Cinzel text — functioning as a section header. Real Missal rubrics are italic, sentence case, in red (hence "rubric" from Latin *rubrica*). The component was restyled to italic body font with accent color. All 40+ flows that used rubrics as section labels ("Opening Verse", "Hymn", "Psalmody") were migrated to `subheading` type instead. Mass flows received comprehensive rubrics at every major gesture (genuflections, bows, signs of the cross, hand positions) following the 1962 Missale Romanum (EF) and GIRM (OF).

- **Mass view modes use grouped flows, not engine filtering.** We considered two approaches for Propers/Readings views: (1) a single flow with engine-level section filtering by `viewMode` in `FlowContext`, or (2) separate flow JSON files with a `group` field on `FlowEntry` linking sub-flows to their parent. We chose (2) because it's zero engine complexity — just JSON content + a `group` field. The `ViewModeSelector` component renders a segmented control inside the pray screen; switching modes swaps the loaded flow via `useState` without navigation. The `FlowButtons` component on the detail screen filters out grouped flows so only primary flows appear as entry points.

- **OF propers: Liturgia Diária API gives complete PT-BR propers, Evangelizo gives EN readings only.** The `liturgia.up.railway.app/v2/` API (from `Dancrf/liturgia-diaria` on GitHub) provides all OF Mass propers in Portuguese — collects, prayers, antiphons, and readings with full text. Found via `servusdei2018/awesome-catholic`. For English, only readings are available (Evangelizo) — ICEL copyright blocks English collects/antiphons. Universalis was investigated thoroughly and confirmed to only serve readings despite vague docs. See `docs/content/of-propers-research.md` for full research.

- **Evangelizo has no psalm response/refrain.** The API returns only the psalm verses — the responsorial refrain (e.g., "Keep me, O God, for in you I take refuge") is not included. The Liturgia Diária API provides this for PT-BR via the `refrao` field, but no structured English source has been found. This is a known English data gap.

- **Evangelizo response format is HTML, not JSON.** The `feed.evangelizo.org` API returns HTML with `<br />` tags, `&quot;` entities, and `<font>` tags for citations. Sections are separated by triple `<br />` tags. Needs HTML stripping and section detection based on content (Psalms → psalm slot, Gospel → gospel slot, etc.).

- **Proper form must come from flow JSON, not user preferences.** A user may prefer the EF liturgical calendar but still be viewing the OF Mass practice. The `form` field on `proper` sections in flow JSON determines which data source is used. This is passed through the content engine to the `ProperSlot` component.

- **EF Mass propers: Tempora/Sancti precedence must use the liturgical calendar.** A naive merge (Sancti overlays Tempora) is wrong — on Holy Thursday 2026, St. Francis of Paola (Sancti `04-02`, III class) was overriding the Triduum epistle with an empty `Lectio`. The fix: use `DayCalendar.principal` from `buildYearCalendar()` to determine which source provides the Mass. If the principal's `category` is temporal (`solemnity_temporal`, `feast_of_the_lord`, `liturgical_season`) → use Tempora propers. If it's a saint category → use Sancti propers. Never merge both. Fallback without a calendar: Tempora-only (safe default — missing a saint is less wrong than overriding the Triduum). Future: commemorated saints should contribute only their Collect (Oratio) to the Mass of the day.

- **Friday abstinence rules differ significantly by jurisdiction.** Universal canon law (Canon 1251) requires meat abstinence on ALL Fridays year-round. However, the USCCB (1966 Pastoral Statement) replaced obligatory Friday abstinence outside Lent with a general penance obligation (any form). The CNBB (Brazil) kept year-round Friday abstinence but allows substitution with charity or other food — subtly different from the US approach. EF (1962 rubrics) maintains complete abstinence on all Fridays plus vigil days. Modeled as `AbstinenceLevel: 'full' | 'partial' | 'penance-required' | 'none'` in `packages/liturgical/src/obligations.ts`.

- **Books are raw HTML/markdown chapters inside `.pray`, not EPUB files.** The original plan was to embed `.epub` files in `.pray` archives and use epub.js or Readium for rendering. This was simplified: book chapters are raw `.html` or `.md` files bundled directly in the `.pray` zip under `books/{bookId}/{lang}/`. The app reads them from disk via `expo-file-system` and renders in a WebView with CSS column pagination. Markdown files (`.md`) are converted at runtime using `marked` + `marked-footnote` (~45KB). This eliminated the pandoc build dependency, JSZip from the reader path, and the double-unzip problem (.pray ZIP containing .epub ZIP). Content structure: `content/libraries/{libraryId}/books/{bookId}/{lang}/`.

- **Ember days in calendar-data.ts are placeholders, not properly resolved.** The `ember-days` entry has a dummy fixed date (Jan 1) and `ember-days-advent` only resolves to the Advent Ember Wednesday. The obligations module computes all 12 ember days (4 seasons × Wed/Fri/Sat) from scratch using anchor dates. Future: consider moving ember day resolution into the calendar builder.

- **Practice-flow readings and psalmody surface inline retry on fetch failure (2026-04-18).** `BibleReadingBlock` / `CccReadingBlock` return `undefined` when data is missing, and `usePsalmsForHour` previously filtered to successful results, so an errored Bible / CCC / psalm fetch disappeared silently from the composed practice flow (no empty, no error — just a gap). Fixes: `PracticeFlow` tracks `bibleErrors` / `cccErrors` maps of `queryKey → refetch()` alongside the data maps and renders a small inline retry card at the reading's position; `usePsalmsForHour` now returns `slots: PsalmSlot[]` (per-ref `{verses?, retry?}`) and `PsalmodyBlock` renders the same retry at each failed psalm's position. `isDynamicLoading` already falls through on errors (it only checks `isLoading`), so the inline state is reached without extra gating. Reused existing `common.couldntLoad` / `common.retry` strings (same keys used by `ReaderErrorState`) via a shared `InlineRetry` component — the full-screen `ReaderErrorState` uses `flex={1}` and wouldn't compose inside a section list, so `InlineRetry` is a separate, slim variant.

- **Base library expanded with catechetical chapters — creeds, commandments, sacraments, virtues (2026-04-19).** The `base` library (previously named `ember-default`) was broadened from "prayers + practices" to "the essentials of the Christian life." Added 8 reference chapters: Ten Commandments, Beatitudes, Precepts of the Church, Seven Sacraments, Corporal/Spiritual Works of Mercy, Virtues (theological + cardinal), Capital Sins and Opposing Virtues, Gifts and Fruits of the Holy Spirit. Also added the Nicene-Constantinopolitan Creed (EN/PT-BR/Latin) alongside the Apostles' Creed. Design choices: (1) each chapter is a short prose reference — not a practice — following the `sacred-heart/chapters/twelve-promises` pattern (3–4 sections: intro → body → optional second body, divided by simple line); (2) estimated minutes kept to 7–8, aimed at a single reading; (3) tone is catechetical without being encyclopedic — formative, quotes CCC and Scripture only when it adds weight; (4) no flow DSL required (chapters are static prose). Concurrently renamed all `ember-*` libraries to unbranded names (`ember-default`→`base`, `ember-extra`→`devotions`, `ember-novenas`→`novenas`) — branding is owned by the app, not by content.

- **Base library formation expansion — opinionated chapters on how to pray (2026-04-19).** Extended the `base` library beyond "what Catholics believe" with 7 formation chapters on *how* to live the interior life: **How to Pray** (CCC 2559 / forms / expressions / distractions), **Lectio Divina** (Guigo II's four rungs), **The Ignatian Examen** (five-step method from the Spiritual Exercises), **Mental Prayer — Carmelite Method** (drawing on Fr. Gabriel of St. Mary Magdalen's *Divine Intimacy*: presence, subject, consider, affection, colloquy, resolution), **How to Pray the Rosary Well** (JPII's *Rosarium Virginis Mariae*, Ignatian composition of place, per-mystery fruit tables), **How to Make a Good Confession** (preparation via commandments/beatitudes/capital-sins examination, full Act of Contrition in EN/PT, Escrivá's "soul's shower" frequency), and **A Plan of Life** (Benedict / Francis de Sales / Escrivá, daily + weekly + monthly + yearly norms, references the app's Plan of Life feature). Design choices: (1) opinionated — cites specific saints (Teresa, John of the Cross, Ignatius, Francis de Sales, Curé d'Ars, Padre Pio, Thérèse, Brother Lawrence) and traditions (Ignatian, Carmelite) rather than generic pan-devotional language; (2) each chapter is 4–5 prose sections with an estimated 8–9 min read; (3) CCC and Scripture cited only when they carry the weight better than paraphrase; (4) chapters cross-reference one another (Plan of Life points to Examen / Rosary / Lectio / Mental Prayer chapters) — the library begins to function as a coherent formation curriculum, not a grab-bag. Added 8 standard prayers to complete the essentials: *Memorare, Angele Dei, Adoro Te Devote, Tantum Ergo, Te Deum, Veni Creator Spiritus, De Profundis, Eternal Rest* — each with EN/PT-BR/Latin. Rosary art enhancement (stunning per-mystery images) deferred as a follow-up since image sourcing isn't feasible in this session. Stats: 41 practices, 33 prayers, 15 chapters.

- **Base library: Chaplet of Divine Mercy + Stations of the Cross (2026-04-19).** Two major Catholic practices the `base` library was still missing, both added as first-class flows using the existing DSL. Chaplet of Divine Mercy: 5 decades of `Eternal Father` + 10× `divine-mercy-response`, closing with 3× `holy-god`, 10-min estimate, opt-in daily slot. Stations of the Cross: 14 stations in a `repeat from: stations` block with per-station name + meditation data entries, traditional Alphonsus Liguori meditations rewritten in modern English/Portuguese (compunction-forward, no archaic thees/thous when avoidable), versicle/response at each station, closing prayers for the Pope's intentions (plenary indulgence custom). Opt-in weekly Friday slot. All building-block prayers (`eternal-father`, `divine-mercy-response`, `holy-god`) already existed in `base/prayers/`. Stats: 43 practices, 33 prayers, 15 chapters.

- **Base library: Holy Hour practice (2026-04-19).** Added a scaffold for the traditional 60-minute Eucharistic Hora Santa. Structured as six unequal movements — Approach, Adoration, Lectio, Contrition, Intercession, Silence, Offering — rather than a rigid minute-by-minute script. Drawn from Fulton Sheen's pattern and the Margaret Mary / Sacred Heart Thursday-night devotion. Uses existing prayer refs (`adoro-te-devote`, `anima-christi`, `act-of-contrition`, `fatima-prayer`, `suscipe`, `sub-tuum-praesidium`) and cross-references the *Lectio Divina* chapter. Opt-in weekly Thursday slot. The practice is intentionally light on words — the meditation sections prompt silence rather than fill it, consistent with Sheen's rule. Stats: 44 practices, 33 prayers, 15 chapters.

- **Home IA: Rule of Life leads; companions/library collapsed into Explore (2026-04-18).** The home screen had grown to eight ShortcutRows (Oratio/Kyrie/Examen/Intentions/Gratias/Memoria/Catechism/Saints) sitting *below* the Plan of Life, visually competing with Fidelity as the primary pillar. Restructure: lift the Rule of Life block above liturgical context, collapse the three "companions" (Oratio/Kyrie/Examen) and the two "library" surfaces (Catechism/Saints) into a single `/explore` destination with grouped sections, and surface Intentions/Gratias/Memoria as a compact horizontal chip row (`QuickCaptureChips`) below the GreenWall. The duplicate Reading tile in `AppShortcuts` (it pointed to `/bible`, same as the Bible tile) was repurposed to route to `/explore`. Net effect: one screen, one pillar up front, ambient quick-captures below, tile nav for the rest. Non-obvious bit — `PageHeader` accepts only `{ title }`, so the Explore subtitle is a separate italic `<Text>` stacked under it inside a wrapping YStack.

- **Spiritual checkup seed content authored as library JSON (2026-04-19, #156).** The checkup is content, not app code. Lives at `content/libraries/base/checkup/` alongside the rest of the `base` library so it ships in the same `.pray` archive. Three files: `archetypes.json` (6 archetypes loosely mapped to the Three Ways — Quaerens, Redivivus, Discipulus, Proficiens, Perseverans, Contemplativus), `questions.json` (9-question intake, Q9 non-scoring, per-option `weights: [Q,R,D,P,S,C]` vector, plus consent screen, tie-breakers, and UI labels), `tracks.json` (per-archetype starter tracks — essentialPractices / idealPractices / extraPractices arrays of existing practice IDs, weeklyFormation with chapter or book or cccRange or scripture refs, milestones, closingPrompt). All bilingual en-US + pt-BR; Latin preserved in archetype names only. The `tracks.json` flags four classical books — *Introduction to the Devout Life*, *Way of Perfection*, *Imitation of Christ*, *Ascent of Mount Carmel* — with `importCandidate: true` so the Content Platform track knows what the checkup depends on. `library.json` deliberately not extended with a `checkup` field — the app reader will load by path convention when the feature is implemented, and the content ships automatically because `build-libraries.sh` zips the entire library dir. PT-BR descriptors drafted deliberately warm and non-clinical (*etapa*, *momento*, *caminho* — never *perfil*, *tipo*, *categoria*) to resist personality-quiz framing. Stats: base library still 44 practices / 33 prayers / 15 chapters — seed adds 3 checkup JSONs.

- **Base library: four more core prayers (2026-04-19).** Added standalone prayer files for `prayer-of-st-francis` (Peace Prayer — universally known though only attributed to St. Francis since 1912; honest title), `prayer-before-crucifix` (*En ego, O bone et dulcissime Iesu* — plenary-indulgence prayer traditionally prayed before a crucifix, Fridays of Lent especially), `o-salutaris-hostia` (Benediction companion to `tantum-ergo`, already present), and `regina-caeli` (standalone — extracted from the inline in the `regina-caeli` practice, which was updated to use a `ref`). Side-effect: fixed a typo in the former inline (`Qia` → `Quia`) and normalized capitalisation to sentence case. Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 15 chapters.

- **Base library: "Returning to Confession" chapter (2026-04-19, #156).** Authored a bilingual formation chapter specifically for the long-lapsed Catholic — not a duplicate of `how-to-make-a-good-confession` (which assumes a practicing Catholic), but a complementary companion for the checkup's **Redivivus** track (largest audience segment). Five sections, ~7 min: (1) *The Father is Running* — Prodigal Son framing, interrupts the rehearsed speech; (2) *What Keeps Us Away* — three named fears (forgetting sins, priest's shock, forgetting the form) each addressed concretely; (3) *What the Priest Will Do* — practical reassurance including "if the priest is harsh, find another" and the plain fact that absolution is actual forgiveness, not hopeful; (4) *A Simple Path* — 9-step script including the exact opening line with gap-acknowledgement; (5) *After the First Confession* — **do not pile on devotions**; three sustainable habits (Sunday Mass, short daily prayer, short examen) + return-to-confession deadline of one month. Tracks.json Redivivus week-1 formation updated to point at this chapter instead of `how-to-make-a-good-confession` (which remains in the library for practicing Catholics). Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 16 chapters.

- **Base library: "What the Mass Is" formation chapter (2026-04-19).** A large (~12 min) bilingual chapter on the central act of Catholic life, serving every archetype of the spiritual checkup: Quaerens needs it before returning, Redivivus after years away, Discipulus for deeper participation, Perseverans for perspective on dryness. Six sections: (1) *Source and Summit* — LG §11 / LG §3 framing, Mass as the hinge of everything; (2) *The Sacrifice* — transubstantiation as re-presentation, Trent Session 22 ch. 2 on same Priest / same Victim; (3) *Real Presence* — John 6 "hard saying", Ignatius of Antioch (*Smyrn.* 7), transubstantiation defined; (4) *The Shape* — four-part structure (Introductory / Word / Eucharist / Concluding) unchanged since Justin Martyr c. 150; (5) *Interior Participation* — Sacrosanctum Concilium §14 "full, conscious, and active" re-centered on *conscious*, Pius X's original meaning (pray the Mass, don't just watch it), three concrete disciplines; (6) *When You Feel Nothing* — Thérèse on dry Communion, "the Mass does not require your feelings to work," practical dryness counsel. Sources cited: Vatican II (LG, SC), Trent (Session 22), Catechism (§§1373–1381), Hebrews, John 6, St. Ignatius of Antioch, St. Justin Martyr, St. Thérèse. Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 17 chapters.

- **Base library: "The Predominant Fault" chapter (2026-04-19).** Classical spiritual-life concept cited across Discipulus, Proficiens, and Perseverans tracks as a milestone — now a first-class formation chapter. Five sections, ~8 min: (1) *What It Is* — de Sales (*Introduction to the Devout Life* III, 21) on the one ruling passion; (2) *Why It Matters* — Scupoli/Fénelon "strike the root, not the leaves", St. Francis de Sales on the counter-virtue; (3) *How to Find It* — five examen questions + concrete-sentence over labels + confirm with a priest; (4) *How to Fight It* — Ignatian particular examen, counter-virtue, frequent confession naming the pattern, mental prayer that touches the wound; (5) *When It Hides* — hiding under virtue, shifting after surrender, returning in new forms across life-stages, Beatitudes as the aim. Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 18 chapters.

- **Spiritual checkup #156 redesigned from flat weighted-sum to decision tree (2026-04-19).** The v1 nine-question intake (`content/libraries/base/checkup/questions.json`) summed per-option `[Q,R,D,P,S,C]` weight vectors to produce primary and secondary archetypes. Pastoral defect: souls with opposite topologies score adjacent. An *apologetics nerd* who knows the Catechism cold but never prays picks "weekly Mass / familiar with scripture / basics known" and lands in Discipulus or Proficiens — same region as a soul praying the Rosary daily with a steady sacramental rhythm. Their starter-track needs are opposite. Same failure on the other end: complete catechumens and long-lapsed cradle Catholics both land in Quaerens/Redivivus despite needing very different first weeks. Redesign: a decision tree that routes early on orthogonal axes — sacramental status, doctrinal formation, prayer life depth, moral posture, interior state — and arrives at **profiles** (archetype instantiated by topology) rather than at bare archetypes. Four levels: consent → sacramental status (widest split) → depth/imbalance → refining axis → tension pair. Three yes/no overlays (scrupulosity, convert, traditional-leaning) modify any profile without changing routing. Primary/secondary retained, but secondary is now chosen from a leaf-authored tension pair via a single question ("When you imagine growing from here, which resonates more — [X] or [Y]?") rather than from top-two weight sums. Authored 9 profile docs + 3 overlay docs + README + tree sketch under `docs/features/spiritual-checkup-profiles/`: Curious Unbaptized, Nominal Catholic, Lapsed Returner, Apologetics Nerd, Emotional Enthusiast, Sacramental Baseline, Committed Practitioner, Hidden Dark Night, Well-Formed Seeking Wellspring. No app code — `questions.json` remains as a fallback, the six archetypes and `tracks.json` are retained as-is. Each profile flags candidate chapters still to author (e.g., "When Prayer Goes Dark" for the Hidden Dark Night profile, "Feeling and Faith" for the Emotional Enthusiast, "The Book and the Silence" for the Apologetics Nerd). The design principle that emerged and should govern future work: **shrinking the next step is a feature** — for a catechumen, "pray the Our Father slowly each morning" is plenty, and piling more on is the app's failure mode, not the user's.

- **Base library: "When Prayer Goes Dark" chapter (2026-04-19, #156).** The chapter flagged as highest-leverage in the spiritual-checkup profiles redesign (referenced by profiles 07 Committed Practitioner and 08 Hidden Dark Night). Addresses the single most dangerous pattern an app can reinforce: souls in ordinary dryness diagnosing themselves into the Dark Night of the Senses and prescribing themselves a contemplative regime they are not ready for. Five sections, ~10 min: (1) *What Dryness Is* — ordinary, 2000 years old, named by the Fathers (acedia), Carmelites, and English spiritual writers; three things the tradition says early: not a sign of fault, not a sign of divine withdrawal, not by itself mystical; (2) *The Three Signs* — St. John of the Cross's three signs from *Ascent* II.13 and *Dark Night* I.9 laid out plainly (no pleasure in God *or any created thing*; painful anxious memory of God; can no longer meditate or imagine even when placed deliberately), with St. John's own warning that the one in the state is usually the last to know and the name *Dark Night* is too large to claim; (3) *What Not to Do* — four refusals (don't add penance, don't diagnose yourself, don't parish-shop for felt warmth, don't stop) plus a subtler refusal (don't blame surroundings); (4) *What to Do* — five things (find a director and make it the week's task, reduce not add, pray the Psalter starting with lament psalms, pray Compline / Lauds / Vespers, before silent prayer open Gospel and set the crucifix without reading) + a note that Mass does not require feelings to work; (5) *Grace Through Nature* — a medical section that is the honest mirror of the interior section: fatigue / sleep / loss of interest / sustained sadness / thoughts of self-harm are reasons to see a doctor alongside the spiritual work. Closes with Christ's cry on the cross as the First Word of the dry phase — the soul praying those words in dryness is praying with Him even when she cannot tell. Cites St. John of the Cross, Teresa of Ávila, St. Thomas on bodily remedies, Scripture (Pss 5, 6, 22, 26, 42, 63, 130, 138; Lam 3; Prov 12; 1 Sam 15). Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 19 chapters.

- **Foliate paginator has no public `index` getter — and `setStyles` DOES exist (2026-06-11, post-#266 fixes).** Two long-standing wrong assumptions in `bootstrap.raw.js` caused the reader to jump to the start of the book on *every* settings change (font, palette, flow toggle, rotation): `setConfig` restored position via `paginator.index`, but the vendored paginator only has private `#index` (always `undefined → ?? 0 → chapter 0`); and a comment claimed "foliate-paginator has no setStyles" when `setStyles()` is a first-class API (`#display` wires per-doc `<style>` elements into a `WeakMap` on every section load). The rewrite never reopens the book on config change: set the `margin`/`flow` attributes (the `flow` attributeChangedCallback triggers `render()` which re-anchors to the last visible Range — foliate preserves position natively), call `paginator.setStyles(buildStyle(cfg))` for the live chapter, and drop cached blob URLs so future section loads re-blob with the new CSS. Trap for any future "fix": `goTo` to the *current* index takes the `index === #index` path which skips the section reload — naive index-tracking would silently stop style changes from applying.

- **Scrolled-mode fraction is normalized over the scrollable range, not content height (2026-06-11).** Foliate's scrolled relocate fraction is `scrollTop/contentHeight`, which maxes at `1 − screen/chapterHeight` — a chapter shorter than ~20 screens could never reach the 0.95 completion threshold. `postRelocate` now reports `scrollTop/(contentHeight − screen)` (clamped; `1` for single-screen chapters), and the restore paths (`openBook`, `__foliate.goTo`) invert it via **function anchors** — foliate evaluates `anchor(doc)` after the section loads, which is the only moment `viewSize` is known. Page counts are also mode-aware: the two sentinel pages (`pages − 2`) exist only in paginated layout. Chapter nav in scrolled mode is over-scroll: a swipe that *starts* while already pinned at the chapter boundary (same `start`/`viewSize − end` checks as foliate's own `#scrollPrev/#scrollNext`) triggers `paginator.prev()/next()`, making the advance a deliberate second gesture.

- **Base library: "Feeling and Faith" chapter (2026-04-19, #156).** Second highest-leverage chapter from the profile redesign — flagged explicitly by profile 05 Emotional Enthusiast (needs consolation/desolation framework) and profile 08 Hidden Dark Night (needs discernment language to distinguish dryness from desolation from abandonment). Universal reach: every profile except perhaps the bare catechumen will cross this terrain. Five sections, ~10 min: (1) *The Names* — consolation and desolation in Ignatius's technical sense (movements *with* a vector, toward/away from God), distinguished from moods and caffeine; Christ's Gethsemane cry as the charter against feeling-as-faith equation; (2) *Why Feelings Mislead* — three reasons the tradition is careful: same feeling has many sources, the enemy imitates consolation (only the trail reveals it), God also sends dryness that is not desolation; ends on the Carmelite/Jesuit shared rule "trust the direction, not the weight"; (3) *Rules in Consolation* — four movements (receive with thanks and without hoarding; use the light to see what you could not see in the dark — make decisions not promises; prepare for the desolation that will follow; do not tell everyone) + note on "natural" consolations that are good but not themselves grace; (4) *Rules in Desolation* — the first-and-last rule "never change course in desolation" given with Ignatian bluntness, then four more (intensify quietly, remember it will pass, examine gently, read Psalms of lament and the Passion) + explicit list of things *not* to do (no big decisions, no changing director, no abandoning committed devotions) + closing that desolation is not failure but a station every serious soul passes through; (5) *What Asks for Action* — taxonomy of four questions to bring to any interior movement (toward or away from duties of state of life? toward or away from the cross? survives prayer? bears the test of the director?) + the observation that mature inspirations are almost always small and specific, not grand; closes with a carve-out for the scrupulous: they need *less* discernment not more, and obedience to a confessor is always the right answer. Sources: Spiritual Exercises rules §313–336 (both sets — lay and proficient), Lallemant's *Spiritual Doctrine*, St. Teresa's *Way of Perfection* ch. 20, de Sales *Treatise on the Love of God* IX; Scripture (Mt 26:38; Jas 1:17; Heb 11:1; Ps 39; 1 Sam 3). Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 20 chapters.

- **Base library: "The Book and the Silence" chapter (2026-04-19, #156).** Third chapter from the profile redesign — the dedicated diagnosis for profile 04 Apologetics Nerd, the soul well-formed doctrinally but undeveloped in prayer. This profile is large and growing in our moment (catechetical collapse → adults formed mostly by online apologetics → strong on argument, weak on adoration). Four sections, ~8 min: (1) *The Student and the Pray-er* — St. Thomas's December 1273 "straw" moment as icon of the passage from scientia to sapientia; every age produces these souls, ours by the thousand; St. Bernard on kinds of seekers; (2) *Why It Goes This Way* — names the modern pipeline honestly: catechesis thin so adults formed by apologetics (argumentative genre, not prayer formation), study gives measurable returns and prayer does not, doctrine is easier to love than God is, subtle pride in being the-one-who-knows; (3) *Prayer of the Full Mind* — four dispositions (come empty-handed; shorten the material — one verse is often too much; refuse the reflex to explain; accept feeling you're wasting time) integrated by lectio divina as Guigo the Carthusian formalized it (lectio / meditatio / oratio / contemplatio), 15-20 min enough; (4) *Learning Not to Instruct* — the conversion from instruction to adoration; what changes (mouth slows, reading shifts toward ascetical shelves, silence becomes bearable, argumentative posture softens everywhere); explicit warning against over-correction by burning the apologetics — the cure for imbalance is not opposite imbalance but balance. Closes with John 11:28: "Magister adest, et vocat te." Sources: St. Thomas (Tocco's life), St. Bernard (*De Consideratione*), Guigo II (*Scala Claustralium*), Augustine, de Sales, Scupoli; Scripture (1 Cor 1, 8; Ps 45; Jn 11). Stats: 44 practices, 37 prayers, 21 chapters; base-1.0.0.pray now 473K.

- **Base library: imported Aquinas's *Catechetical Instructions* (2026-04-25).** First Aquinas import and the first full-length book in `base`. The 1939 Collins translation (English) of Aquinas's catechetical *Opuscula* — sermons preached at Naples in Lent 1273 on the Apostles' Creed, the Ten Commandments, the Lord's Prayer, and the Hail Mary. 40 chapter `.md` files at `content/libraries/base/books/catechetical-instructions/en-US/`, hierarchical TOC under four parents. Source PDF (137 pages, text-based) from The Catholic Primer (2004) — underlying translation public domain. Skipped Bandas's Introduction and the Index of Key Terms per scope; included Translator's Preface and the back-matter Questions for Discussion. Articles 1 and 5 of the Creed each have a "(continued)" sub-article in Collins's translation with footnote numbering resetting at 1, so they were split into `creed-01a/01b` and `creed-05a/05b`. Word counts: 70,260 words across the 40 chapters vs. 73,909 words in the source `.txt` (the gap is the skipped front matter). One-time script `scripts/parse-aquinas-catechetical.py` does the conversion — kept around as a forensic record but not maintained; manual edits to the chapter files are the source of truth from here on. Footnote audit after parsing: 35/40 chapters have refs and defs balanced; 10 chapters carry source-defect numbering glitches from the 1939 transcription (orphan endnote definitions for unreferenced numbers, or vice versa — all baked into the OCR and not fixable without editorializing). The Seventh Petition and the Short Explanation share a single endnotes block in the source; the script splits notes 1–15 to the Petition and 16–17 to the Explanation by intersecting with each chapter's body refs. One OCR-merged note (`creed-05b` note 11 had two Bible refs welded together as `Acts, ii. 3~. Rom., vi, 4.`) is recovered via a hand-coded override for that specific note. base-1.0.0.pray grew from ~15 MB to 16.3 MB (further increased to ~19 MB after the Pius-X catechism import landed alongside).

- **PDF book imports — extraction technique that actually works (2026-04-25).** The `scripts/parse-aquinas-catechetical.py` workflow is reusable for any text-based (non-scanned) Catholic-classic PDF. Three quirks worth knowing:
  - **Hybrid extraction.** `pdftotext -bbox-layout` emits XHTML where each `<block>` is a paragraph — *the* signal `pdftotext -raw` and default mode both lose. Use `-bbox-layout` for body, but `-raw` for any 2-column endnote section: `-bbox-layout` reads the columns interleaved (1, 11, 2, 13, 3, 14, …) while `-raw` linearizes them top-down per column (1, 2, 3, …, 17). Parsing endnotes off the bbox output and sorting by N is also workable but loses multi-line note continuity.
  - **Form feeds glue page numbers to text.** `pdftotext -raw` joins page-break footers to the previous token via `\x0c` — `ENDNOTES66` (note 66) and `Penance.66` style glitches. Strip `\x0c` from raw text before any line-based regex.
  - **Python 3.14 ships with a broken pyexpat on this machine** (`Symbol not found: _XML_SetAllocTrackerActivationThreshold`); ElementTree raises on import. Use `python3.13` for any XML parsing in import scripts. The shebang `#!/usr/bin/env python3.13` is the safe default for poppler-XML pipelines.
  - **PDF heading detection by ALL-CAPS works for ~80% of cases.** Inline sub-section headings buried mid-block (e.g. `"HAIL MARY"` and `"FULL OF GRACE"` quoted ALL-CAPS subheaders inside the Hail Mary chapter) survive as inline quoted phrases. They're readable but not indexed as `##`. Acceptable for v1; revisit if a future book has many of them.
  - **Scholarly editions can mix footnote conventions.** Collins's body uses `[N]` brackets; his Translator's Preface uses bare-superscript style that pdftotext renders as digits attached to a year (`year 1225.1 The` for `year 1225.[1]`). One regex (`\b\d{4}[.,]\d{1,2}\s+[A-Za-z]`) caught both instances in this PDF.

- **Catechism in Pictures imported (2026-05-17, fr-FR + en-US + pt-BR).** Maison de la Bonne Presse, 1908 (French canonical) / 1912 (English translation by E. E. Fernandez) / Brazilian Portuguese rendered fresh from the French. Source: https://missiondesainteanne.wordpress.com/cat-4a/. 68 plates split across 4 parts (Creed, Sacraments, Commandments, Prayer/Last Things/Sin/Virtues/Mercy). Plate 4 is sub-divided into 4.0 (Transfiguration), 4.1 (Annunciation), 4.2 (Nativity) — handled with composite IDs `plate-04-0/1/2`. Source files: clean transcribed DOCX for English (pdftotext gave nothing useful from the scan-based 1938 reprint, but the DOCX has every paragraph as its own `<w:p>`); pdftotext **with `-layout`** for the 1908 French PDF, which already has a clean OCR text layer (page-form-feed marker preserved page boundaries — picture-only pages produce zero-content pages, giving a clean 1-page gap between every plate's 2-page text block, which is how the splitter detects plate boundaries). Portuguese (Lisbon 1910 *Catecismo Illustrado*) NOT used as a source: scanned image-only PDF with pre-1911 European orthography (*introducção*, *christã*, *Padre Nosso*) — translated fresh from the French canonical via the `translate-book` skill into modern pt-BR (post-1971 orthography). The pt-BR translation substitutes the standard Brazilian liturgical Pai-Nosso / Ave-Maria where the French quotes the Pater/Ave verbatim, and replaces the French versified Decalogue couplets (plates 27–51) with the single-line Brazilian formula by Mons. Inácio José de Sousa Bandeira (e.g. "Amar a Deus sobre todas as coisas." rather than a forced Portuguese rhyme). Cardinal-virtue term standardized to *fortaleza* (not *força*); capital-sin term to *soberba* (not *orgulho*). Images: chose the *later colored edition* over the 1908 B&W (user preference) — `picture-Xb.jpg` is the colored illustration, `picture-Xa.jpg` is the text page (counterintuitive — `a/b` here means *left page / right page in the opened book*, not *picture / text*). Compressed 68 images from 84MB → 32MB via `sips -Z 1600 -s formatOptions 82`. Image placement: right before the "Explanation of the Plate" / "Explication du tableau" / "Explicação do quadro" section per user direction, since that section explicitly references picture regions ("the top picture on the left", "in the middle"). Catalog entry `book/catechism-in-pictures` registers with 68 chapters × 3 langs + 68 image blobs + style; added to `collection/spiritual-classics` → Catechesis section. Word counts: fr 54,332 (src 54,330, delta ≈ 0); en 58,995 (src 59,023, delta = removed part headers + article subheads); pt-BR 50,248 (typical compression vs. French).
  - **Watch out**: `pdftotext -layout` with no output flag prints page-headers/footers ("Source : ..." URL lines) on every page — strip these in post-processing, not before, because the form-feed `\f` page boundaries are the only reliable way to identify picture-only pages (which are otherwise indistinguishable from blank space).
  - **Watch out**: when joining PDF-wrapped lines into paragraphs, never treat the line *immediately following* a non-sentence-terminated line as a header — "Jésus-\nChrist." would otherwise be parsed as a `## Christ` heading. Hyphen-split compounds (`Jésus-` + `Christ`) need explicit handling (keep the hyphen, join without space). Article subheads in this book also wrap across two PDF lines (`Quatrième article : ... a été\nenseveli`), so after matching an article-pattern line, absorb subsequent fragment lines until you hit a numbered paragraph, a known section-header keyword, or a blank line.
