- **Navigation restructure: hub-and-spoke → 5 tabs (2026-05-27, Phase 1).** Killed the two competing hubs (the `AppShortcuts` home-tile row + the `/explore` shortcut hub) and the single deep `(home)` stack that every feature hung off. Replaced with five tabs — **Today · Explore · Library · You · Search** — where each tab is a destination you scroll, not a grid of links (Search is the deliberate tile/feature-map exception). The `(home)` route group was renamed to a **shared array group** `(today,explore,library,you)/` so the ~25 detail routes (bible, browse, creators, pray, custody, …) exist once but back all four content tabs, each with its own independent stack. Phased rollout: Today is the daily cockpit (header + carousels + rule + schedule + fidelity; tile row and `LatestRow` removed — the creator feed now lives only on Explore); Explore = `LatestRow` feed + the extracted `DiscoverySections` (the old browse landing, now a reusable component shared by `/browse` and the Explore root); Library + You are thin skeleton scroll-lists for now; **Search is the temporary catch-all** that surfaces every secondary feature (oratio/kyrie/examen/memento/catechism/saints/calendar/dies-domini/journal/custody/piano/Mass/Bible) so nothing is orphaned, plus live corpus search via the existing `SearchAutocomplete` when a query is present. Later phases drain features out of Search into permanent homes.
  - **Array-group + NativeTabs is the right mechanism, and simpler than expected.** Expo Router expands `(a,b,c,d)/` into four sibling group routes at build time, so `unstable-native-tabs` just sees four children — `NativeTabs.Trigger name="(today)"` / `"(explore)"` / … (parenthesized segment, *not* bare `today`, or the tab renders empty). Per-tab root selection is purely declarative: `export const unstable_settings = { anchor: 'index', explore: { anchor: 'explore' }, library: { anchor: 'library' }, you: { anchor: 'you' } }`. **Keys are the bare group name (no parens)**; verified in `node_modules/expo-router/build/getRoutesCore.js` (`unstable_settings[groupName].anchor ?? .initialRouteName`, falling back to the top-level `anchor`). A **bare `<Stack>`** consumes the computed per-segment `initialRouteName` automatically (`useScreens.js` → `getSortedChildren(children, order, node?.initialRouteName)`) — **no `segment`-branching or explicit `<Stack.Screen name=…>` needed**, contrary to first instinct. The anchor must name a real child route in the group or boot throws "invalid anchor".
  - **Keep the Today root as `index.tsx`, and list `today` first in the group name.** `/` then resolves canonically to `(today)/index`, so the app cold-launches on Today (first tab) and the existing "go home" calls (`router.push('/')` in `PageHeader`/`BibleReader`, `router.replace('/')` + `<Redirect href="/">` in `dev/reset`) keep working untouched. Naming it `today.tsx` would have broken all of those. All other `router.push` targets are already group-agnostic absolute paths (`/plan`, `/pray/[practiceId]`, `/browse/book/[bookId]`), so they resolve unchanged — a push now lands in the *current* tab's stack instance (the desired per-tab back-stack behavior).
  - **The now-playing player was never tied to the home stack.** It's a `NativeTabs.BottomAccessory` at the tabs-layout level, so it persists across all five tabs regardless of stack depth; the only reason detail routes were nested under `(home)` was to keep the tab *bar* visible while inside them — which the array group preserves. The accessory's `usePathname().endsWith('/episode/${itemId}')` hide-check is unaffected because Expo strips group names from `usePathname()`.
  - **Known Phase-1 wrinkle:** Search is its own group (not in the array group, because of `role="search"` + its header-search stack), so a `router.push('/pray/x')` from a search result lands in the anchor (Today) stack and may switch the visible tab. Accepted for now; revisit with explicit-target navigation if jarring on device.

- **Navigation restructure: the You tab (2026-05-27, Phase 2).** Turned You from eight dead `ShortcutRow` links into a scrollable "State of the Soul" whose **primary job is the rule-of-life config** (the user's framing), wrapped in live interior state below. Top-to-bottom: settings gear in the header (a `PageHeader action` slot, since the post-typography `PageHeader` is left-aligned) → **Rule of Life** (the full old `/plan` body) → **Custody** (prominent, flag-gated, live commitment count) → **Carrying** (intentions / gratias / confession as live `ShortcutRow` trailing values) → **Chronicle** (a 3-entry Memoria peek + on-this-day + "See all"). The "More" catch-all and the Plan/Fidelity duplicate links are gone.
  - **Today = execution, You = configuration.** Today renders the *daily* interactive schedule (`TimeBlockSection`/`useToggleSlot`) + a recent 10-week wall; You renders the rule-of-life *management* (tiered practice list, add/archive, resolutions) + the long-arc 20-week wall. The only thing both draw is the read-only `GreenWall`, at different week windows — intended, not duplication. You deliberately never imports `TimeBlockSection`.
  - **Extract-don't-fork to share the rule-of-life body.** The old `/plan` screen body (resolutions, wall, stats, Browse/Catalog, Pin-All, tiers, archived) became `features/plan-of-life/components/RuleOfLifeSections.tsx` (a Fragment, no `ScreenLayout`/`PageHeader` — each caller owns those); `/plan/index.tsx` slimmed to a thin wrapper that still renders it, so every `router.push('/plan')` (Today heading, empty-CTA, `ResolutionLine`) keeps working and `/plan/[practiceId]` is untouched. The custody card was *pulled out* of that body (it isn't rule config) and promoted to its own You section — so `/plan` no longer shows custody (intentional; custody lives on You + Search). Same pattern for the Memoria peek: `EntryRow` + `getEntryIcon`/`getEntryBody` extracted to `features/memoria/components/EntryRow.tsx` and re-exported, with `memoria/index.tsx` rewired to import them.
  - **Typography discipline (post-`8f4d5bae`).** Newly authored You UI uses `<Typography variant>` only — section labels are `variant="label"`, the on-this-day line `variant="caption"`, live counts/days `tone="muted"` (gold is rationed to *preciousness*, not counts). The two extractions are faithful moves of pre-migration code and keep their raw `Text`/`$heading`/`$body` — a Typography migration of those screens is deliberately out of scope.
  - **Search drained of the journal rows.** Removed Search's "Capture" section (intentions/gratias/memoria/confessio) now that they're homed on You; pruned the orphaned lucide imports. Custody stays dual-listed (You + Search "Tools") on purpose.
  - **Pre-existing test debris, fully cleared (2026-06-01).** 6 test files imported the Phase-1-renamed `@/app/(tabs)/(home)/…` route paths and failed to load — repointed to `(today,explore,library,you,search)`. `custody/time.test.ts`'s `parseHHmm('10:6x')` was a real bug: `Number.parseInt('6x')` returns `6` so trailing garbage passed — fixed with a `^\d+$` guard per part. With the routes loadable, the pray-screen render tests surfaced a chain of missing native test mocks the praying UI now pulls in (glass buttons, settings sheet, keyboard-aware inputs):
    - **The "Unexpected token 'typeof'" red herring → `react-native-keyboard-controller`.** A sync `require()` deep in the render hit `react-native/index.js`'s Flow line (`import typeof * as … from './index.js.flow'`), which Node can't parse. It survives `deps.inline: true` and userland `Module._resolveFilename`/`_load` hooks because the require runs through Vite's own module runner, not Node's CJS loader — so neither the `react-native → react-native-web` alias nor `vi.mock` reaches it. The actual culprit: **`react-native-keyboard-controller`** (imported by `ScreenLayout` → every screen) is a native shim that pulls the *real* `react-native`. Mocking it (`KeyboardProvider`/`KeyboardAvoidingView` → `View`) dissolved the whole cascade — no Node loader hook needed. Lesson: an "Unexpected token typeof" from a render is almost always *some* package loading real RN's Flow source; find and `vi.mock` that package rather than fighting the loader.
    - Then plain missing mocks, added in `setup.ts`: `expo-glass-effect` (`GlassView`/`isLiquidGlassAvailable`→false) and `expo-blur` (`BlurView`) for `GlassSurface`; `@expo/ui/community/bottom-sheet` (`BottomSheet`); a `Proxy` replacing the hand-listed `lucide-react-native` allowlist (any icon → no-op; `has`/`get` traps, `then`→`undefined` so vitest doesn't see a thenable); the reanimated layout-builder set extended with the `FadeIn*`/`FadeOut*` directional family and the chainable methods (`withInitialValues`, `withCallback`, `reduceMotion`, spring params…); and a stub for react-native-web's missing iOS-only `AccessibilityInfo.isReduceTransparencyEnabled` (in `beforeAll`, try/catch'd so files that locally `vi.mock('react-native')` skip it). One genuinely stale assertion fixed: `ResolutionLine.test.tsx` still expected the pre-Altar "today's resolve" attribution (now `t('altar.resolutions')`). Whole app suite green again: **51 files / 450 tests**.

- **Audit: Catecismo Romano pt-BR cross-checked against a second OCR (2026-05-28).** The pt-BR text of `book/trent-catechism` is the Frei Leopoldo Pires Martins / Editora Vozes 1951 translation, originally OCR'd from an Internet Archive scan (auto-OCR `_djvu.txt`) and hand-cleaned. Audited it for surviving OCR errors by aligning it against a **second, independent OCR**: the ClearScan PDF of a later reprint of the same translation (apologetica.net.br; same text as the PD-marked `catecismos-da-igreja-catolica` IA item). Method — `pdftotext` + a light de-spacing pass on the ClearScan, then a whole-book word-level `difflib` alignment, collapsing spacing-only (2,753) and accent/orthography-only (308) divergences, leaving 377 substantive cases triaged by 8 parallel subagents (one per book section). **Result: only 9 genuine OCR-survivor errors** in the ~300k-word book — `inculá-los`→`incuti-los`, `cruelíssima`→`crudelíssima`, `idea`→`ideia`, `oria`→`orla`, `alcivosas`→`aleivosas`, `aquebrantado`→`alquebrado`, `empedernecidos`→`empederniram`, `dileto`→`diletíssimo`, `conservá-lo`→`conservá-la`. The text was already high quality; the audit's main value is the confirmation.
  - **The crucial gotcha: the ClearScan reprint is a *modernized* edition, not ground truth.** It silently updates the 1951 text — `David`→`Davi`, `Job`→`Jó`, `Padre Nosso`→`Pai Nosso`, `Madre Igreja`→`Mãe Igreja`, `dominga`→`domingo`, `subtileza`→`sutileza`, `graveza`→`gravidade`, `Santiago`→`São Tiago` — and drops pre-1971 accents/trema (`sòmente`, `freqüente`). Treating it as authoritative would have *de-faithfulled* hundreds of correct 1951 readings. Every divergence was verified against the Archive 1951 source; only clear corruptions (non-words or broken grammar, confirmed by both witnesses) were fixed.
  - **ClearScan's own failure modes that masquerade as md errors** (all ignored): dropped edge letters (`Apartai`→`partai`, `Assim`→`ssim`), internal drops (`suspeitar`→`supeitar`), `m`→`rn` (`tomai`→`tornai`), word-splitting marginalia injected mid-word (`importância`→`impora adaptar se aos tância`), and mojibake. When the two OCRs disagreed on a word it was *more often* ClearScan that was wrong, so a one-sided "fix toward ClearScan" pass would have introduced errors — cross-verification against the source is essential, not optional.

- **Search redesign + accent/typo-tolerant matching (2026-05-30).** The Search tab's empty state was retired from three bordered `ShortcutRow` lists into an **illuminated portfolio**: Cinzel section labels (Rezar / Estudar / Biblioteca) over two-column jewel-tone tile grids reusing the Explore/Library idiom. New `features/search/ShortcutGrid.tsx` renders `ArtCoverCard`s at a window-derived size (`min(width,640) − 2·24 padding − 16 gutter, ÷2`) so tiles sit two-up on phone and the centered desktop column alike; feature tiles show a large gold lucide glyph, the Biblioteca section pulls **live corpus collections** (filtered to `getEntriesByKind('collection')` ids that have art via `artFor` — that set *is* the curated non-meta collections, so no extra meta-filter needed) plus an "all collections" tile → `/browse/all`.
  - **Matching was accent-blind, not just strict.** The root cause of "Rosario" never finding "Rosário" was plain `.toLowerCase().includes()` in **three** sites — `SearchAutocomplete.scoreText`, `resolver.searchManifests`, `contentIndex.search`/`localizedMatches`. New `lib/search.ts` centralizes `normalizeForSearch` (NFD + strip `̀-ͯ` + lowercase) and `fuzzyScore` (exact 100 / prefix 80 / substring 60 / per-token bounded-Levenshtein 40). **Invariant: all three sites must use it** — the candidate-gatherers (`searchManifests`, `search`) must be fuzzy-aware too, or a typo'd query never reaches the ranker. Hand-rolled, no fuzzy dep (corpus is small enough to score in-memory).
  - **Tiles own their look, not `ArtCoverCard`.** First pass reused `ArtCoverCard` (title *below* the block), but the desired look is the title *inside* the card, big and prominent (Junicode `$title`, cream `blockInk`, bottom-aligned, 3 lines) over the ground — like image #4 / Apple Podcasts. So `ShortcutGrid` renders its own `ShortcutTile`: art-less = soft jewel gradient with the lucide icon as a faint (opacity 0.16) corner watermark; art tiles = the painting under a bottom scrim so the title still reads. `ArtCoverCard`'s title-below layout is left untouched for Explore/Library.
  - **Gradients use `react-native-svg`, not `expo-linear-gradient`** (the latter isn't a dep; same approach as `home/DailyCarousel`). **Soften the jewel tones — the raw `BlockTone` diagonal read "flashy/modern".** The tile mutes each tone toward warm parchment gray (`mix(tone.from, '#8A7D6E', 0.26)`) then makes a *low-contrast vertical* gradient (`+5% white` top → `+16% black` bottom) — aged-vellum-under-a-wash, not neon. The `mix(hexA, hexB, t)` helper lives in `ShortcutGrid.tsx`; tune the parchment ratio / contrast there.

- **Medieval nav bar: full-color illuminated icons + Junicode labels, *inside* NativeTabs (2026-05-31).** Replaced the five SF-Symbol/Material tab icons with custom full-color PNGs (gold-and-bronze illuminations — altar-shrine / compass rose / chained codex / orant medallion / quatrefoil lens) and set the labels in Junicode, **without abandoning `NativeTabs`** — so iOS 26 Liquid Glass, the native selection morph, `role="search"`, `minimizeBehavior`, and the `NowPlayingBar` `BottomAccessory` all stay. It's a ~6-line edit to `(tabs)/_layout.tsx` plus the assets in `apps/app/assets/nav-icons/{today,explore,library,you,search}.png`.
  - **`renderingMode="original"` is the make-or-break prop.** `NativeTabs.Trigger.Icon` accepts `{ src: require(...), renderingMode?: 'template' | 'original' }`, and the **default is `'template'`** (verified in `node_modules/expo-router/build/native-tabs/types.d.ts`, `SymbolOrImageSource`), which tints the image to one flat color — i.e. it would obliterate the gold art into a solid silhouette. Must pass `renderingMode="original"` on every icon. On Android all image icons render original-color regardless; the prop is iOS-only.
  - **`labelStyle` supports `fontFamily` — the web docs are wrong/incomplete.** The public Expo docs only show `labelStyle={{ color }}`, but the installed types define `NativeTabsLabelStyle = Pick<TextStyle, 'fontFamily' | 'fontSize' | 'fontStyle' | 'fontWeight' | 'color'>` (and nothing else — **no `padding`/`margin`/`lineHeight`**, so there's no way to nudge label spacing). So `<NativeTabs labelStyle={{ fontFamily: 'Junicode-Light', fontSize: 12 }}>` sets the label font for all tabs. Leave `color` unset so the existing `tintColor` (gold `DynamicColorIOS`) still owns the *selected* label color. There's also per-trigger `selectedLabelStyle` and a `{ default, selected }` form if needed.
  - **The native label needs the font's PostScript name (hyphen), NOT the `useFonts` key (underscore) — this was the actual gotcha.** Junicode is runtime-loaded via `useFonts` (`app/_layout.tsx`) under underscore keys (`Junicode_Medium`, `Junicode_MediumItalic`, …), and a React Native `<Text>` resolves those. But a *native* UITabBar label resolves `fontFamily` straight through UIKit, which only knows a font by its **PostScript name** — so `'Junicode_Medium'` (underscore) silently falls back to the system font, while `'Junicode-Medium'` / `'Junicode-Light'` / `'Junicode-MediumItalic'` (hyphen) work. The trap: `fontSize` still "takes" with the underscore key (size is font-independent), so it *looks* like the font applied when it didn't — only trying italic exposed the fallback. **No build-time embedding needed** — runtime `expo-font` registration already exposes the font to UIKit by PostScript name (the `expo-font` plugin in `app.json` stays the plain string form). Get the exact name via `mdls -name com_apple_ats_name_postscript Junicode-Light.ttf`: regular→`Junicode-Regular` (the base `Junicode.ttf`), plus `Junicode-Light` / `-Medium` / `-SemiBold` / `-Bold` and the matching `-Italic` / `-LightItalic` / `-MediumItalic` / … italics. Junicode ships no Thin (100); Light (300) is the thinnest.
  - **No lever to add icon↔label spacing.** `titlePositionAdjustment` (the legacy `UITabBarItem` offset) is **ignored on the iOS 26 Liquid Glass bar** (react-native-screens doesn't wire it through), so labels can't be nudged down; and `labelStyle` has no padding/margin. The only way to open a gap is to make the icon smaller or bake transparent padding into the icon PNG. Left as-is.
  - **A tab-bar `src` image renders at its logical POINT size — ship density-suffixed assets, not one big PNG.** First cut used a single `today.png` at 120px (no `@Nx` suffix), so RN treated it as 120 *points* and it filled the entire bar (the native bar does **not** auto-scale a `renderingMode="original"` image down to the ~28pt icon slot the way it does for template/SF icons). Fix: a density set per icon sized so the logical *height* lands at ~28pt — `today.png` 28px / `today@2x.png` 56px / `today@3x.png` 84px, resampled by **height** (`sips --resampleHeight 84 src -o name@3x.png`) so all five tabs share a uniform row height despite different source aspect ratios. `require('.../today.png')` resolves the whole density set automatically (no `_layout.tsx` change); iOS picks `@3x`. Single state per tab (no `selected` art) — selection = native highlight pill + gold label. No `ScreenLayout` clearance change — native bar height is system-fixed.
  - **`search` keeps `role="search"`** and overrides the system glyph with the custom `src` (a custom icon overrides the system icon while preserving the circular expand-into-field affordance, and the Junicode `labelStyle` *does* reach its label). **Its circular button renders the glyph larger than a normal tab icon**, so the shared 28pt asset looked blurry (upscaled) — search uses a bigger density set (~32pt / 96px@3x) to stay crisp. Trade-off: a bigger asset also displays bigger, so search reads slightly larger than the other tabs. Part of that is that the lens art fills its frame edge-to-edge while the medallions sit inside their square with transparent margins — truly matching sizes would mean baking transparent padding into the search PNG, which needs a compositing tool (no ImageMagick/Pillow/sharp on this machine; `sips` can't pad with transparency). Left at ~32pt; the full-res masters live in `assets/nav-icons/source/` for re-derivation.

- **A Big Book of Little Offices imported (2026-06-13, 88 offices + collection).** Imported every little office from *A Big Book of Little Offices* (lobvm.com / Little Office Guild, © 2021, texts mostly PD) as one practice each plus `collection/little-offices`, via a one-time generator `scripts/import-little-offices.py` that parses the book's LaTeX source (`BigBookOfLittleOffices.tex`, ~1.8MB, cached to gitignored `scripts/.cache/`). Faithful import per user direction: **Latin (`la`) for every office, English (`en-US`) only where the source already prints it** (~19 offices) — no machine translation, no pt-BR body text. Flow shape mirrors `little-office-bvm`: a top-level `select on:"hour"` defaulting to the current canonical hour, with shared non-hour blocks (Ordinaria / Conclusio / Commendatio) hoisted before/after the select. 88 offices across the book's 4 parts (Divine Mysteries 19, BVM 17, Saints 43, Misc 9) + a supplementary `office-preparatory-prayers` (Aperi Domine / Sacrosanctae, hardcoded since they're front-matter). The 10 BVM rites get clean override ids (`little-office-bvm-roman`/`-ambrosian`/`-carmelite`/…); existing `little-office-bvm` left untouched per user.
  - **Two LaTeX layouts, detected per office.** Latin-only offices use `\begin{multicols}{2}` (just column layout); bilingual ones use `\begin{paracol}{2}` with `\begin{leftcolumn}` (Latin) / `\begin{rightcolumn}` (English). For paracol, parse each column's blocks independently then **pair the Nth Latin block with the Nth English block** (headers appear in matching order in both columns) and merge language keys onto the same flow node. ~79 multicols vs ~19 paracol.
  - **Blocks are delimited by centered-bold headers** `\begin{center}\textbf{Hour}\end{center}`, but the header regex must tolerate a trailing `\\` *inside* the center env (`\textbf{Ad Vigilias}\\` in the Cistercian/Monastic rites) — that one missing `(?:\\\\)?` silently emptied two whole offices. Header text → canonical hour id via an alias table (Matutinum/Vigilias→matins, Laudes→lauds, Prima→prime, …; "Ad "/"At "/"The " prefixes stripped). Non-hour headers before the first hour → shared opening; after the last → shared closing. Offices with <2 recognized hours (Akathist, Mozarabic, Against Evil Spirits, Stational Vespers, Serotina) render **flat** (no select) — the parser must keep headerless content, not filter it.
  - **Faithful primitive mapping kept deliberately simple.** Each block body is split into paragraphs (blank-line separated — this naturally bundles each liturgical unit: a refrain+℣/℟ run, or a Hymnus label + its verse lines), then logical lines on `\\` **at brace depth 0** (so a red-italic instruction spanning an internal `\\` survives as one rubric). A leading **red** span (`\textcolor{red}{...}` / `\textit{\textcolor{red}{...}}`) = a `rubric` (Hymnus→"Hymn", Ant.→"Antiphon", Capitulum→"Chapter", seasonal "Vel a Septuagesima…" notes, Akathist speaker tags); everything else = `prayer` with `inline`. **℣/℟ are rendered as inline prayer text with the ℣./℟. glyphs, NOT the `response` primitive** — robust (no renderer assumptions, no cross-language verse pairing) and reads fine. The `\Vbar`/`\Rbar`-detection must run *before* the leading-red check, or every versicle line gets miscategorized as a rubric.
  - **Gotchas in `clean_latex`:** `\textcolor{color}{content}` has TWO brace groups — naive brace-matching grabs `{color}`; handle it specially. `\lettrine{D}{eus}` (and the spaced `\lettrine{O} {Lord}` variant) must reassemble to `Deus`/`O Lord`. The ✠ cross is `{\symbolfont ✠}` — stripping `\symbolfont` + braces leaves the glyph. The last office's range runs to `\end{document}` (clamp it) so the bibliography doesn't leak in. JSON written with `ensure_ascii=False` (project convention) so Latin accents stay literal.
  - **Re-running is idempotent** — `scripts/.cache/generated-offices.json` ledgers the generated ids and prunes their dirs first, so changing slug logic doesn't orphan dirs. Both practices and the collection are auto-discovered by `build-corpus.py` (no registry edit).
  - **Second-pass fixes from a multi-agent QA review (Sonnet reviewers spot-checking generated JSON against the source).** The structural/artifact validators were green, but reviewers caught semantic defects only a source comparison reveals — worth recording because they're the non-obvious failure modes of LaTeX→content parsing:
    - **Don't assume column = language.** The book's `paracol` columns aren't consistently ordered (the Sacred Heart *Conclusio* puts Latin in the `rightcolumn`, English in the `left`), and several offices are *English-only* despite the Latin-only `multicols` layout (Holy Tear, both Passion offices, Immaculate Heart of Mary, S. Dominic/Gertrude/Norbert, Serotina, Akathist). Fix: a per-block `detect_lang()` (distinctive Latin vs English stopword scoring, default Latin on tie) assigns the `la`/`en-US` key from the *text*, not the column position. This one change fixed both the column-swap and the English-in-`la` bugs at once.
    - **Centered-bold sub-labels masquerade as hour headers.** Some offices (Child Jesus) typeset `Hymnus`/`Antiphon` as their own `\begin{center}\textbf{…}\end{center}` block rather than the usual inline `\textit{\textcolor{red}{Hymnus}}`. Treating every centered-bold block as a hour/section *delimiter* shattered each hour — the hymn/antiphon/collect leaked out as top-level closing sections. Fix: a `_SUBLABELS` set; centered-bold blocks whose name is a sub-label fold *into* the current block as inline rubrics instead of splitting it.
    - **Header regex must tolerate trailing text inside the center.** `\begin{center}\textbf{Ad Matutinum}\\ I. Dolor ex Simeonis Vaticinio\end{center}` (Seven Dolours) — the dolour subtitle after the bold name made the old regex fail to match, collapsing the whole office. Capture the bold as the hour name and keep the trailing text as a leading rubric.
    - **Bilingual paragraph counts diverge between columns**, so naive index-zip (and even a type-sequence `difflib` align) drifts — Latin chapter split into 2 paragraphs vs 1 in English shifts every subsequent pairing until the next rubric. Fix: merge using rubrics/subheadings as sync anchors and, *between* anchors, concatenate each language's consecutive prayer paragraphs into one bilingual prayer node — Latin can never cross-pair with the wrong English.
    - **Merge duplicate hours, don't go flat.** An office with two seasonal `Ad Sextam` variants (Ambrosian) was falling back to a flat (no-select) flow; now duplicate hour ids append (divider + sections) into the one option.
    - **Source typos to absorb:** Lyon's `Ad Teriam` (→ terce) and `Ad Officium Noc.` (→ matins) needed alias entries; `\Vbar..` double-period collapsed; vocative `\lettrine{O}{dulcis}` → "O dulcis" (space) but `\lettrine{O}{mnipotens}` stays "Omnipotens" via a small O-word join-list. Note `\lettrine{D}{a}nobis` still renders "Danobis" (source glued the next word) — left faithful. The pt-BR None-hour label was "Noa" → corrected to "Nona".
  - **A SECOND review round (covering the Saints batches the first round's rate limit cut off) found more, all now fixed:**
    - **Don't slice to the first `\begin{multicols}`.** S. John Nepomucene splits its hours across *two* `multicols` blocks; the non-greedy `\begin{multicols}…\end{multicols}` regex captured only the first, silently dropping 4 of 7 hours. Fix: ignore the multicols env entirely (it's layout-only) — strip the attribution and feed the whole section to `split_blocks`, which keys off headers.
    - **`{\color{red} …}` is a different macro from `\textcolor{red}{…}`** and appears in 8 offices. The simple command-stripper left the `{red}` arg → "red℣." / "redA Septuagesima". Fix: `re.sub(r"\\color\{[^}]*\}", "", s)`. (My structural validator initially MISSED this — "red℣." contains no backslash/brace — so I added `\bred\b`, `[a-z]Ant\.`, `\bcolor\{`, and `fancyhead` to the artifact scan.)
    - **`\textbf{}` content needs balanced-brace extraction**, not a non-greedy regex: a header like `\textbf{\textcolor{red}{Invitatory}}` made the regex stop at the inner `}`, yielding the literal "red" as the heading. Affects 5 nested-bold headers.
    - **`\fancyhead[…]{\small{\textcolor{red}{Office of the Sick…}}}` has nested braces** the arg-stripper couldn't span, so the running-header title leaked in as a prayer. Added a balanced-brace `_strip_balanced("fancyhead")`.
    - **Non-bold centered rubrics were masked.** A `\begin{center}\textcolor{red}{\textit{Ad Vesperos.}}\end{center}` (centered but not bold — common in the Mozarabic offices) wasn't unwrapped, so the leading `\begin{center}` blocked leading-rubric detection and the label rendered as a prayer. Fix: `_fold_centers` now unwraps *all* center blocks (bold → folded rubric label; non-bold → keep inner markup).
    - **Verb endings typeset as a red "Ant.".** `dissipab\textcolor{red}{Ant.}` → "dissipabAnt."; restore with `re.sub(r"([a-z])Ant\b", r"\1ant", s)` (a real antiphon marker never directly follows a lowercase letter).
    - **Per-VALUE language re-keying.** Per-block detection mis-keys when a column mixes languages — the Roman BVM's Latin column carries English editorial rubrics ("Lesson I – …", "Little Chapter During Advent"). A post-pass (`_rekey`, applied tree-wide in `build_flow`) re-files any ≥4-word string under the language it actually reads in; the language detector's wordlists gained liturgical terms (lectio/capitulum/responsorium ↔ lesson/chapter/responsory/blessing/advent) so these short-vocabulary labels classify. Short labels (<4 words) are left alone (too ambiguous).
  - **Accepted residual (faithful-to-source or low-impact):** a few bilingual rubric *pairings* in the most structurally-divergent offices (s-benedict, bvm-dominican) can mismatch where the two columns don't share a rubric sequence — content is complete and correct per-language (the app shows one language at a time), only simultaneous bilingual display is affected. Cross-references like "cum Oratione, ut supra." in S. Ignatius / Office of the Dead render as prayer text because the source prints them inline. Source-inherited typos kept verbatim: "Ever-Vrgin" (Akathist title), "S. Igantius", "Hymnn", "rapbat", "chotidiano".

- **Hybrid agent-repair pass over the bilingual offices (2026-06-15).** The algorithm transcribes verbatim and is reproducible, but it can't *semantically* align the bilingual `paracol` offices whose two columns split differently (S. Benedict, Roman/Dominican BVM) — that needs a reader who knows "℣. Deus in adiutorium" ↔ "O God, come to my aid". So the algorithm stays the baseline, and a multi-agent **Workflow** (`scripts/.cache/repair-workflow.js`, generated by `gen-repair-workflow.py`; both gitignored) ran one Sonnet agent per office to fix *structure/alignment only* — copying source text verbatim, never re-transcribing. 85 of 88 offices were agent-repaired; the bilingual ones went from partial pairing to ~complete (Roman 147/147 prayer nodes carry both languages, S. Benedict 90/90).
  - **The guardrail is programmatic, not the agent's word.** A Python fidelity gate (`fidelity-check.py`) diffs every content word of each repaired flow against its source slice: >4% words-absent-from-source = fabrication, >30-45% source-words-absent = dropped content. It caught the one real agent failure — `serotina` was silently truncated ~50% (0% fabricated, 58% dropped) when the agent abridged a long English office — and that office was restored from the snapshot baseline (`scripts/.cache/baseline/`). Net: **0 fabrication, 0 truncation** across all 88 after restore; structural 0 errors; engine `resolveFlow` 356/356.
  - **Practical gotchas:** (1) Drop the agent *verify* stage — the programmatic gate is the authoritative check and the agent verifier just doubled token cost. (2) A single agent can't rewrite a huge office in one Write — the Dominican repair (2,177 source lines) blew the 32k-output-token cap; the fix is targeted Edit calls (add the missing `en-US` node-by-node) rather than a full-file rewrite. (3) Snapshot the algorithmic baseline before letting agents edit in place, so any fidelity failure restores cleanly and the generator stays the regenerable source of truth. (4) Repair agents write files in place, so partial progress survives a mid-run rate-limit and the Workflow resumes from cached completions (`resumeFromRunId`).
- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M1: importer + lossless parser + corpus (2026-06-10).** Started the from-scratch EF Mass + Divine Office rebuild (spec: `docs/features/divinum-officium.md`). New package `@ember/divinum-officium` (parser), `pnpm import:do` (pinned shallow clone at repo-root `.divinum-officium/`, commit recorded in `content/do/meta.json`), `pnpm build:do` (7,292 files / 215k lines → `content/do/**.json`, deterministic across runs), `build_do()` in `build-corpus.py` (15 `do-data` catalog items, per-file per-language blobs, ~19.8MB; largest index manifest 152KB), pinning collector for `do-data`.
  - **Store raw lines, not tokens — DO conditionals are line-stream operators.** `SetupString.pl::process_conditional_lines` evaluates `(…)` directives against the *surrounding line array*: a true conditional with backscope **removes already-emitted lines** (`SCOPE_LINE` pops one, `SCOPE_CHUNK` pops to the last blank, `SCOPE_NEST` truncates to the last weaker conditional), and forward scope gates upcoming lines until line/blank/stronger-conditional. Since evaluation depends on runtime context (version, tempus, feria, hora…), the tokenizer + scope machine must live in the app engine anyway — so `content/do` stores sections as **raw line arrays** (beautiful upstream diffs, byte-exact round-trips) and the importer runs the shared tokenizer over every line as a build-time validation gate only (`content/do/inventory.json`).
  - **Perl quirks worth knowing when porting:** `split` drops *all trailing* empty fields (so trailing blank lines at EOF vanish in `do_read`); only the **last** repeated stopword is captured by `($stopwords_regex\b)*`; a line like `(Fit reverentia)` at line start IS parsed as a conditional (subject defaults to `tempore`, predicate becomes a regex test) — faithful port means keeping that behavior; section headers with decorated conditionals (`(sed die in Cœna Domini dicitur)`) only use the expression — stopword/scope are ignored at headers (4 occurrences in the wild).
  - **Tabulae transfers are precomputed per Easter date.** `Tabulae/Transfer/322.txt`–`425.txt` (and `Stransfer/`) are keyed by the year's Easter (Mar 22–Apr 25), entries version-qualified with `;;1960 M1617 …`. The M3 kalendar engine loads tables instead of computing transfer logic — DO itself generates them with `admin/createTransferTables.pl`.
  - **Cofficium golden harness works on modern Perl with two vendored CPAN dists.** Perl ≥5.22 removed CGI from core; `Cofficium.pl`/`Cmissa.pl` need only pure-Perl `CGI` + `URI` — vendored into `.do-golden-lib/` (gitignored). Run: `PERL5LIB=$REPO/.do-golden-lib perl Cofficium.pl 'date1=06-10-2026' 'command=prayLaudes' 'version=Rubrics 1960 - 1960' 'lang2=English'` — CGI params as CLI args. Verified it computes a full correct office (winner + commemoration + bilingual columns).
  - **Language fallback is mandatory, confirmed by dir layout:** `Portugues/` has no `TemporaM` at all and partial `Tempora` (43 vs Latin's 638 files); DO layers vernacular sections over the fallback language over Latin *per section* (`setupstring`), with a `Rank`/`Officium` merge safeguard — the engine loader must replicate this chain (pt-BR → en-US → la), not per-file pick-one.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M2: condition evaluator + reference resolver, differentially verified (2026-06-10).** Ported `vero()`, `parse_conditional()`, the `process_conditional_lines` scope machine, and the full `setupstring()` pipeline (language layering, preamble whole-file inheritance, @-inclusion resolution) to TS. **The killer tool: differential harnesses that drive DO's real Perl** (`packages/divinum-officium/test/perl-harness/`) — JSON requests on stdin, the actual `SetupString.pl` underneath. Every conditional-bearing Latin section × 3 versions and ~190 sampled files × 3 langs × 3 versions must be byte-identical. Found 4 genuine porting bugs that hand-reasoning missed:
  - **`vero`'s atom skip is an unanchored substring test.** `next if /et|nisi/` skips any condition atom merely *containing* 'et' — so `(Populus respondet:)` (a rubric in parens, which the format happily parses as a conditional) evaluates TRUE because its only atom is skipped and the alternative is vacuously satisfied. Equality-checking the separator tokens (the "obvious" port) breaks hundreds of Ordo lines.
  - **Perl `$` ≠ JS `$`.** Data substitutions like `s/$/~/` rely on Perl matching before a string-final newline; JS anchors at the absolute end. Rewrote unescaped `$` outside char classes to `(?=\n?$)` when /m is absent.
  - **Perl `split` drops trailing empty fields** — bit twice in one milestone (Rank `;;` fields keep their trailing `\n\n` since "\n\n" is non-empty; line-range selections on `\n`-split text need the trailing `''` popped).
  - **Replacement strings pass through a double-quoted eval**: `\n` in an s/// replacement is a real newline. And the cyclic-inclusion check compares `<file>.txt` against the full path — the extension is what keeps `Commune/C2` from false-matching inside `Commune/C2a`; dropping extensions (as our loader ids do) reintroduces the collision.
  - Method note: when porting crufty Perl, **write the differential harness before hand-debugging** — each mismatch class was found, root-caused, and regression-locked in minutes once real-Perl output was one stdin line away.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M3: kalendar engine, full-year differential green (2026-06-10).** Ported Date.pm, Directorium.pm, `officestring` (monthday overlay), the 820-line `occurrence()`, and `precedence()` → `resolveDay()`. **Gate: 3,287 days (2025–2027) × 3 versions match real `horascommon.pl` on every output field.** Porting notes:
  - **kalendaria values are only the first `=`-field.** `load_kalendar` does `my ($day, $file) = split(/=/)` — title and rank in the line are display debris. Keeping the whole remainder silently empties the sanctoral cycle (10k+ mismatches).
  - **The `$version =~ //i` empty-pattern guard in `officestring` is effectively FALSE** — so the monthday overlay (Tempora/093-3 etc.) replaces `Rank` too; that's precisely how September Ember days become 'Feria major;;4.9'. The appended "III. Septembris" week title survives only when no overlay file exists.
  - **Perl global pollution is part of the spec.** `$monthday` is set only when officestring hits an Aug–Dec Pent/Epi file and read by `get_tempus_id` afterwards; a long-lived harness must reset it per request or goldens chase ghosts. Same class: occurrence's `!$tommorow` typo (always true), `my %w = setupstring(...)` assigning a hashref to a hash (vigil-on-empty-day office loses its Rank), `@transfers = @transfers || @tr` (scalar-context collapse). All replicated or noted in code comments.
  - **Harness must live inside the clone**: Directorium.pm computes data paths from FindBin's `$Bin`, so the precedence harness is copied to `web/cgi-bin/horas/_ember-precedence.pl` at test time.
  - The 3-year × 3-version sweep runs in ~11s (Perl ~6s, TS ~4s) — cheap enough to keep in the normal test suite rather than a separate gate.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M4 (engine + golden): Mass assembly differentially verified (2026-06-10).** The missa port (specials walker, ScriptFunc registry, oratio/commemoration machinery, $/& expansion) now matches real `Cmissa.pl` output **word-for-word through the entire Mass** for 14 dates × {1960, Divino Afflatu} × {Latin, English} (`src/mass/differential.test.ts` — Cmissa run with version1=version2, columns extracted from the HTML table, both sides normalized to word streams). Porting finds:
  - **`resolve_refs` strips ALL dots from `$`/`&` reference lines** before expansion — `$Per Dominum.` (with trailing period, common in propers) must become the `Per Dominum` Prayers lookup, else the conclusion renders as the literal two words.
  - **Order matters: specials → Prelude/Post-Missam splice → expansion.** Palm Sunday's palm-blessing (`Prelude` rule) contains `$Gloria`/`&`-refs of its own; splicing after expansion leaves them raw.
  - **`setcomment` is dead code in the missa flow** — it pushes to `@s` after `specials` has already returned a list copy, so the `{Source}` headline suffixes never render. A faithful port must NOT push (ours leaked 3 phantom heads at the very end of the stream).
  - **Runtime defaults live in `missa.setup` / `horas.setup`** (`$pope='Leone,Leo'`, bishop placeholder) — now imported as `do-data/dialog` entries; the Te igitur's `N.p`/`N.b` substitution needs them.
  - **The pre-1955 Suffragium accretes from the displaced feria**: on semiduplex-or-lower sanctoral days, `Suffr=…;;` is pulled from `scriptura{Rule}` (or the commemoration) — and whether the feria's Rule still HAS the spec depends on the version's monthday-overlay week-numbering (old-style Sunday-nearest-Sept-1 vs 1960 first-Sunday), which decides if Tempora/09N-D exists to overlay it. Differential testing caught this chain end-to-end.
  - Known M8 TODOs: multi-Mass days (`missanumber`, e.g. All Souls — DO itself renders 'Introitus missing' without a selection), Ordo/Communio.txt inline content for the faithful's communion, votive Masses.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild: rebased onto the OF rework (2026-06-11).** Rebasing the 10-commit DO branch over #269–272 (iBreviary LOTH, `producer/mass-of`, missal tooling) landed cleanly with two conflict points, and main's restructure made the cutover *simpler*: on main `producer/mass` had become EF-only, so the M4 cutover now deletes that producer entirely (`mass-flow.ts` + test) instead of carving the EF branch out of a shared one — `practice/mass` is `producer/mass-of` (OF) + `producer/do-mass` (EF). `packages/mass` keeps only main's new `of/calendar` (resolveOfDay); buildEFFlow/ef are gone. **`mass-fragments.json` and `scripts/build-mass-fragments.mjs` are retired** — main's OF builds directly to primitives and the deleted EF producer was the only remaining consumer. Post-rebase: engine differentials all green (63 tests), and the app suite's 36 failures are a strict subset of origin/main's own 37 (all environmental — they expect a locally served Hearth catalog), i.e. zero regressions from this branch. The OF LOTH (iBreviary, `practice/liturgy-of-the-hours`) and the upcoming EF breviary practices are distinct features — keep practice ids distinct (breviary-* = EF/DO).

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M5: minor hours + Compline differentially green (2026-06-11).** The hours port (Ordinarium walker, psalmi_minor, capitula, preces, Compline concurrence) now matches the real Perl **char-for-char** for 12 dates × 5 horas × 3 versions × 2 languages (`src/hours/differential.test.ts`). App side: `producer/do-hour` + `content/practices/breviary-{prime,terce,sext,none,compline}/`. Harness + porting finds:
  - **Use `Pofficium.pl`, not `Cofficium.pl`, for hour goldens.** Cofficium is the two-VERSION compare tool — when version1==version2 it silently forces column 2 to Divino Afflatu (the column you'd diff is the wrong version). Pofficium with `version=` + `lang1`/`lang2` gives the one-version bilingual layout. Body cells start right AFTER the 'Ante Divinum officium' framing cell — anchoring on the 'Incipit' heading misses offices that omit it (Compline of the Dead).
  - **The site's default display setup is `noinnumbers=1` + `noflexa=1`, decoded from the horasp cookie defaults** — the variables are never assigned anywhere in the code. `handleverses` must strip the first subverse letter (`2a`→`2`) and the first inline `(N)` per line, and fold the flexa (`‡ x *` → `* x`, daggers dropped). Each Perl substitution applies ONCE per line unless /g. Missing this produced 102 differential failures that looked like deep engine bugs.
  - **Compare char streams, not word streams.** Perl splits red initials into their own cells ('S' + 'alve'), which desynchronizes any single-letter word-merging heuristic; joining the normalized words and diffing characters sidesteps it (`node/wordStream.ts charDivergence`, shared with the Mass differential).
  - **Several "section text" features are actually render passes in webdia/horas.pl**, so they live in our `renderFinish`: each item's first `#Heading` is translated through Translate.txt's literal `[#Name]` keys (guarded by an omit-regexp built from Comment.txt's Preces/Suffragium words); `Ant.` lines get a final dot; `Benedictio.`/`Absolutio.` line prefixes are translated; `process_inline_alleluias` unbrackets `(alleluia…)` in Paschaltide and deletes it otherwise; `suppress_alleluia` fires in Lent (`Quadp|Quad[1-5]|Quad6-[0-5]` && !Septuagesima_vesp).
  - **Perl quirks, continued:** `&psalm(n,1)` two-arg call is a *nogloria flag*, not a range (ranges need 3 args) — De profundis at the Commemoratio defunctorum gets Requiem æternam instead of Gloria; `state $precdomfer = $hora eq 'Prima'` is a per-process counter *initialized on first call* (so the two columns of one assembly get 'Dominicales Prima 1' then '2' — a module-level 0 breaks the first column); `$weekname` in concurrence is an unset global (its Pasc clause is dead code); `$ccomrank !~ 1.5` is a regex match on the number string; trailing-empty `split` semantics bit a third time (Divinum_auxilium indexes lines from the end).
  - **English `Sancti/11-02.txt` ends Compline with `&special('Conclusio', 'Latin')` — a data bug DO faithfully renders.** Every other call in the file says 'English'; `special()` picks `%winner` vs `%winner2` by the call's *literal* lang argument, so the English column keeps the Latin 'Requiéscant in pace' while the embedded `&Gloria` still expands in English. Our `special` must honor the literal argument (it had used the rendering column's lang and "fixed" the bug, diverging from Perl). Debugging method: when the data and the push are both provably correct, instrument `setcell`/`resolve_refs` in the clone — the divergence was a *deferred* `&special` call resolved at render time, invisible at specials() time.
  - Monastic Prima reads the **daily Rule of St. Benedict** — new `Regula` dataset (125 Latin / 123 English plain files + Regulatable.txt lookup; no Portuguese). The date key shifts +1 day after Feb 23 in non-leap years, and Feb 23 appends the Feb 24 portion; the lectio-brevis label merge when the Rule replaces it is bug-compatible (`reg.replace(/\.?:\/\s*$/, ' '+comment+':/')`).
  - Martyrologium (4 variants + lunar computus, ~1.5MB) deferred to M8 — Prima emits an explicit unavailability note and the differential prunes the block on both sides, so the surrounding Pretiosa versicle stays compared.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M6: Lauds + Vespers differentially green (2026-06-11).** psalmi_major, capitulum_major + the Monastic major responsory, hymnusmajor (with the Directorium Hy-table hymn shift/merge), the Benedictus/Magnificat canticum path, the full oratio() commemoration machinery (getcommemoratio / vigilia_commemoratio / getrefs / delconclusio), getsuffragium, the Rogation-days Laudes Litania, and the Office-of-the-Dead conclusions. **Gate: 16 dates × 7 horas × 3 versions × 2 languages char-identical with Pofficium** (672 office-columns), including octave/concurrence dates (Dec 26, Aug 15, Jan 1, Nov 2). Porting finds:
  - **`extract_common` always overwrites commune.** The Perl returns a two-element list even on no-match, and `if (my ($ct,$c) = extract_common(...))` evaluates the list assignment's element COUNT (2 → always true) — so the winner branch in occurrence() always reassigns communetype/commune, clearing them on plain offices. Invisible in single-run resolutions (M3's full-year sweep passed); fatal once concurrence runs occurrence() twice — tomorrow's commune (the commemorated saint's `vide C2-1`) leaked into today's Vespers and pulled the chapter from the Common instead of the Psalterium.
  - **`$caller` gates real rubric logic, not just link rendering.** `occurrence()` skips the "Office of All Souls' day ends after None" Vespers rule when `$caller` is set (it's the URL param marking sub-requests). Our assembleHour passed caller=1 (cargo-culted from link codes) and kept All Souls as the Vespers winner; Pofficium runs with caller unset, where Nov 2 second Vespers belong to the day within the octave of All Saints. App assembly must use caller=0.
  - **Pofficium prints no 'Ante/Post Divinum officium' framing cells for 1570/1955/196x/Altovadensis** (`print_content`'s fifth argument) — a differential harness that anchors on the Ante cell must fall back to the FIRST table cell, not the first 'Incipit' cell, or it silently drops `[Prelude <hora>]` cells (Easter's Paschal-Vigil rubric).
  - **The 1960 one-commemoration cap sorts keys as strings.** `sort(keys(%cc))` is lexicographic; the commemoration priority keys are engineered to be 4-digit so it happens to equal numeric order — port the string sort anyway (`Object.keys(cc).sort()`), not a numeric one.
  - Perl's `%cwinner` (the concurrent office's sections) is a long-lived global read by getsuffragium and Septuagesima_vesp — persisted as `KalendarState.cwinnerSections` at the end of concurrence().
  - getrefs' `@feria:` branch reads `Psalterium/Major Special.txt` — a path that doesn't exist (the real file is under `Special/`) — so its lookups always miss and emit 'missing' placeholders; ported bug-compatibly. Its `$octavam` octave-dedupe accumulator is request-scoped global state (lives across both columns) — modeled on HoursState like precdomfer.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M7: Matins differentially green — the full office now matches the Perl (2026-06-11).** specmatins.pl (1,856 lines) + the Matins half of monastic.pl ported: invitatorium, Matins hymns, Roman + Monastic psalmi_matutinum, nocturns, absolutions/benedictions, the 700-line lectio() lesson selector with responsories and Te Deum. **Gate: 16 dates × all 8 horas × 3 versions × 2 languages char-identical with Pofficium** (768 office-columns; 66 package tests green). Porting finds:
  - **Perl `split` trailing-empty semantics struck three more times** — now load-bearing in *indexed* arrays: getantmatutinum's versicle interspersing (`@vers = split` then push — a trailing '' shifts every later nocturn slot), the `Ant Matutinum 3N` override loop (`for 0..$#t` — the trailing '' OVERWRITES the third-nocturn versicle at psalmi[17]), and the proper-vs-target line count (`@wprop < $target` decides whether versicles get interspersed at all). Rule of thumb adopted: in ported hours code, never `.split('\n')` section content — always the trailing-empty-dropping splitPerl().
  - **JS dot ≠ Perl dot, twice in one function.** responsory_gloria repeats only the LAST R-line (`(R\..*?)$` — Perl '.' excludes newlines; a [\s\S] port repeats from the FIRST R), and its strip branch `s/.\&Gloria.*//s` needs the dot to eat the preceding newline (/s) — a JS '.' silently leaves '&Gloria' in the stream.
  - **`s/14.*16 /16 \u/s` — Perl's `\u` in s/// replacements is real syntax in the data** (titlecase the next character, including across `$1`: `s/.*; (.)/\u$1/`). The generic "unknown \X drops the backslash" rule turned it into a literal 'u' glued to the next word. references/substitutions.ts now evaluates replacements with Perl double-quote semantics via a replacer function.
  - lectio() is deferred: lectiones() pushes literal `&lectio(N)` lines and the lesson text is resolved at *expansion* time (registered as an hours ScriptFunc) — the Perl mutates `%winner`/`%winner2` (Responsory substitution from the commemorated office) as a side effect, which the port mirrors by writing into the shared winnerSections.
  - `prevdayl1`'s `if ($day = 0)` is an assignment — the month never rolls back; and `my $c = split(...)` in the monastic Cantica branch takes the split's COUNT and then reads the unrelated global `@c`, so the special Quad canticles are simply blanked. Both ported bug-compatibly.

- **Divinum Officium rebuild, M8: Martyrologium + settings — the rebuild is complete (2026-06-11).** The Martyrologium (368 day files × {base, 1960 variant} × {Latin, English} + Mobile.txt) is imported as two new `do-data` datasets, and `martyrologium()` is ported together with the **Gregorian epact computus** (`gregor()` — golden number → epact table → lunar-month walk to the day of the moon) and the epact-2008 `luna()` fallback. It passed the differential on the first run, so the hours comparison now covers the complete office with no pruned blocks: 16 dates × 8 horas × 3 versions × 2 languages, char-identical with the Perl. Also: `&special('#Martyrologium')` re-entry runs the walker over a single item with saved/restored state (Perl re-enters specials() with a `special` flag that suppresses the Special-override and resets `$octavam`); `doVersion` is now a PillSelector in Settings; `collection/breviary` groups the 8 hour practices. Warm assembly: 37ms Matins / <20ms other hours / 8ms Mass on the fs loader — far under the 200ms gate. v1 closes here; deferred items (multi-Mass days, votives, Communio, more versions, date browser) are listed in the spec status.

- **Divinum Officium: Phase-1 multi-version expansion — 8 more breviary versions (2026-06-13).** Added the Roman rubric-years (Tridentine 1570/1888/1906, Divino Afflatu 1939, Reduced 1955) and Monastic sub-versions (Tridentinum 1617, Divino 1930, Barroux) to `versions.ts`, the settings selector (`doVersionOrder` + a compact label map), and the corpus (imported `Martyrologium1570` + `Martyrologium1955R` for their Prime). **The engine already version-gates everything, so most of these worked on the first differential run** — the rubrica conditionals were ported with the v1 three but fire correctly for the older years. Findings:
  - **`<i>…</i>` HTML italic in source rubrics leaked into output.** The Tridentine/Monastic antiphona-finalis carries `<i>(sicut ad Laudes)</i>`; the Perl renders it as italic and the differential's `htmlCells` strips the tags, but our pipeline passed the literal `<i>`/`</i>` through — which `toWords` turned into stray `i` tokens AND would have shown literally in the app. Fixed in `cleanItemMarkers` (shared render): strip `<i|b>` tags, keep the text. This alone took Monastic 1617/1930 from 8/10 to 10/10.
  - **The Quicumque (Athanasian Creed) condition was under-ported.** v1 dropped the two trailing clauses of the Perl `if` (the `trident`/`cist` season tests + the `Tempora || !cist` guard) since those rites were out of scope. "Monastic Tridentinum 1617" matches `/trident/i`, so it was missing the Creed at Sunday Prime. Restoring the full condition (safe for the existing versions — they match neither `trident` nor `cist`) made Monastic 1617 fully green across the 16-date matrix.
  - **The require-cycle bug.** `psalmi.ts ↔ matins.ts` was a static import cycle (psalmi imported `psalmiMatutinum`, matins imported `antetpsalm`/`splitPerl`). Lazy under Node (all tests passed) but Metro/Hermes can resolve a cycled binding to `undefined` → `undefined is not a function` on-device. Broke it by moving the Matutinum dispatch up into the assembly walker, so `psalmi.ts` no longer imports `matins.ts` (graph now acyclic). Lesson: a green Node test suite does not prove the module graph is Hermes-safe — watch Metro's "Require cycle" warnings.
  - **Verification split.** 7 versions are now strict-differential-green (the 3 originals + DA 1939, Reduced 1955, Monastic Divino 1930, Monastic 1617). 4 are imported/selectable with journaled gaps: **Tridentine 1570/1888/1906 have a systematic Matins divergence** (the Tridentine `Daya` psalm scheme + lessons — a Phase-1.5 port; their other hours are green), and **Barroux** diverges only on the Saturday-BVM Sext chapter. The hours differential runs the 7 strict and asserts the 4 partial assemble without throwing (the crash/regression guard). One journaled exception inside the verified set: the **English column of the Corpus Christi 12-lesson Nocturn III antiphon** for Monastic Divino 1930 / Tridentinum 1617 — the Perl inherits the Latin antiphon (the English file has no translation of that `Ant Matutinum 3N` section), where our setupstring language-layering resolves an English one. Encoded in `knownHourDivergences`; the rest of those versions' 16×8 matrix is strict. (Caught only because the canonical 16-date set includes 6-11 Corpus Christi, which the ad-hoc validation date list had omitted — a reminder to validate against the real test matrix, not a parallel one.) Phase 2 = Dominican (OP), Phase 3 = Cistercian — both need their data dirs imported and substantial rite-specific engine porting.

- **Divinum Officium, Phase 1.5: all 11 versions fully hour-verified (2026-06-14).** Closed every known multi-version divergence; `partialHourVersions` is now empty. Five engine fixes, each a faithful-port gap:
  - **Tridentine ferial Matins psalm scheme.** psalmi_matutinum was missing the entire `if ($version =~ /trident/i)` `@psalm_indices` block — the Tridentine `Daya` tables pack 12 psalms into one nocturn at indices 0–7 (psalmi[3..5] are psalms, not the Roman versicle lines), with a different versicle source and the Saturday-BVM Ps 99→91 swap. This was wrong on *every* Tridentine date (we emitted ~3 psalms then jumped to the lessons).
  - **Tridentine sanctoral responsory.** Ported the Perl "dirty hack" (`$w{Responsory1..3}=''` when `$version =~ /Trident/ && $winner =~ /Sancti/`) that clears the occurring-scripture responsories so a saint's-feast 1st-nocturn responsory falls back to the Commune (C2 'Iste Sanctus' on a Martyr) rather than the September scripture ('Conforta me', Tempora/094-0).
  - **Contract8.** Ported the missing lessons-8+9 combination (always under Tridentine): wraps lesson 9 in a «…» rubric joined via `$rubrica NonaAdjuncta` when a commemoration takes lesson 9's slot — needed on All Saints / Conversion of St Paul Matins.
  - **Corpus Christi Nocturn III antiphon.** The Monastic 12-lesson `Ant Matutinum 3N` fallback must read the **Latin** `%commune` global, not `%commune2`, even in the vernacular column (Perl literally uses `$commune` there) — so a Commune antiphon the English file doesn't translate (Corpus Christi 'Caro mea') stays Latin in both columns instead of our resolving an English one.
  - **`\l$1` substitution.** The Perl s/// replacement `\l$1` lowercases the first char of the *group's* value; my `\l` wrongly consumed the literal `$`, producing a literal `$1` and dropping the Barroux Sext chapter continuation. Now `\l` sets a lowercase-next flag mirroring `\u` — a general substitution-engine fix.
  - Lesson reinforced: validate against the *canonical test matrix*, not a parallel date list. My ad-hoc validation set used 6-13 where the real differential uses 6-11 (Corpus Christi), so a green ad-hoc run masked the monastic Nocturn III bug. The strict differential now runs all 11 versions (16×8×2 each).

- **Divinum Officium: votive offices — engine differential + UX (2026-06-14).** The breviary now exposes votive offices (Office of the Dead C9, Little Office BVM C12, Our Lady on Saturday C10, St Joseph V4, the Passion V6) through one extra **Votive Office** tab on `practice/breviary`, opening a `pickerStyle: 'cards'` picker with no default; each card holds its own hour picker bound to `producer/do-hour` with `{hour, votive}`. The engine threading (`votive` through `resolveDay`/`assembleHour`) already existed; what was missing was (a) the UX plumbing — `pickerStyle` + per-option `excerpt` now flow through the static `select` end-to-end (`content-engine` `types.ts`/`resolve.ts` → `preprocessFlow` → `SelectBlock`, which gained a cards branch and a `selectedId: ''` "nothing selected" path) — and (b) a **votive differential** to actually prove the offices match the Perl. The differential immediately caught a real divergence I'd previously hand-waved as a "niche Prima edge":
  - **`suppress_alleluia` runs *after* the renderer reds the marker abbreviations.** A Marian votive antiphon carries alleluias year-round (`Allelúja, * deduc me…, allelúja, allelúja.`); in Lent they're suppressed. The Perl wraps `Ant.` in `<FONT COLOR=red><I>Ant.</I></FONT>` *before* `suppress_alleluia`, so the regex `s/[,.]?\s*$alleluia//` can't reach the marker's dot — it strips ` Allelúja` (leading space + word) and leaves `Ant., *…`. Our pipeline keeps `Ant.` as plain text, so the same regex ate the dot → `Ant, *…`, which `toWords` (whose marker-strip needs the literal `Ant.`) then failed to drop, surfacing as a spurious `ant` token. Fix: `suppressAlleluia` guards a line-initial marker dot with a negative lookbehind (`(?<!(?:^|\s)(?:Ant|Ps|[AVRSOMCDP]|v|r))`), anchored so it can't fire mid-word; the space before the alleluia is still consumed. Lesson: when porting a Perl renderer, the *order* of text passes matters — markup that protects punctuation upstream changes what a downstream regex can match. A behavior only "verified" by eyeballing is not verified; the votive differential is now part of the suite.

- **Explore: a "Daily Meditations" row of medium text-on-image cards (2026-06-16).** Added a new second row to the Explore feed (directly under the hero `FeaturedCarousel`, before The Library) surfacing the daily-meditation practices — St Alphonsus, Divine Intimacy, Opus Dei. New patterns worth knowing:
  - **`FeatureTile` is the medium tier between hero and cover.** `FeatureBlock` (hero, 340pt, full width, caption overlaid on art) and `ArtCoverCard` (small cover, ~150pt, caption *below*) had no in-between. `FeatureTile.tsx` is the hero card shrunk to ~200×240 with a 20pt headline: same lower-third scrim + cream caption, but it sits in a horizontal `ArtCarousel`/`CardRow`. Reuses `blockInk`/`blockLabelInk`/`BlockTone` and the `ZoomLink` href pattern.
  - **The row config carries curated subtitle keys, not just ids.** Manifest names alone are thin for this row (Opus Dei's is the generic "Meditation of the Day"), so `meditationRow` in `pickFeatured.ts` is `{ id, subtitleKey }[]`, resolved by `practiceRow()` (mirrors `collectionRow` but filters `kind === 'practice'`). Tiles title from the localized manifest `name`, subtitle from `t(subtitleKey)`, tap → `/pray/[practiceId]`.
  - **Renamed the St Alphonsus practice display name only.** `practice/meditacoes-ligorio` was "Meditações de Ligório" / "Liguori's Daily Meditations" — awkward for most. Changed `manifest.json` `name` to "Meditações de Santo Afonso" / "St Alphonsus' Daily Meditations"; the **id stays `meditacoes-ligorio`** (referenced by its flow's `book: liguori-meditacoes`, the liturgical map, and `collection/alphonsus-liguori` — renaming the id was out of scope).
  - **Dedicated art is pending a fetch with network access.** `artMap.ts` points the practices at `meditation-{alphonsus,intimita,opus-dei,patristic}.jpg` and the targets are in `fetch-explore-art.mjs`, but Wikimedia Commons is firewalled from the web/sandbox runner (HTTP 403), so the JPEGs must be fetched and committed from a machine with access (`node scripts/fetch-explore-art.mjs <ids>`). Until then the tiles fall back to the jewel-tone versal initial.

- **Explore: the patristic-reading card — LOTH ≠ Divinum Officium (2026-06-16).** The 4th Daily-Meditations tile surfaces today's **second (patristic) reading from the modern Office of Readings**, as its own `practice/patristic-reading`. The trap worth recording: the "Office of Readings patristic reading" belongs to the **modern Liturgy of the Hours**, *not* the traditional breviary. Our `packages/divinum-officium` / `practice/breviary` is the pre-Vatican-II office (Matins lessons) — a different corpus; a first research pass wrongly proposed extracting Matins `Lectio2/5/8` from it. The modern LOTH is `practice/liturgy-of-the-hours`, fed by **iBreviary** (`producer/breviary-of-the-day`), whose Office-of-Readings page already carries both readings.
  - **The new source composes rather than re-fetches.** `producer/office-of-readings-reading` calls `ctx.sources.fetch(breviarySource, { hour: 'office-of-readings' })` — reusing the iBreviary session queue + per-day cache instead of duplicating fetch/parse — then slices the parsed `Primitive[]`: from the rubric matching the edition's second-reading label (`SECOND READING` / `SEGUNDA LEITURA` / `LECTIO ALTERA`) up to the next responsory rubric (`RESPONSORY` / `RESPONSÓRIO` / `RESPONSORIUM`), dropping the bare label line. Labels verified against all three `__fixtures__/{en,pt,la}-ufficio_delle_letture.html`; a fixture-driven test (`office-of-readings-reading.test.ts`) asserts the slice keeps the Cyprian reading and excludes the first reading + both responsories.
  - **Failure throws, never caches junk.** If the second-reading rubric isn't found (layout/parse change), the source throws — native shows preprocessFlow's retryable placeholder, matching `breviarySource`'s contract. On web the breviary source's no-CORS placeholder passes straight through.
- **St. Josemaría Escrivá works via the escriva.org API — the first *external books* (2026-06-16).** Escrivá's works (The Way, Furrow, The Forge, the homilies, Conversations, the Rosary, the Way of the Cross, the Letters) are now readable in-app, fetched live from `https://escriva.org/api/v1` and **deliberately never stored in Hearth** — they're in copyright (© Fundación Studium / Opus Dei), so we render from the publisher's own API and cache per-device only. Findings and decisions:
  - **The data model already had external-book hooks, unused.** `BookEntry.source = { type:'external', producer, homepage }` and an external `ChapterRef = { type:'external', url }` existed in `manifestTypes.ts` with a "presence flips the runtime loader path" comment — but **no loader honored them**; `bookContent.ts`/`resolver.ts` returned empty for external refs. This integration is the first to implement that path. Lesson: a type can advertise a capability the runtime doesn't have — grep for an actual consumer before assuming it works.
  - **The catalog is replaced wholesale, so non-Hearth entries need a separate layer.** `loadCatalogFromHearth` runs cache-first on boot *and* revalidates in the background that same session, each call `setCatalog`-ing a fresh object. Injecting Escrivá entries into `catalog.items` once is silently wiped by the next refresh. Added a `localItems` map in `contentIndex.ts` that `getEntry`/`hasEntry`/`getAllEntries` read alongside `catalog.items` (Hearth wins on collision) — registered once at boot via `registerLocalEntries`, immune to `setCatalog` churn. Plus a `setManifestBodyResolver` hook so `ensureManifestBody` builds an Escrivá `BookEntry` on demand instead of `getJson`-ing a synthetic hash against Hearth (which would 404).
  - **Three endpoint families, discovered only by probing.** `book_group` decides the shape: `base` → `/chapters` + `/points`; `cartas` (the letters) → `/cartas-chapters` + `/cartas-points`; `one-level`/`holy-rosary` → `/one-level-texts` (the text *is* the chapter). A book record exposes its own `chapter_list_url`/`point_list_url`, but the *group* is what tells you which to expect. The Rosary and Way of the Cross silently returned `count: 0` from `/chapters` and `/points` until I followed the book detail's urls to `one-level-texts`.
  - **Languages are site ids, and the two app languages don't fully overlap.** `en-US` = site 1 (9 books), `pt-BR` = site 6 (12 books). "In Love with the Church" is English-only here; "Em Diálogo com o Senhor" and the Letters are Portuguese-only. Books carry a per-language `languages` array so a work shows only where it exists. Chapters are keyed by a shared ordinal (`ch-0`, `ch-1`, …) and zipped across languages, since each language's chapters are *different* API objects with different ids.
  - **Pinning had to learn to skip external chapters.** `pinningManager`'s book traversal assumed every chapter ref was a `{hash,size}` blob; with real external refs present it was both a type error and a latent bug (it would try to pin a hashless ref). Guarded with `if (!('type' in ref))`. Per the chosen scope, pinning an Escrivá book pins its metadata but does not bulk-prefetch chapters — those stay on-demand + cached.
  - **Worktree gotcha (process, not code):** absolute paths under `/home/gustavo/Documents/ember/...` hit the *parent* checkout, which sits on a different feature branch — not the assigned `.claude/worktrees/…` worktree. Edits landed in the wrong tree and had to be transferred via `git diff | git apply --3way` + restoring the parent. Always anchor file ops to the worktree root.

- **Explore: AI-generated card art + live meditation subtitles (2026-06-16).** Two follow-ups to the Daily Meditations row. (1) The four cards' paintings are **AI-generated** late-Baroque devotionals, not PD masters — no single public-domain work fit each subject (no good PD St Alphonsus portrait; the Carmelite "Ecstasy of St Teresa" is already `collection/carmelite`). `content/art/CREDITS.md` labels them AI-generated with the exact prompts, and they're dropped from `fetch-explore-art.mjs`'s target list so a PD re-run can't clobber them. Also removed the redundant "Meditation of the Day" block from the hero `FeaturedCarousel` — the row now carries it. (2) The tile subtitles are **lazily resolved to today's meditation title/theme** instead of fixed taglines (`useMeditationSubtitle` / `meditationSubtitle.ts`), with the fixed tagline as fallback. Things worth knowing:
  - **Four cards, four different title sources.** Opus Dei → the existing `fetchMeditationSummary().title` (a separate lightweight page fetch). Patristic → the **second-reading source line** ("From a treatise of St Cyprian…"): re-runs the iBreviary office-of-readings fetch (`fetchSectionHtml` + `parseHourPage` + `extractSecondReading`) and takes the first sliced primitive's first line. Alphonsus / Divine Intimacy → resolve the practice's *own* `liturgical-map` (`loadPracticeData(id)['liturgical-map']` → `resolveLiturgicalDay(date)`) to today's chapter slug, then `loadBookChapterText` and read the **first markdown ATX heading**.
  - **Book meditation chapters aren't standalone catalog items**, so there's no cheap title lookup — the slugs (`temeridade-pecador-dia-juizo`) live only in the book's `chapters` map, not as `chapter/` entries. The title is the chapter body's first `#` heading; getting it means loading the chapter blob. Divine Intimacy is Italian-only, so the resolver tries the app language then the book's own `languages` (the subtitle shows the Italian title — that's the content's language).
  - **Don't swallow — let React Query catch.** The resolvers `throw` on fetch/parse failure (rather than `catch {}`); the `useQuery` records the error and `data` stays undefined, so the card falls back to its fixed tagline without hiding the failure. Web disables the two network-backed titles (iBreviary has no CORS; Opus Dei summary is native-only), again falling back.

- **Mass Times backend: Cloudflare Workers + D1 — first real backend, the tooling traps (2026-06-16).** New `packages/api` (`@ember/api` — Drizzle schema as source of truth, rrule `expand.ts`, Zod validators) + `apps/backend` (`@ember/backend` — Hono, geohash geo queries, FTS5, corrections). Built and verified locally (26 tests, `wrangler dev`); deploy is account-gated. The non-obvious bits:
  - **`@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` v0.16 (vitest 4) dropped `defineWorkersConfig` and the `./config` subpath.** Old configs `import { defineWorkersConfig } from '@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers/config'`; v0.16 exposes a **`cloudflareTest` plugin** used inside a plain `defineConfig` from `vitest/config` (`plugins: [cloudflareTest({ wrangler: { configPath }, miniflare: { bindings } })]`), plus `readD1Migrations`. The error "Missing './config' specifier" is the tell. There's a `vitest-v3-to-v4` codemod in the package that documents the transform.
  - **A raw-TS workspace package consumed by `tsx` needs `"type": "module"`.** Without it, Node/tsx loads the package's barrel as CJS and `import { church } from '@ember/api'` collapses to a single `default` export ("does not provide an export named 'church'"). Matching `@ember/missal-schema`, adding `"type": "module"` to `@ember/api` fixes named exports. **But** the barrel re-exports `expand.ts` → `rrule` (CJS), which then breaks under raw Node ESM ("rrule does not provide an export named 'RRule'") — so the import CLI **deep-imports `@ember/api/src/schema` + `/src/dump`** to get the rrule-free surface. (esbuild/vitest handle the barrel fine; only the tsx CLI path hits this.)
  - **drizzle-kit can't emit FTS5 virtual tables.** The `church_fts` vtable + its AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE sync triggers live in a **hand-written `0001_church_fts.sql`** alongside the generated `0000`. Keep `--> statement-breakpoint` between statements so `wrangler d1 migrations apply` / `readD1Migrations` split on the marker, not on the `;` inside the trigger `BEGIN…END`. Don't model `church_fts` in the Drizzle schema — query it via raw `sql`.
  - **FTS5 `MATCH` must name the virtual table, not an alias.** `... JOIN church_fts ON church_fts.rowid = c.rowid WHERE church_fts MATCH ?` works; `WHERE f MATCH ?` (aliased) fails with "no such column: f".
  - **geohash prefix range upper bound = `prefix + '{'`.** `'{'` (0x7B) sorts just after base32 `'z'` (0x7A), so `geohash >= 'abc' AND geohash < 'abc{'` captures exactly the prefix's full-precision hashes. SQLite TEXT defaults to BINARY collation, so the plain `(geohash)` index is already sargable for these ranges — `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` confirms `SEARCH … USING INDEX church_geohash_idx`. No spatial extension exists on D1.
  - **The Workers Rate Limiting binding (`ratelimits` in wrangler.jsonc) AND R2 bindings run in the local miniflare/vitest-pool-workers runtime** — so write endpoints (fingerprint dedup, R2 attachment round-trip) are fully testable with no Cloudflare account. Only actual 429 *enforcement* under load is worth confirming remotely. Fingerprint = SHA-256(`CF-Connecting-IP` + optional `X-Client-Id` **header**), centralized in a `writeGuard` Hono middleware.

- **Liguori meditations: post-Pentecost off-by-one + reserve-before-fixed ordering (2026-06-16).** `meditacoes-ligorio` showed reserve-pool filler instead of the day's meditation for ~31 days/year, most visibly the week after the Sacred Heart novena (June 12–18 in 2026 — reported as "only additional meditations render"). Two independent defects, both confirmed against the book's own chapter text (the meditation literally headed *"Domingo de Pentecostes"* was resolving on Trinity Sunday). Note this also corrects the Alphonsus Explore-card subtitle above, which resolves the same `liturgical-map` via `resolveLiturgicalDay`:
  - **The temporal map was one week out of phase with `ef-position.ts`.** The book lays meditations in calendar order and its first post-Easter block is *"Semana de Pentecostes"* (the Pentecost octave week), then *"2ª semana depois de Pentecostes"* = Trinity, *"3ª"* = Sacred Heart, … The map filed *"Semana de Pentecostes"* at `post-pentecost/1/*`, but the engine keys the Pentecost week as `easter/8/*` (Easter season runs through Pentecost Saturday) and starts `post-pentecost/1` at Trinity. So every post-Pentecost meditation rendered a week late, `easter/8/*` was empty (→ reserves all Pentecost week), and the Sacred Heart seam (`post-pentecost/2/5`–`3/4`) had no entries (→ reserves June 12–18). Fix: re-keyed the temporal section — `easter/8/*` ← book Pentecost week; `post-pentecost/j` ← old `j+1` for j=1..23; the book's *"25ª e última"* (end-of-world, Matt 24) stays on the engine's dedicated last-week slot `post-pentecost/25`, with `post-pentecost/24` duplicating the penultimate to fill the engine's 25-slot model (the book supplies 24 post-Trinity weeks; the engine wants 25 + leftovers). The Sacred Heart **novena override** (Easter+59..67) exactly covers the now-sparse `post-pentecost/1/3..6` and `2/0..4`, so those holes never surface — and Corpus Christi (Easter+60) lands on novena day 2 = `solenidade-corpo-deus` by design. Year-sweep after the fix: **0 reserve days in 2024/2026/2027** (was 31).
  - **`resolveLiturgicalDay` tried the reserve pool *before* fixed-date content** (`liturgical-day-resolver.ts` steps 1c/1d were swapped). Since the reserve list is non-empty, step 1c always fired and the fixed-date fallback (1d) was dead code — so Christmas-season fixed days (Dec 25–31, Jan 1–10) showed a random reserve as the *main* meditation while the real one (Nativity, Circumcision, Epiphany…) was demoted to an "additional" card. Same symptom class as the June bug, different cause. Swapped the order so the reserve pool is the true last resort; `pushAdditional`'s exact-entry dedupe then collapses the now-temporal fixed entry out of the additional list (no double render). Five resolver tests had encoded the buggy expectations (incl. one literally named "Trinity 2026" asserting the *Pentecost* id) — updated to the correct alignment.
  - **Remaining by design:** 1–2 "octave of Epiphany" days (Jan 11–12 in years when the first Sunday after Epiphany falls late, e.g. 2025/2030) still use a reserve — the book has no meditation for them, which is exactly what the reserve pool is for. Resolves `docs/future-plans/fix-liguori-liturgical-map.md`.

- **Immaculate Heart of Mary (and every movable sanctoral) was silently dropped by the MR→`content/of` transform (2026-06-17).** Reported as "the Saturday after Sacred Heart shows no Immaculate Heart memorial, though it's in the Missale Romanum data." Root cause was in the **transform**, not the data: `tools/missal/src/enrich/calendar.ts` carries an `easterRelativeSanctoral` table for the two movable memorials that have no fixed upstream date (Immaculate Heart = Easter+69; Mary, Mother of the Church = Easter+50), but it was **keyed by invented slugs** (`sanctorale.immaculate-heart`) the pipeline never emits. The real formulary ids are the upstream movable-section numbers — `sanctorale.movable.05-32` / `05-35`. So `sanctoralEntry()` missed the table, fell through to the `MM-DD`-from-id regex (which doesn't match `…movable.05-32`), produced no `dateRule`, and dropped the entry. Net: `content/of/calendar/sanctoral.json` had **351 fixed entries and zero easter-relative** — every movable sanctoral absent. (Sacred Heart was unaffected: it's *temporal*, resolved by movable math, not the sanctoral table.) Since #280 unified the OF card + Mass onto `resolveOfDay`, the gap hit both surfaces. Things worth knowing:
  - **The resolver was already correct.** `packages/mass/src/of/calendar/sanctoral.ts` `entryMatches` handles `dateRule.type === 'easter-relative'`/`offsetDays` against both the current and prior liturgical year — it just had nothing to match. The bug was purely upstream data the transform failed to emit.
  - **`tools/missal`/`content/of` is machine-derived but ember-extra is effectively retired**, so you can't re-run `missal build` (needs the ember-extra baseline). But the calendar statics don't need it: **all 351 fixed dates are fully determined by their formulary ids** (verified zero divergences), so `buildCalendarStatics(formularies, new Map())` re-derives the sanctoral statics from the committed `content/of/formularies/**` alone (fixed → id MM-DD parse; movable → the corrected table). Regenerated that way, then spliced only the two new entries into `sanctoral.json` (the existing 351 differ only in JSON key order — `color` position — so a full rewrite would be a noisy no-op diff).
  - **Two coinciding memorials stay both-offered.** 2026-06-13 carries St. Anthony of Padua (fixed memorial) too; both being memorials, GIRM makes them optional and `resolveOfDay` returns both — the picker shows the choice. Tests assert presence, not sole occupancy.
